# Database stack, round two

The SQLite-to-Postgres-to-MySQL track, reviewed PR by PR against `docs/database.md`. Round one was `stack-review-2b3e365.md`, when the stack was three deep and based on the unmerged PR #1. Since then PR #1 merged to master as `dd733fe`, the stack was recomposed on top of it, and it grew to five:

| PR | Branch | Head | Base | Reviewed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| #2 store descriptors | `codex/database-store-descriptors-final` | `1ef641e` | `master` | round one and two |
| #3 store identity | `codex/sqlite-store-identity` | `f4f1dca` | #2 | round one and two |
| #4 DbOps and backup provenance | `codex/sqlite-db-ops` | `7d47607` | #3 | round one and two |
| #7 migration histories | `codex/migrations-resumed` | `aaf6396` | #4 | first review |
| #8 portable SQL, Postgres and MySQL clients | `codex/database-portability-resumed` | `686e07c` | #7 | first review |

## Verdicts

| PR | Verdict | Findings | Comment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| #2 store descriptors | mergeable with changes | 13: 8 minor, 5 note | [5644305395](https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/artifactory/pull/2#issuecomment-5644305395) |
| #3 store identity | needs changes | 11: 1 blocker, 1 major, 4 minor, 5 note | [5644334307](https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/artifactory/pull/3#issuecomment-5644334307) |
| #4 DbOps and backup provenance | needs changes | 15: 2 major, 7 minor, 6 note | [5644329084](https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/artifactory/pull/4#issuecomment-5644329084) |
| #7 migration histories | needs changes | 11 confirmed, 2 refuted: 2 major, 5 minor, 4 note | [5644472625](https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/artifactory/pull/7#issuecomment-5644472625) |
| #8 portable SQL, Postgres and MySQL clients | needs changes | 16: 4 major, 5 minor, 7 note | [5644467131](https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/artifactory/pull/8#issuecomment-5644467131) |

66 findings confirmed across the stack, 2 refuted, 1 blocker. No PR is rejected and none of the majors is a lost-write defect: every one is either a refusal with no way out, a named design deliverable half-landed, or a claim the tests cannot support.

**The pattern worth naming.** Every new refusal in this stack is correct about what to reject and wrong about leaving a way out. PR #3's adoption record pins the absolute store path and refuses forever, and the same PR's ordering disables the restore route its own error message recommends. PR #3's upgrade gate is raised inside the layer, so its explanation reaches no route and the operator gets a retriable 503 for a permanent condition. PR #4's retention counts foreign-engine bytes it refuses to prune, so the budget becomes unsatisfiable. PR #7 treats a derived mirror as a second authority and refuses on disagreement, with no in-band repair on either store. Four PRs, one failure shape: a guard that proves a property and strands the operator who trips it.

**The second pattern: named deliverables half-landed at the least important sites.** PR #4 centralises the backup path in the four readers and leaves both writers hand-rolling it. PR #8 routes `readTransaction` at two of seven sites and misses the one transaction every read runs inside, and routes `isDescendant` through a new portable helper while the superseded SQLite-only module keeps the live boot-side caller. PR #8 also ships `distinctFrom`, `globPrefix` and `lockRow` with zero production callers. In each case the abstraction exists, the tests pass, and the property the abstraction was introduced to guarantee does not hold.

**What is genuinely strong.** PR #2's parser validates after decoding, so percent-encoded traversal cannot reach the privileged keeper. PR #3's two-phase adoption survived an independent kill-point analysis from both reviewers. PR #4 has the best validation in the stack and closed the Linux, image and QEMU acceptance the last round demanded. PR #7's ledger DDL is the vendored `Migrator`'s own SQLite branch, so it is wire-compatible rather than a parallel invention. PR #8's SQLite equivalence was proven by differential execution rather than asserted, and its credential redaction could not be broken by a reviewer trying to.

Method, per PR: two Opus finders on orthogonal dimensions, each re-read by an Opus skeptic that re-opens every citation and defaults to refuting what it cannot confirm, plus one agent that installs, checks, builds and runs tests in that PR's own detached worktree. A finding appears below only if the skeptic let it stand.


---


## PR #2 — store descriptors, at `1ef641e`

**Verdict: mergeable with changes.** Both finders and both skeptics independently reached it. Thirteen findings confirmed, none refuted, no blocker and no major. Comment posted: https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/artifactory/pull/2#issuecomment-5644305395

**Validation reproduced at the head.** `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, `bun run check` and `bun run build` pass with an empty `git status` afterwards. Two targeted batches: the twelve files this PR touches, 33 tests; and 21 recovery, cutover, backup, restore and keeper files, 80 tests with one environment-gated skip. Zero failures; the four descriptor-relevant files pass twice more identically. The staged runtime seed installs frozen with `@comms/storage` present. The body vouches for `d062269` while the head is `1ef641e`; both later commits are test-only and pass.

**The previous asks.** (a) `render` returns `Redacted.Redacted` and the test asserts the wrapper hides the value: fixed. (b) `redactUrls` is still absent but the deferral is now stated plainly rather than implied: fixed as a disclosure. (c) Fixture consolidation is admitted but owned by no step, and the surface grew from the design's 47 files to a measured 67 files and 84 call sites: partly.

**What this PR gets right, including beyond the design.** The parser validates after decoding, so percent-encoded traversal cannot reach the privileged keeper, and its one throwing call is wrapped so a bad escape is a typed failure rather than a defect. Emitting both environment variables and cross-checking them, independently at the child and at the keeper before any file authorization, is stronger than the design's rename-with-alias. Making `APP_STORE` mandatory while the alias defaults to absent means a child that receives only the legacy variable refuses rather than quietly opening the aliased path. The compatibility test is real: an alias-only generation whose candidate deletes rows and dies, with the acknowledged row surviving fallback and restart. Production is fully consolidated, with exactly one `SqliteClient.layer` call left in all of `src`.


### The item to fix before merge

An image rollback cannot bring the board live. `boot-channel.ts:33` reads `APP_STORE` with no default while the alias defaults to absent; master set only `APP_DATABASE`, and installed app source lives in `/data`, not the image. Upgrade, let the source move on, then roll the image tag back: the old boot sets no `APP_STORE`, so every candidate and every retained generation fails its channel layer before serving. No acknowledged write is lost, but nothing can come live and the only way in is boot source editing. The alias exists to keep rollback targets launchable and covers one direction only.

#### A-1 [minor] `render` is not a total inverse of `parse`: a control character in the filename produces an unparseable descriptor with no error channel

`packages/storage/src/store.ts:17` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** `render` percent-encodes every byte that `parse` rejects pre-decode, but `parse` re-rejects the control-character class *after* decoding, so `render` can silently emit a descriptor that no consumer can parse. `render` has no error channel to say so.

**Evidence.** store.ts:17-18 is `Redacted.make(`file:${store.filename.split("/").map(encodeURIComponent).join("/")}`)`. store.ts:24 rejects raw `/[?#\\\x00-\x20\x7f]/` — which `encodeURIComponent` has already escaped away — and then store.ts:33 rejects the *decoded* filename on `/[\\\x00-\x1f\x7f]/`. I reproduced both halves outside Effect: `render("/data/a\tb.db")` yields `file:/data/a%09b.db`, and feeding that back through parse's exact predicates returns `store_descriptor_invalid`. Every other case I tried round-trips (`/tmp/a b/é?#%.db`, `/tmp/100%25.db`, `/data/a%2Fb.db`, `/tmp/日本語.db`), and the test at packages/storage/test/store.test.ts:5-11 only covers filenames that do round-trip, so the asymmetry is unpinned.

**Scenario.** A deployment whose `DATA_DIR` or board directory contains a tab or newline (POSIX permits both). `supervisor.ts:142` renders `APP_STORE` from it without complaint; `linux-ownership.ts:111` calls `childStore(...).pipe(Effect.orDie)` and the privileged keeper dies before spawn on every attempt, so no generation can ever launch. The only diagnostic is the bare code `store_descriptor_invalid` (store.ts:9-13 carries no `detail`, and the design's §4.2 `detail` field was dropped), naming neither the variable nor the offending character. Boot's own stores are fixed paths today, so this is reachable only through an unusual volume layout — but it is a hard wedge when reached.

**Suggestion.** Make the two ends agree. Either have `render` refuse (or `Effect.die` on) exactly the character class `parse` rejects at :33, so a bad filename fails where it is constructed rather than where it is consumed; or drop the post-decode control-char test and let the encoded form be the canonical carrier. Add one test case asserting whichever direction you pick, and restore the design's `detail` field so the failure can name `APP_STORE`.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in code. packages/storage/src/store.ts:17-18 encodes per segment with encodeURIComponent; :24 rejects the raw form on /[?#\\\x00-\x20\x7f]/ (already escaped away by render), then :33 re-rejects the DECODED filename on /[\\\x00-\x1f\x7f]/. So render("/data/a\tb.db") -> "file:/data/a%09b.db" which parse then refuses. The finder understates the reach: a plain backslash in a filename has the same fate (encodeURIComponent("\\")="%5C", passes :24, dies at :33), which is more plausible than a tab. Failure path is as described: supervisor.ts:142 renders without complaint, linux-ownership.ts:111 is childStore(...).pipe(Effect.orDie) before any file authorization, so the privileged keeper dies pre-spawn every attempt with only the bare code (StoreError carries code only, :8-13; the design's detail field at database-design.md:275 was dropped). Reachable only via an unusual DATA_DIR, so minor is the right severity, not major.


#### A-3 [minor] `BootChannel` still carries `filename: string`, so §4.4's `Store.clientLayer(boot.store)` row is implemented by re-wrapping at each consumer — and the read-worker wire message becomes a plaintext credential carrier at step 5

`packages/server/src/kernel/boot-channel.ts:45` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** The descriptor is parsed at the channel and then immediately unwrapped back to a filename, so the channel contract is unchanged and the two consumers reconstitute a descriptor from a string. One of those consumers serializes that string across a process boundary.

**Evidence.** boot-channel.ts:33-36 parses `APP_STORE` and then does `const filename = store.filename;`; the channel exposes `filename` at :45 (rehearsal) and :209 (live) and never exposes `store`. Consumers re-wrap: `server.ts:237` is `clientLayer({ _tag: "file", filename: boot.filename })` and `sql-read.ts:19` puts `filename: boot.filename` into the wire struct, whose schema is `filename: Schema.String` (sql-read-wire.ts:7), decoded and handed straight to `clientLayer({ _tag: "file", filename: request.filename })` at sql-read-worker.ts:54. docs/database.md:332 specifies this row as `Store.clientLayer(boot.store)`.

**Scenario.** Behaviourally identical on SQLite. At step 5 the same field holds `postgres://app:secret@db:5432/comms_app`: it is JSON-encoded into the read worker's stdin, and the parent's failure path at sql-read.ts:53 decodes the worker's reply with `Schema.decodeUnknownEffect` whose issues can quote inputs. The PR body commits only to "credential-URL scrubbing at stored stderr boundaries" — this third boundary is not in that list, and it is the one that crosses a process. (The mitigation today is real: sql-read.ts:31 sets `stderr: "ignore"`, so the worker's own output never reaches a log.)

**Suggestion.** Carry `store` on the channel alongside `filename` (or instead of it) and make `ReadRequest` hold the rendered descriptor as a `Schema.Redacted` field, so the two consumers receive a descriptor rather than rebuilding one. Then add the read-worker wire to the PR body's list of boundaries that must be scrubbed before a remote engine is enabled.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed line by line. boot-channel.ts:33-36 parses APP_STORE via Config.Redacted then immediately does `const filename = store.filename;`, and the channel exposes only `filename` (:45 rehearsal, :209 live). server.ts:237 re-wraps `{_tag:"file", filename: boot.filename}`; sql-read.ts:18-22 puts `filename: boot.filename` into the wire struct whose schema is `filename: Schema.String` (sql-read-wire.ts:7), decoded and re-wrapped at sql-read-worker.ts:54. database-design.md:332 does specify this row as `Store.clientLayer(boot.store)`, so it is a design row implemented by an equivalent-but-different shape. Behaviourally identical on SQLite; the cost is real but at step 5. Minor stands. The mitigation the finder cites is accurate (sql-read.ts:31 stderr: "ignore").


#### A-4 [minor] `check-invariants.ts` still permits `server → boot` wholesale, so the §4.3 invariant the whole `packages/storage` workspace exists to satisfy is unenforced — and the test fixtures cannot catch a violation

`scripts/check-invariants.ts:43` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** This PR's central design argument is that `packages/server/src` must not reach `@comms/boot` because an editable generation does not contain it. The guard the design cites as the enforcement mechanism adds the storage clause but leaves `server → boot` globally allowed, and the integration fixtures resolve `@comms/boot` successfully, so nothing in the repo fails when the rule is broken.

**Evidence.** check-invariants.ts:41 adds the new clause `((owner === "boot" || owner === "server") && dependency === "storage")`, but :43 still reads `(owner === "server" && dependency === "boot")` with no file-level qualification — unlike the `ui` clause at :44, which does carry one (`!label.startsWith("packages/ui/src/")`). Today only `packages/server/src/start.ts:1` imports boot, and `packages/server/runtime/package.json` deliberately omits it while `stage-runtime.ts:12` deletes `start.ts` from the seed. But the test harness hands every staged generation `packages/server/node_modules` as its dependency root (packages/boot/test/fixtures/launcher.ts:11-14), where bun's workspace link for `@comms/boot` exists because `packages/server/package.json:18` declares it.

**Scenario.** Someone adds `import { something } from "@comms/boot"` to `packages/server/src/kernel/foo.ts`. `oxfmt`, `oxlint`, `tsc --noEmit` and `bun run check:invariants` all pass; every integration test passes, because the seed resolves `@comms/boot` through `packages/server/node_modules`. In a real deployment, generation preparation installs from `runtime/package.json`, the candidate child dies on an unresolved specifier, and boot rolls back. The core guarantee survives (the broken generation never goes live), but the deployment burns a cutover and a rollback on a defect every layer of CI was positioned to catch.

**Suggestion.** Qualify :43 the way :44 already is: allow `@comms/boot` only from `packages/server/src/start.ts` (and the server test fixtures already exempted at :30-34). One conjunct, and the argument in docs/database.md:316 becomes a checked property instead of a description.

**Skeptic.** Both halves confirmed. check-invariants.ts:41 adds the storage clause; :43 is still a bare `(owner === "server" && dependency === "boot")` with no file qualifier, unlike :44's ui clause which carries `!label.startsWith("packages/ui/src/")`. Only packages/server/src/start.ts:1 imports @comms/boot today, stage-runtime.ts:12 deletes start.ts from the seed, and runtime/package.json omits @comms/boot while listing storage. The fixture claim holds: packages/boot/test/fixtures/launcher.ts:11-14 defaults the dependency root to ../../../server/node_modules and packages/server/package.json:18 declares @comms/boot, so a staged generation resolves it in tests. check:invariants does run in CI (package.json:16 inside `check`, .github/workflows/linux.yml:49), so the "CI is green on a broken build" scenario is real. Not a regression introduced here, and a bad generation still never goes live, so minor is correct severity; it is nonetheless the most actionable item in the set.


#### A-6 [minor] `AppRecovery.store` is a plain constant beside a retained `filename`, not the `Ref` step 1 specifies, and the PR body does not list the deferral

`packages/boot/src/app-recovery.ts:92` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** The design names this rename "the mechanical bulk of step 1" and specifies a `Ref` for a stated reason (§10.3: a restore has to move the pointer). The PR adds `store` as a value derived once at layer construction and keeps `filename` as the live field, and says nothing about it.

**Evidence.** app-recovery.ts:92 is `const store: FileStore = { _tag: "file", filename };` inside `make`, returned at :101 next to `filename` at :102 and `dataDirectory` at :103. `filename` is still the operative field at cutover.ts:208 (`fs.exists(recovery.filename)`) and supervisor.ts:265-266 (`fs.stat`/`chmod`), while `store` is used only to feed `launch` at supervisor.ts:262, cutover.ts:246 and database-restore.ts:146. docs/database.md:1067 says "Replace `AppRecovery.filename` with `AppRecovery.store`, a `Ref`"; docs/database.md:338 gives the reason. The PR body mentions neither `AppRecovery` nor the `Ref`.

**Scenario.** Zero difference on SQLite, where the design itself says the `Ref` never changes. On Postgres, `restoreInto` journals a new database name and must point the supervisor at it; with `store` captured at layer construction there is nowhere to write the new value, so every `launch` after a remote restore re-renders the pre-restore descriptor and the child opens the old database until boot restarts. PR #4 records this as "no authoritative-pointer mutation", so the gap is known downstream — it is just not disclosed here, where the design placed it.

**Suggestion.** Either make it a `Ref<FileStore>` now (the three `launch` call sites become `yield* Ref.get(recovery.store)`, and `filename` becomes a derived read) or add one sentence to the PR body listing it with `app_store_database` as deferred to the restore work, so the step-1 ledger is honest.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed: app-recovery.ts:92 is `const store: FileStore = { _tag: "file", filename };` inside make, returned at :101-103 beside the retained `filename`, and `filename` is still the operative field at cutover.ts:208 and supervisor.ts:265-266 while `store` only feeds launch (supervisor.ts:262, cutover.ts:246, database-restore.ts:146). database-design.md:1067 does name the Ref as step-1 work and :338 gives the reason, and the PR body mentions neither. However the design itself says the rename is not behaviour-preserving and depends on §10.3 identity work, which the body explicitly assigns to PR #3, so this is an undisclosed-but-coherent partial, not a claimed-done gap. Minor stands as a disclosure defect only.


#### B-1 [minor] The descriptor is decomposed back to a filename at every open site; `boot.store` never exists

`packages/server/src/kernel/boot-channel.ts:36` (durability-regressions)

**Claim.** Design §4.4 replaces `SqliteClient.layer({ filename: boot.filename })` with `Store.clientLayer(boot.store)`. The PR instead parses the descriptor, keeps only `store.filename`, and re-wraps a fresh `{_tag: "file", filename}` literal at each of the eight remaining open sites, so `FileStore` is an unvalidated literal type rather than a parse result and nothing threads an engine-bearing value.

**Evidence.** boot-channel.ts:35-36 `const store = yield* childStore(...); const filename = store.filename;` then exports only `filename` (:45, :209). The open sites rebuild the descriptor: server.ts:237 `clientLayer({ _tag: "file", filename: boot.filename })`, sql-read-worker.ts:54 `clientLayer({ _tag: "file", filename: request.filename }, { readonly: true, busyTimeout: "100 millis" })`, app-recovery.ts:80 and :92, app-backup.ts:13 and :48, app-store-layout.ts:86, index.ts:65. The read wire still types the store as a path: sql-read-wire.ts:7 `filename: Schema.String`, fed by sql-read.ts:19 `filename: boot.filename`.

**Scenario.** Cost, not a runtime failure: `FileStore` admits anything a literal can hold, so it does not mean "a selection `parse` accepts" — `{_tag:"file", filename:":memory:"}` and relative paths type-check and open fine through `clientLayer` (12 test files already pass `:memory:` to `SqliteClient.layer`), while `parse` rejects both. At steps 5-6 all eight sites plus the `ReadRequest` wire must be revisited again, because none of them can be given a postgres or mysql descriptor without a second edit; the compiler will not point at them.

**Suggestion.** Expose `store` from boot-channel alongside `filename` and pass `clientLayer(boot.store)` at server.ts:237; have `AppRecovery`/`AppBackup` layers take the `FileStore` they already build once rather than reconstructing it per call site. If `FileStore` is to stay freely constructible, say so in packages/storage/docs/README.md and name the `:memory:` exception, since §12.4's fixture work will hit it.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in full. packages/server/src/kernel/boot-channel.ts:33-36 parses then immediately discards the descriptor (`const store = yield* childStore(...); const filename = store.filename;`) and exports only `filename` (:43-46). Every open site rebuilds a literal: packages/boot/src/index.ts:65 `clientLayer({ _tag: "file", filename: path.join(options.dataDirectory, "boot.db") })`, packages/boot/src/app-recovery.ts:80 and :92, packages/boot/src/app-backup.ts:13/:48, packages/boot/src/app-store-layout.ts:86, packages/server/src/server.ts:237, packages/server/src/kernel/sql-read-worker.ts:54; the wire still types the store as a bare path at packages/server/src/kernel/sql-read-wire.ts:7 `filename: Schema.String`, fed by sql-read.ts:19. Design §4.4 (database-design.md:332-334) specifies `Store.clientLayer(boot.store)`, so `boot.store` genuinely never exists. The cost is as stated and not a runtime bug: packages/storage/src/store.ts:4-7 makes `FileStore` a freely constructible literal, so `:memory:` and relative paths type-check and open through clientLayer while parse (store.ts:30-39) rejects both. Minor is right — every site resolves to exactly the file master used (verified against `git grep SqliteClient.layer origin/master`: the same 8 sites, same filenames, same options), so nothing durability-bearing changes today; the cost is the re-edit at steps 5-6.


#### B-2 [minor] Boot renders a descriptor from an unresolved data directory, and a rejected one fails with a code that names neither the variable nor the cause

`packages/boot/src/index.ts:50` (durability-regressions)

**Claim.** `parse` is narrower than the filesystem (absolute paths only, no backslash, no control characters), but boot renders `APP_STORE` from `path.join(options.dataDirectory, ...)` without resolving it, and the resulting refusal carries only `store_descriptor_invalid` — not the variable name that design §13 step 1's acceptance requires ("fails startup with `store_descriptor_invalid` and a message naming the variable").

**Evidence.** index.ts:50 `const appFilename = path.join(options.dataDirectory, isolated ? "store/comms.db" : "comms.db");` (no `path.resolve`), rendered verbatim at supervisor.ts:142 `APP_STORE: Redacted.value(render(store))`. store.ts:24 rejects anything not starting with `file:/` and any `[\\\x00-\x1f\x7f]`; store.ts:31-39 repeats the checks after decoding. StoreError carries `code` only (store.ts:8-14) and `message` returns the code, so the child's stderr tail says `store_descriptor_invalid` and nothing else; `Effect.orDie` at linux-ownership.ts:111 does the same in the keeper. `packages/server/src/start.ts:23` does resolve (`path.resolve(dataDirectory)`), but `packages/boot/test/fixtures/launcher.ts:9` passes `DATA_DIR` through unresolved.

**Scenario.** Run boot with `DATA_DIR=data` (or any legal POSIX path containing a backslash) through a launcher that does not resolve: boot opens `./data/comms.db` happily, renders `file:data/comms.db`, and every candidate and rehearsal child dies inside `channelLayer` before printing `COMMS_CHILD_PORT`. All three attempts on every generation fail identically, the board never goes live, and `generations.stderr` plus `/_boot/status` show `store_descriptor_invalid` with no mention of `APP_STORE` or of the data directory being relative. Note the other half of this is an improvement over master and should be credited: with a relative path the old code had boot open the store relative to boot's cwd while the child opened it relative to its snapshot cwd, i.e. two different files and acknowledged app writes in one boot never reads. Failing closed is the right behaviour; only the diagnosis is missing.

**Suggestion.** `path.resolve(options.dataDirectory)` once in `boot()` (index.ts:50) so boot cannot render a descriptor it would itself reject, mirror it in test/fixtures/launcher.ts:9, and carry the variable name into the failure — either a `variable` field on StoreError or a wrapping error at boot-channel.ts:35 and linux-ownership.ts:111 — so the step-1 acceptance criterion is actually met.

**Skeptic.** Both halves confirmed, severity correctly minor. (a) packages/boot/src/index.ts:50 `path.join(options.dataDirectory, isolated ? "store/comms.db" : "comms.db")` has no resolve, and it is rendered verbatim at packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:142. The internal inconsistency the finder implies is real and sharper than stated: inside the same function, supervisor.ts:144 resolves for `PAGES_DIRECTORY`, app-store-layout.ts:22/:24 resolve both root and filename, and seed-source.ts:12-13 resolves/realPaths — only the descriptor does not. (b) Design line 1073 is verbatim 'A malformed descriptor fails startup with `store_descriptor_invalid` and a message naming the variable', and StoreError (packages/storage/src/store.ts:8-14) carries only `code` with `message` returning it, while boot-channel.ts:35 and linux-ownership.ts:111 (`Effect.orDie`) add no variable name, so the acceptance criterion is unmet and the PR body does not list it as deferred. One scope correction to the scenario: the production entrypoint does resolve (packages/server/src/start.ts:14/:23 `path.resolve(dataDirectory)`), so a relative DATA_DIR is only reachable through a library/test caller such as packages/boot/test/fixtures/launcher.ts:9 and storage-launcher.ts:9; the backslash variant survives resolve and is reachable in production. That keeps it minor rather than major, and the finder's credit for fail-closed behaviour over master's two-different-files split is correct.


#### B-3 [minor] The descriptor-only child contract (APP_STORE with no alias) is never executed, and the child-side mismatch refusal is untested

`packages/server/src/kernel/boot-channel.ts:34` (durability-regressions)

**Claim.** The compatibility test proves only the legacy half of the matrix (alias present, descriptor ignored). Nothing in the suite launches a child with `APP_STORE` and no `APP_DATABASE`, so the `Config.withDefault(undefined)` branch — the contract that has to work when §4.4's alias is eventually removed — is dead code, and the child-side `store_descriptor_mismatch` refusal is never exercised end to end.

**Evidence.** boot-channel.ts:34-35 `const legacy = yield* Config.String("APP_DATABASE").pipe(Config.withDefault(undefined)); const store = yield* childStore(Redacted.value(descriptor), legacy);`. Every launcher sets both: supervisor.ts:142-143, linux-ownership.ts:136-137, and all 11 test sites (e.g. kernel-route-guard.test.ts:77-78, fixtures/rehearsal.ts:42-43, boot-event-wait.test.ts:40-41, migrations.test.ts:86-87). The mismatch path has a unit test on the helper (storage/test/store.test.ts:42-45) and a Linux-only keeper assertion (linux-keeper-acceptance.sh:218, mode `mismatch`), both of which bypass the child. store-descriptor-compatibility.test.ts:15-20 covers the alias-only generation.

**Scenario.** A later PR removes or reorders the cross-check while dropping the alias (exactly the §4.4 follow-up), or a refactor makes an absent `APP_DATABASE` a config error instead of a default. The default `bun run test` suite stays green because no test ever starts a child without the alias, and the regression surfaces only on the first retained-generation rollback in production, which is the path SPEC §7.1 relies on. The same blind spot covers non-isolated deployments, where no keeper runs and boot-channel's check is the only enforcement of a conflicting pair.

**Suggestion.** Two cheap cases: delete `APP_DATABASE` from one existing child-launching fixture (kernel-route-guard.test.ts is enough) to pin the descriptor-only contract, and add one case that starts a child with a deliberately conflicting pair and asserts it refuses with `store_descriptor_mismatch` before serving, so the non-isolated path has a test of its own.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed by exhaustive grep: every site that sets APP_STORE also sets APP_DATABASE — packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:142-143, packages/boot/src/linux-ownership.ts:136-137, packages/boot/test/kernel-route-guard.test.ts:77-78, packages/server/test/{rehearsal-rollback.ts:22-23, boot-storage-refusal:41-42, backup-schedule:105-106/140-141, boot-handler-failure:47-48, boot-publication:48-49/95-96, migrations:86-87, fixtures/rehearsal:42-43, kernel/boot-event-wait:40-41} and scripts/linux-keeper-acceptance.sh:116/:217-218. So boot-channel.ts:34's `Config.withDefault(undefined)` branch is never taken by any running child, and the child-side `store_descriptor_mismatch` refusal is only unit-tested on the helper (packages/storage/test/store.test.ts:42-45) plus a Linux-only keeper assertion. The non-isolated observation is correct: with no keeper, boot-channel.ts:35 is the only enforcement and nothing executes it. Minor: a test-coverage gap on a contract that must hold when the alias is dropped, not a present defect.


#### B-4 [minor] One commit silently repairs a test that is red on the merged base and is unrelated to descriptors

`packages/server/test/extension-routes.test.ts:153` (durability-regressions)

**Claim.** `d6509bf` ("Check onboarding route precedence against the served source") fixes a pre-existing failure on master, not a descriptor regression, and neither the commit nor the PR body says so — which makes step 1's "the suite passes untouched" acceptance unreadable from this branch.

**Evidence.** At dd733fe the case asserts `expect(await (await fetch(`${app.url}/init`)).text()).toContain("# comms")` (origin/master:packages/server/test/extension-routes.test.ts, two sites plus one after the edit). `/init` serves the page source verbatim — onboarding.ts:36 `const source = yield* pages.read("init.md")`, :49 `const stable = `${source}\n\n## Live routes...``, and `Pages.read` is `fs.readFileString` (ext/core/pages.ts:127-130) — and `packages/server/pages/init.md` contains `# Work with comms`, with zero occurrences of `# comms` at both master and this head (`grep -c '# comms'` → 0). The PR replaces the literal with the actual file content (extension-routes.test.ts:140, :153-154, :171).

**Scenario.** A reviewer reading the body's validation paragraph ("The additional group's only failure was an outdated diagnostic instrumentation anchor") concludes the base suite was green and that this PR changed nothing outside descriptors. In fact `extension-routes.test.ts` fails on dd733fe, the repair rides along inside the descriptor PR, and a revert of the descriptor work would re-break the base. It also means nobody can use this branch's green/red signal to judge whether the descriptor change broke the suite.

**Suggestion.** Land `d6509bf` as its own PR against master so the base goes green independently, or at minimum state in the body that this commit fixes a pre-existing failure in `extension-routes.test.ts` caused by `782d694`'s rewrite of `pages/init.md`. The replacement assertion itself is an improvement and should be kept.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed end to end. `git show origin/master:packages/server/pages/init.md | grep -c '# comms'` is 0 at both master and this head (the heading is `# Work with comms`, init.md:6), and /init serves the page source plus a route table and version stamp with nothing matching '# comms' (packages/server/src/onboarding.ts:36, :48, :51; the 503 fallback at :71 is 'Onboarding is unavailable.'). Master's packages/server/test/extension-routes.test.ts therefore asserts `toContain("# comms")` against text that cannot contain it — the case is red on dd733fe, and d6509bf (one commit, 5 insertions) repairs it by reading the served source (extension-routes.test.ts:140, :154-155, :174). Unmentioned in the PR body, which instead claims the only additional-group failure was a diagnostic anchor — that was d062269 (packages/server/test/fixtures/preparation-phases.ts:48). Minor is right: the replacement assertion is an improvement, the cost is that step 1's 'the suite passes untouched' becomes unverifiable from this branch and a revert of the descriptor work re-breaks the base.


#### A-2 [note] `FileStore` is a bare structural interface, so nine production sites build descriptors without ever going through the one parser

`packages/boot/src/app-backup.ts:48` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** §4.1's premise is "one opaque string, parsed in exactly one place". `parse` is indeed the only parser, but the descriptor *type* is an unbranded interface, so a descriptor that was never validated is indistinguishable from one that was, and boot constructs nine of them by hand.

**Evidence.** packages/storage/src/store.ts:4-7 declares `export interface FileStore { readonly _tag: "file"; readonly filename: string }` with no brand and no private constructor. Hand-built literals: app-backup.ts:13 and :48, app-recovery.ts:80 and :92, app-store-layout.ts:86, cutover.ts:216, restore-generation.ts:61, index.ts:65, linux-ownership.ts:136, plus server.ts:237 and sql-read-worker.ts:54. None of them passes through `parse`, so none of the invariants `parse` enforces (absolute, no traversal segment, no control characters, no empty segment) is checked for boot's own stores — only for the child environment, at boot-channel.ts:35 and linux-ownership.ts:111.

**Scenario.** Today every one of those paths is boot-derived, so nothing is reachable by an attacker and nothing is wrong at runtime. The cost lands at step 5: `withDatabase(store, cloneName)` has to mint descriptors for clone and restore targets, and with nine precedents for hand-construction the natural next move is a tenth literal — at which point a descriptor carrying an unvalidated, credential-bearing URL is typed identically to a parsed one, and the "parsed in exactly one place" guarantee is a convention rather than a property.

**Suggestion.** Brand the descriptor (`_brand` symbol field, or a `Schema.Struct` whose decoded type is the only way in) so the single construction path is `parse`/`render`/`withDatabase`, and export a cheap `fromFilename` for boot's own fixed paths that runs the same predicates. That converts nine judgement calls into one compiler rule before the credential-carrying variants exist.

**Skeptic.** Facts confirmed: store.ts:4-7 is an unbranded interface, and I count the hand-built literals exactly where claimed (app-backup.ts:13, :48; app-recovery.ts:80, :92; app-store-layout.ts:86; cutover.ts:216; restore-generation.ts:61; index.ts:65; linux-ownership.ts:136; server.ts:237; sql-read-worker.ts:54). But the design's §4.1 wording is "parsed in exactly one place", and parse IS the only parser (store.ts:21); constructing a descriptor from a boot-owned literal path is not parsing, and every one of those paths is boot-derived. The finding is a forward-looking shape argument with no current defect and no design row it violates, so I downgrade it to a note.


#### A-5 [note] The import guard has no test of its own; the new storage clause is exercised only positively

`scripts/check-invariants.ts:41` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** Nothing in the repository asserts what `checkInvariants` rejects. The new clause is proven only by the fact that the build passes while boot and server import `@comms/storage`.

**Evidence.** `grep -rn 'checkInvariants|scripts/check' packages/*/test` returns nothing; the only reference to the script anywhere outside docs is `package.json:16` (`"check:invariants": "bun scripts/check-invariants.ts"`). The forbidden directions this PR's design depends on — `ui → storage` (the README's "must never enter browser source", packages/storage/docs/README.md:5) and `storage → boot`/`storage → server` ("imports neither application nor boot code") — are never exercised. `walk(packages)` at :148 does cover `packages/storage`, so the rules exist; they are just untested.

**Scenario.** A refactor that reorders the `allowed` disjunction at :40-44, or that changes `workspace()` at :16-19, can silently stop rejecting `ui → storage`. The UI bundle then pulls `@effect/sql-sqlite-bun` into browser source and nobody learns until the bundle breaks or grows.

**Suggestion.** A small table-driven test over a temp package tree (or just a refactor of `checkInvariants` to return `failures` instead of setting `process.exitCode`) that asserts one allowed and three forbidden specifiers. Roughly 20 lines, and it is the only mechanical guard on the trust boundary.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed: the only references to the script anywhere are package.json:16 and its own definition/call at check-invariants.ts:7 and :157. No test exercises a forbidden specifier, and the script signals via process.exitCode (:152) rather than returning failures, which is why it is awkward to test. Note is right.


#### A-7 [note] Section 12.4's fixture consolidation is admitted but unscheduled: 67 test files and 84 `SqliteClient.layer` call sites remain, and no step now owns them

`packages/storage/test/store.test.ts:1` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** Previous ask (c) asked for the consolidation to be scheduled explicitly rather than dropped silently. The body now admits it, which is progress, but it assigns it to no step, and the design still places it in step 1 while arguing that deferring it is strictly more expensive.

**Evidence.** Measured in the worktree: 67 test files contain `SqliteClient.layer` (84 occurrences), against the design's count of 47 at docs/database.md:1051. `COMMS_TEST_ENGINE` appears nowhere in `packages/`, `scripts/` or `deployment/` (only in docs). `packages/boot/test/fixtures/store.ts` exists but is the pre-existing `bun:sqlite` inspection helper invoked as a subprocess by packages/server/test/fixtures/conversation.ts:18-25, unrelated to a client-layer fixture. Exactly one `SqliteClient.layer` remains in all of `packages/*/src` — packages/storage/src/client.ts:12 — so the *production* routing is complete; it is only the test surface that is untouched. The PR body's sentence is "Credential-URL scrubbing at stored stderr boundaries and broader engine fixture consolidation must land before enabling remote engines."

**Scenario.** The design's own argument (docs/database.md:1051-1055) is that this change is provably behaviour-preserving only while one engine exists, and that without it "each engine means writing the suite again, which is how a third dialect stops being maintained". Gating it on "before enabling remote engines" puts it in the same PR as the first engine, i.e. exactly when it stops being provably behaviour-preserving — and the file count has grown from 47 to 67 since the design was written, so the cost is compounding.

**Suggestion.** Name the step. Either add it to this PR as a mechanical follow-up commit, or add a step 4b to docs/database.md §13 that owns it before step 5, and correct §12.4's "47 test files" to the measured 67 so the next estimate starts from the truth.

**Skeptic.** Counts re-measured in the worktree and they match: 67 test files contain SqliteClient.layer, 84 occurrences; exactly one src occurrence remains (packages/storage/src/client.ts:12); zero COMMS_TEST_ENGINE anywhere outside docs; zero redactUrls. database-design.md:1051 still says 47 files and places the work in step 1 while arguing deferral is strictly more expensive, and the body's sentence gates it on "before enabling remote engines" without naming a step. Real, and minor is right for a scheduling/cost finding.


#### A-8 [note] Three design-doc corrections this PR earns but does not make

`packages/storage/src/store.ts:9` (design-conformance)

**Claim.** The implementation adds an error code and relocates a module in ways §4.2 and §13 step 1 have not caught up with, and §13 step 1 still describes work that has moved or changed shape. The doc was updated for this PR's two *accepted* deviations (§4.3, §4.4, and the §17 rows) but not for these.

**Evidence.** (1) store.ts:9 declares `store_descriptor_mismatch`, which docs/database.md:258-269's `StoreError` code list omits — yet docs/database.md:336 relies on the code by name. (2) docs/database.md:252 still heads the §4.2 snippet with the comment `// packages/boot/src/store.ts` even though docs/database.md:316 now says the module lives in `packages/storage`, and docs/database.md:1067 still opens step 1 with "Add `packages/boot/src/store.ts`". (3) docs/database.md:235 says "`file:` takes a single-slash absolute path with each segment percent-encoded" but does not say the decoded filename is re-validated, which is what store.ts:30-39 actually does and what makes `%2e%2e` safe (pinned at packages/storage/test/store.test.ts:22).

**Scenario.** The next implementer reads §4.2's code list, finds no `store_descriptor_mismatch`, and either re-invents it under another name or treats §4.4's reference as an error. The stale `packages/boot/src/store.ts` paths send them to a file that does not exist.

**Suggestion.** Add `store_descriptor_mismatch` to the §4.2 literal list; fix the snippet comment and step 1's path to `packages/storage/src/store.ts`; add one clause to §4.1 recording that validation happens on the decoded path, not the raw string, so traversal and control characters are rejected after decoding. Also worth recording in §4.4: there is no precedence between `APP_STORE` and `APP_DATABASE` — disagreement is fatal at both the child and the keeper — because "precedence" is what a reader will otherwise assume.

**Skeptic.** All three verified. (1) store.ts:9 declares store_descriptor_mismatch; the design's §4.2 literal list (database-design.md:265-272) has store_engine_mismatch but not store_descriptor_mismatch, while :336 relies on the new code by name. (2) database-design.md:256 still heads the snippet `// packages/boot/src/store.ts` and :1067 still opens step 1 with "Add packages/boot/src/store.ts", both contradicted by :252 and :316. (3) §4.1's rule at :246 describes per-segment encoding but not the post-decode re-validation that store.ts:30-39 performs. Doc hygiene, note.


#### B-5 [note] Fixture consolidation is gated on the wrong boundary, and the grammar has no form for the 12 in-memory fixtures

`packages/boot/test/fixtures/store.ts:1` (durability-regressions)

**Claim.** Design §12.4 puts the 47-file fixture consolidation in step 1 precisely because it is provably behaviour-preserving only while one engine exists. The body now admits the deferral but gates it on "before enabling remote engines", which is the same PR as the second engine — the moment the proof is gone. The count at this head is 67 files, up from 28 at the investigation and 39 at the earlier reviews.

**Evidence.** `grep -rl 'SqliteClient.layer' packages/*/test` → 67 files; `grep -rn COMMS_TEST_ENGINE packages scripts` → 0; `packages/boot/test/fixtures/store.ts:1-16` is still the `bun:sqlite` inspection helper, unrelated to the planned factory. PR body: "Credential-URL scrubbing at stored stderr boundaries and broader engine fixture consolidation must land before enabling remote engines." Twelve of those files open `:memory:` (e.g. fixtures/event-delivery.ts:150, fixtures/event-filters.ts:137, fixtures/request-event-contention.ts:81), which `parse` rejects and for which §4.1 defines no descriptor form.

**Scenario.** Deferred to the PR that adds pglite, the consolidation stops being a mechanical refactor and becomes 67 file edits whose behaviour-preservation can no longer be checked against a single engine — which is how the third dialect stops being maintained. The `:memory:` gap is concrete: whoever writes `test/fixtures/store.ts` must either invent an in-memory descriptor or carve out an exception, and neither is written down anywhere today.

**Suggestion.** Name the step in the body (its own PR after #4 and before the pg branch, while SQLite is still the only engine) and add the `:memory:` decision to design §12.4 so the factory's contract is settled before 67 files depend on it.

**Skeptic.** Counts confirmed: 67 test files under packages/*/test reference SqliteClient.layer (42 boot, 25 server; a whole-tree git grep adds only 6 vendored repos/effect files), 0 occurrences of COMMS_TEST_ENGINE anywhere in packages or scripts, 12 test files open ':memory:', and packages/boot/test/fixtures/store.ts:1-16 is still the bun:sqlite inspector. Master has the identical 67, so the PR performed no consolidation. The gating argument is sound against design line 1053, which explicitly places this in step 1 'because at that point the change is provably behaviour-preserving', while the body gates it on 'before enabling remote engines'. The ':memory:' gap is real — §4.1's grammar has no in-memory form and store.ts:31 rejects anything not starting with '/'. Note is the correct severity: disclosed deferral plus an unrecorded design decision, no behaviour at risk.


### Item status


#### design-conformance

From the design-conformance-and-descriptor angle, PR #2 is a faithful and in places better-than-specified implementation of step 1's descriptor half, with step 1's identity half and the fixture half deferred (both disclosed). All three previous asks were acted on: (a) `render` now returns `Redacted.Redacted<string>` (store.ts:17) and the test asserts the wrapper actually hides the value (store.test.ts:9) — fixed; (b) `redactUrls` is still absent from all of `packages/`, but the body now states the deferral plainly instead of implying the parser covers it — fixed as a disclosure; (c) the §12.4 consolidation is admitted in the body but assigned to no step, while the measured surface has grown from the design's 47 files to 67 (84 call sites) — partly.

The parser itself is the strongest part. I re-ran its predicate set outside Effect: it accepts exactly the single-slash `file:` form, refuses `file://`, `file:///`, relative paths, query strings, fragments, empty/`.`/`..` segments and control characters, validates after decoding so `%2e%2e` cannot smuggle traversal, and wraps its one throwing call (`decodeURIComponent`) in `Effect.try` so a malformed escape becomes `store_descriptor_invalid` rather than a defect. `postgres:`/`mysql:` get a distinct `store_engine_unsupported` code and the test proves no password appears in that failure. Two deviations are improvements the design has already absorbed (the `@comms/storage` workspace; both env variables emitted and cross-checked rather than renamed), and two more are improvements the design has *not* yet absorbed and should: the keeper validating the descriptor against the alias before file authorization and rewriting both for its private rehearsal copy (linux-ownership.ts:109-112, :136-137, with `mismatch`/`invalid`/`valid` covered in the container acceptance at linux-keeper-acceptance.sh:198-237), and the decision to make a disagreeing pair fatal rather than define a precedence.

Eight findings, none a blocker, no core guarantee touched. The two worth acting on before merge are small: `render` and `parse` disagree on the control-character class, so `render` can silently emit a descriptor the keeper will reject on every attempt with only a bare code to show for it (A-1); and `check-invariants.ts:43` still permits `server → boot` wholesale, so the invariant this PR's entire workspace-relocation argument rests on is unenforced — and unenforceable by the test suite, because the fixtures hand every staged generation `packages/server/node_modules`, where `@comms/boot` does resolve (A-4). The rest are shape-for-later costs: the descriptor is an unbranded interface that nine production sites construct by hand, bypassing the one parser (A-2); `BootChannel` still carries `filename: string`, so the read worker's wire message becomes a third credential boundary that the PR body's deferral list does not name (A-3); `AppRecovery.store` is a constant rather than the specified `Ref`, silently deferred rather than listed (A-6); and three small corrections the design doc has earned but not taken (A-8).

| status | item | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| improvement | §13 step 1 — `store.ts` with the parser, `file:` scheme only | packages/storage/src/store.ts:21-41 implements `parse`, in a new `@comms/storage` workspace rather than `packages/boot/src/store.ts`. The relocation is correct and the design has already absorbed it (docs/database.md:316, §17 row "The server re-exports boot's store.ts"). Packaging is complete and consistent: stage-runtime.ts:15-20 generalizes the protocol block into a workspace loop, Dockerfile copies `packages/storage/{package.json,src}` into both stages, .dockerignore un-ignores `packages/storage/docs`, and packages/server/runtime/package.json lists `storage` as a workspace and a dependency. |
| fixed | Grammar accepts exactly `file:` and nothing else (pg/mysql typed-refused at this step) | store.ts:23 refuses `/^(postgres\|postgresql\|mysql):/i` with `store_engine_unsupported`; :24 requires the `file:/` single-slash form and rejects `file://`; everything else is `store_descriptor_invalid`. I re-ran the predicate set outside Effect: `file://host/db`, `file:///db`, `file:/`, `file:app.db`, `app.db`, `file:/a?mode=ro`, `file:/a#fragment`, `file:/a//db`, `file:/a/../db`, `file:/a/%2e%2e/db`, `file:/a%00db`, `file:/a\db`, `file:/%ZZ` all reject; `postgres://…` and `POSTGRESQL://…` both give `store_engine_unsupported`. Pinned at packages/storage/test/store.test.ts:12-36. |

Two gaps: `render` emits an unparseable descriptor for a filename containing `\x01-\x1f` (A-1), and the mapping is not injective — a hand-written `file:/data/a%2Fb.db` decodes to `/data/a/b.db`, so `%2F` introduces path structure that per-segment encoding is meant to prevent. Credential round-trip is not applicable and not implemented: there is no pg/mysql descriptor. |

Nothing else in store.ts can throw. The step-1 acceptance line "a message naming the variable" is not met, though: StoreError carries only `code` (:8-13) and the design's `detail` field was dropped, so the failure text is the bare code. |

The PR body now states it plainly: "Credential-URL scrubbing at stored stderr boundaries and broader engine fixture consolidation must land before enabling remote engines." That is the disclosure the previous review asked for, and with `file:` there is no credential to leak, so nothing is exposed today. |

Errors never echo input: StoreError has no `detail` field and store.test.ts:34 asserts no `secret` in a failed pg parse. The keeper's `Effect.orDie` at linux-ownership.ts:111 surfaces only the code. No boot HTTP route returns the store path — `grep` over boot's `*http*.ts`, proxy.ts and events.ts finds no `filename`. The read worker's stderr is discarded (sql-read.ts:31 `stderr: "ignore"`). Three future sites stand out and only two are on the PR's list: the two stderr boundaries (disclosed) and the read-worker wire message (A-3). |

Exactly one `SqliteClient.layer` occurrence remains across all of `packages/*/src` (this one); the six former direct sites now route through it (app-backup.ts:26, :41, :48; app-recovery.ts:80; app-store-layout.ts:86; index.ts:65; server.ts:237; sql-read-worker.ts:54). |

That is better than a precedence rule — there is no silent winner. The alias is load-bearing, not cosmetic, and is pinned by a real integration test: packages/server/test/store-descriptor-compatibility.test.ts rewrites boot-channel.ts into its pre-descriptor form (:15-20, with :21-22 asserting the rewrite actually happened), runs the full server against it, fails a candidate that deletes rows, and checks fallback plus restart preserve acknowledged rows and accept new writes. #### durability-regressions

From the durability angle, the rethreading is correct. I read all thirteen named files in full in the worktree and compared every store-opening site against master: the eight former `SqliteClient.layer` call sites map one-to-one onto `clientLayer` with identical filenames and identical options (the read worker keeps `readonly: true` plus `busyTimeout: \"100 millis\"`; `disableWAL: true` is pinned inside the factory and cannot be overridden), and the five `supervisor.launch` callers pass exactly the store master passed — clone for both rehearsal paths (cutover.ts:216, restore-generation.ts:61), `recovery.store` for the three candidate paths (supervisor.ts:262, cutover.ts:246, database-restore.ts:146). No rehearsal, candidate, keeper or read-only worker can reach a store it could not reach before; `parse` rejects `..` after decoding, so the privileged keeper cannot be pointed at `boot.db` by a percent-encoded descriptor; the child receives the app store only and boot's store is never rendered anywhere; and no error path stringifies a descriptor, because `StoreError` carries nothing but a code. The keeper's cross-check of descriptor against alias before file authorization, and the keeper-acceptance block that proves a rehearsal writes only its private copy while the live counter holds, are both improvements the design did not ask for. The compatibility test is the right test and stronger than the design required: a real alias-only generation, a candidate that deletes rows and dies, then acknowledged rows intact across fallback and a restart. I found one behavioural delta not announced as such: boot never resolves its data directory before rendering, so a relative (or backslash-containing) `DATA_DIR` now makes every child refuse at startup with a bare `store_descriptor_invalid` — fail-closed, and better than master's silent split between boot's file and the child's, but undiagnosable and one `path.resolve` away from being impossible. The gaps are all in the test and bookkeeping layer: the descriptor-only contract (APP_STORE with no alias) is executed by nothing in the suite, the child-side mismatch refusal has no end-to-end test in the non-isolated path where it is the only enforcement, one of the four commits silently repairs a base-branch test failure unrelated to descriptors, and §12.4's fixture consolidation is now gated on the exact boundary where it stops being provably behaviour-preserving. The structural reservation is that the descriptor is decomposed back to a filename at every open site — `boot.store` from design §4.4 never exists — so `FileStore` is an unvalidated literal and all eight sites plus the `ReadRequest` wire must be revisited at steps 5 and 6.

No `@effect/sql-sqlite-bun` or `bun:sqlite` import remains in boot/src, server/src or storage/src outside client.ts. |

### Skeptic corrections


**design-conformance.** Three rows I disagree with, all in the direction of being too generous; everything else I checked matched.

1. "A malformed descriptor fails with a typed error rather than throwing — fixed". Its own evidence contradicts the status: the step-1 acceptance text at database-design.md:1073 requires "a message naming the variable", and StoreError carries only `code` (packages/storage/src/store.ts:8-13, `override get message()` returning `this.code` at :11-13); the design's `detail: Schema.optionalKey(Schema.String)` field (database-design.md:275) was dropped. The typed-error half is genuinely fixed (the one throwing call is wrapped at :26-29). This row should read "partly".

2. "Redaction end to end … — fixed". Overstated in two places. (a) The alias is an unredacted parallel channel for the same selection: supervisor.ts:143 emits `APP_DATABASE: store.filename` in plaintext and boot-channel.ts:34 reads it with `Config.String`, not `Config.Redacted` — harmless for `file:` but it means the redaction discipline covers only one of the two variables boot emits. (b) The finder enumerates "three future sites" and misses a fourth that crosses a privilege boundary: the entire child config, env map included, is JSON-encoded at child-process.ts:56 and handed to the keeper as `COMMS_CHILD_CONFIG`, which the keeper decodes at child-keeper.ts:10-13 with `Schema.decodeEffect(...).pipe(Effect.orDie)`. A decode defect there is printed on keeper stderr, and boot captures keeper stderr into the 8 KiB tail at child-process.ts:101-105 that ends up in `generations.stderr` and in failure bodies, scrubbed only by `redactHex`'s 64-hex pattern (auth-primitives.ts:50) — which covers BOOT_SECRET and WRITER_EPOCH and would not touch a connection URL. Status should be "partly", with this boundary added to the list that needs scrubbing before step 5.

3. "Percent-encoded round-trip — partly" is the right status but the stated cause is wrong. render encodes per segment (store.ts:18) while parse decodes the whole remainder in one call (store.ts:27 `decodeURIComponent(raw.slice(5))`). That asymmetry, not "per-segment encoding", is why `%2F` turns into a separator; the separate post-decode segment check at :34-37 is what keeps `%2e%2e` safe. Worth stating correctly because the fix for A-1 touches the same two lines.

Rows I independently confirmed as stated: the parser/grammar row (store.ts:21-41 plus the test matrix at test/store.test.ts:12-36); `render` returning `Redacted.Redacted<string>` (store.ts:17, test :9); `clientLayer` pinning `disableWAL` after the options spread (client.ts:12) with exactly one `SqliteClient.layer` left in all of packages/*/src and every former site translated without an option change (I diffed all six against origin/master); the keeper's descriptor-versus-alias check and rehearsal rewrite (linux-ownership.ts:109-112, :114-123, :136-137) with the container acceptance at linux-keeper-acceptance.sh:198-237 including the probe's own `APP_STORE === "file:" + APP_DATABASE` assertion at :202; boot's credential never entering the child env (supervisor.ts:138-149, index.ts:65); `server → boot` unqualified at check-invariants.ts:43; no test of the guard; the two `fs.exists` checks surviving (app-recovery.ts:15, cutover.ts:208); and workspace hygiene — packages/storage/package.json declares its own `@effect/sql-sqlite-bun` dependency with subpath-only exports, stage-runtime.ts:15-20 loops `["protocol","storage"]`, Dockerfile:8/15/26/32 copies it into both stages, .dockerignore:14-16 un-ignores it, server/package.json:20 and runtime/package.json list it, linux.yml:62-64 runs its suite.


**durability-regressions.** Two rows need correcting; the rest I verified and agree with.

1. "Keeper validates the descriptor against the alias before file authorization ... status: improvement" over-credits. The live-path equality check it cites is not new: master already had `if (filename !== "/data/store/comms.db") return yield* Effect.die("Invalid live database")` at packages/boot/src/linux-ownership.ts:110, and head just moves it to :114 over the decoded filename. What is actually new and an improvement is only :109-112 (`const descriptor = config.env.APP_STORE; if (!descriptor) die; const store = yield* childStore(descriptor, config.env.APP_DATABASE).pipe(Effect.orDie)`) running before any chown/chmod, plus the :136-137 dual rewrite. The traversal-blocking claim is correct (store.ts:30-39 rejects `..` after decodeURIComponent, so `file:/data/%2e%2e/boot.db` cannot reach the chown at :120-121).

2. "No acknowledged write can be lost by the rethreading ... status: fixed" is right about the rethreading but overstates the evidence base at this head. The keeper is the only component that chowns/chmods the live store (linux-ownership.ts:113-123) and its new descriptor code runs only under `isolated`; nothing in `bun run test` executes packages/boot/src/linux-ownership.ts — it is reached only through child-keeper.ts:1/preparation-keeper.ts:1 inside the image, and .github/workflows/linux.yml:76-78 exercises it exclusively in the `image` job via scripts/linux-keeper-acceptance.sh, which the PR body states has not been run at this head ("Full combined-suite and new-head Linux/image/QEMU acceptance remain pending"). The row should read "fixed by inspection; keeper path unexecuted at this head", which is a disclosed status, not a hidden one.

Minor: the summary lists `packages/storage/src/descendant.ts` as part of the new workspace; the workspace contains only src/store.ts, src/client.ts, test/store.test.ts, docs/README.md, package.json and tsconfig.json (the file does not exist at 1ef641e).

Rows I re-verified and agree with as written: the 8-site clientLayer mapping (master's 8 sites confirmed by `git grep SqliteClient.layer origin/master`, same filenames and options, `readonly: true, busyTimeout: "100 millis"` preserved at sql-read-worker.ts:54, `disableWAL: true` pinned at storage/src/client.ts:12 and unreachable from the options type); every launch caller passing the store master passed (supervisor.ts:262, cutover.ts:216/:246, restore-generation.ts:61, database-restore.ts:146); child env carries the app store only (boot store built inline at index.ts:65, never rendered; child-process.ts:63 passes COMMS_CHILD_CONFIG in memory only); read worker env `{}` (sql-read.ts:27); `render` returning Redacted (store.ts:17); the APP_STORE/alias pair and the compatibility test (store-descriptor-compatibility.test.ts:7-57 — a real source-rewritten alias-only generation, a candidate that `DELETE FROM messages` then dies, rows intact across fallback and restart at :45/:52-55); `AppRecovery.store` as a plain const with `filename` retained and still driving supervisor.ts:265-266 and cutover.ts:208 ("partly"); `fs.exists` retained at app-recovery.ts:15 and cutover.ts:208 with no `store_identity` ("deferred-honestly"); zero `redactUrls`, `dialectOf`, `withDatabase`, `asBoot`; the step-1 variable-naming acceptance unmet; check-invariants.ts:41 permitting only boot→storage and server→storage; d062269 as a one-line anchor update (preparation-phases.ts:48) and 1ef641e as the boot-event-wait descriptor fixture.


### What the skeptics found that the finders missed


**design-conformance.** The compatibility alias that this PR promotes from cosmetic to load-bearing is hard-wired to a file-only field, and nothing in the PR or the design says what it becomes for the engines it exists to carry generations across. `childStore` compares the alias to `store.filename` — packages/storage/src/store.ts:44-50: `legacy !== undefined && legacy !== store.filename ? Effect.fail(new StoreError({ code: "store_descriptor_mismatch" }))` — and the producer side is `APP_DATABASE: store.filename` at packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:143, with the consumer reading it as `Config.String` at packages/server/src/kernel/boot-channel.ts:34 and the keeper cross-checking it at packages/boot/src/linux-ownership.ts:111.

Once `StoreDescriptor` becomes the union at database-design.md:260-263, `store.filename` does not exist on the postgres/mysql arms, so at step 5 every one of those four sites needs a decision that has not been made: emit `store.database` (and the pair no longer identifies the same thing, so the mismatch check stops meaning what it means today), emit the credential URL in plaintext beside the deliberately `Redacted` APP_STORE, or drop the alias — which is the one option §4.4 forbids until a per-generation contract stamp exists so boot can refuse an unstamped snapshot (database-design.md:336). That stamp is not implemented here and is not in the PR body's deferral list, which names only stderr scrubbing and fixture consolidation. So the mechanism the PR adds specifically to keep pre-descriptor rollback targets launchable has an undefined form in the only scenario that motivated the descriptor, and its removal is blocked on unscheduled work.

Concretely: this is the line that decides whether a Postgres-era deployment can still roll back to a generation retained from the SQLite era, i.e. the "a broken generation never becomes live, and a human can always get back in" guarantee at its weakest joint. It is cheap to fix now — give `childStore` an explicit per-engine alias rule (file: compare to `filename`; remote: compare to `database` or require absence) and add one sentence to §4.4 plus a line to the PR body naming the contract stamp as the prerequisite for alias removal — and expensive to fix at step 5, when four call sites and every retained snapshot are already committed to the current shape.


**durability-regressions.** The descriptor variable is mandatory in the new child but the alias it replaces is optional, so this PR is backward-compatible and forward-incompatible — and nothing in the stack notes it. packages/server/src/kernel/boot-channel.ts:33 is `const descriptor = yield* Config.Redacted("APP_STORE");` with no `withDefault`, while :34 gives the legacy alias `Config.withDefault(undefined)`. Master's supervisor set only `APP_DATABASE` (the `APP_STORE: Redacted.value(render(store))` at packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:142 is an addition in this diff), and the installed app source lives in `/data`, not the image — packages/server/docs/README.md:11 "Reuse the same `DATA_DIR` to keep your board; existing installations retain their installed source".

Concrete scenario: operator upgrades the image to this PR (retained pre-descriptor generations keep working, which is what the alias buys), then the source in `/data` is updated or edited to descriptor-aware code, so the live and last-good generations both read `APP_STORE`. The operator then rolls the image tag back — an ordinary deploy rollback, and the pre-descriptor boot is the one in that image. That boot never sets `APP_STORE`, so every candidate and every rehearsal child fails its `BootChannel` layer with a missing-config error before `COMMS_CHILD_PORT`, on the live generation and on every retained one; there is no good generation left to fall back to, and the only way back in is `/_boot` source editing. No acknowledged write is lost, but the board cannot come live, which is the other half of the rollback guarantee. The fix is one line and symmetric with what the PR already does for the other direction: give the descriptor a default derived from the alias (`Config.Redacted("APP_STORE")` falling back to `file:` over `APP_DATABASE`) and require that at least one of the pair is present, rather than hard-requiring the newer one.

Same variable, secondary: packages/server/test/backup-health-timeout.test.ts:22/:30/:32/:34 uses `options.env.APP_DATABASE` as the sentinel path for the backup-health stall, so the §4.4 follow-up that drops the alias will silently neuter a durability test rather than fail to compile — worth listing alongside the boot-channel default when that removal is scheduled.

`git status --short` is **empty** after `check` and `build` — `oxfmt --check` wrote nothing.

**Zero failures.** No failure to re-classify. The one skip is pre-existing and env-gated, untouched by this PR: `packages/server/test/cutover-acceptance.test.ts:20` `it.skipIf(process.env.COMMS_CUTOVER_ACCEPTANCE !== "1")`.

**Batch A** (every test file this PR touches + the two new files): `packages/storage/test/store.test.ts`, `packages/server/test/store-descriptor-compatibility.test.ts`, `packages/boot/test/kernel-route-guard.test.ts`, `packages/server/test/{backup-schedule,boot-handler-failure,boot-publication,boot-storage-refusal,extension-routes,generation-revert,migrations,rehearsal-rollback}.test.ts`, `packages/server/test/kernel/boot-event-wait.test.ts`. The two touched fixtures (`fixtures/preparation-phases.ts`, `fixtures/rehearsal.ts`) are consumed by `generation-revert` and `rehearsal-rollback` respectively, both in this batch. Slowest: `generation-revert > restores a retained generation's whole source and manifest through cutover` 76.7s; `> keeps source and live writes intact when generation dependency preparation fails` 35.1s.

**Batch B** (recovery / cutover / backup / restore / keeper, chosen because those are the paths whose `SqliteClient.layer` call sites and `supervisor.launch` signature this PR rewrites): boot `app-backup`, `app-store-layout`, `failed-recovery`, `child-keeper`, `watchdog`, `scheduled-backup`, `backup-metadata`, `supervisor`, `combined-restore-migration`; server `cutover`, `cutover-acceptance`, `cutover-rollback-restart`, `accepted-cutover-cleanup`, `database-restore`, `database-restore-restart`, `combined-restore-restart`, `kernel-recovery`, `restart`, `backup-failure`, `rehearsal-effects`, `rehearsal-sequence`.

### Claim-by-claim (PR body + `packages/storage/docs/README.md`)

| Claim | Status |

### Prev-review follow-ups (at `2b3e365`)

- **(a) `render` returns `Redacted.Redacted` — done.** `store.ts:17-18` returns `Redacted.make(...)`; the only `Redacted.value` unwraps are the two child-environment boundaries (`supervisor.ts:142`, `linux-ownership.ts:136`) plus test fixtures. README line 9 states the rule.
- **(b) `redactUrls` — done as an explicit deferral** in the body, sentence 3 of paragraph 3. The body no longer implies the parser covers it.
- **(c) §12.4 fixture consolidation — only partly addressed.** See note 3.

### Findings

**Note 1 — the body's validation claim names `d062269`, but HEAD is `1ef641e`; two commits are outside the stated evidence.**
`d6509bf` (`extension-routes.test.ts`) and `1ef641e` (`kernel/boot-event-wait.test.ts`) are test-only and land *after* the commit the body vouches for. Cost: a reader takes "Validation at `d062269`" as head validation. I ran both at head and both pass, so this is a stale sentence, not a gap. Fix: change the sentence to name `1ef641e`, or add one line saying the two later commits are test-only corrections.

**Note 2 — the descriptor stops at `BootChannel`; the app side still carries a bare path, and src has nine inline `{ _tag: "file", filename }` literals.**
`boot-channel.ts:35-36` computes `store` then immediately discards it: `const filename = store.filename;`, and the service exposes `filename` at `:45` and `:209`, never `store`. So `server.ts:237` rebuilds `clientLayer({ _tag: "file", filename: boot.filename })` and `sql-read-worker.ts:54` does the same from `request.filename`. Same pattern in `app-recovery.ts:80`, `app-store-layout.ts:86`, `app-backup.ts:48`, `index.ts:65`. Concrete cost: when PR #5/#6 make a store a URL, every one of these literals is a second edit at a site this PR already touched — the descriptor is currently a wrapper around the same string rather than the carried value. `AppRecovery` got this right (`app-recovery.ts:92,101-102` exposes `store` *and* `filename`); `BootChannel` did not. Not claimed done in the body, so this is a note, not a major.

**Note 3 — prev-review item (c) is mentioned but still not scheduled: 67 test files import `SqliteClient` directly.**
`grep -rln SqliteClient packages/*/test` → 67 files; `grep -rln "new Database(" packages/*/test` → 10. The body's only reference is "broader engine fixture consolidation must land before enabling remote engines" — a gate, not a schedule, with no count. Production is fully consolidated (the *only* `SqliteClient` reference left outside tests is `packages/storage/src/client.ts:1`), so design §13 step 1's "route the 6 source sites" is genuinely done and only the fixture half is outstanding. Cost of leaving it unquantified: the reviewer of PR #5 cannot tell whether the remaining work is 5 files or 67. Suggest replacing that clause with the number.

**Note 4 — the design doc needs one correction that has not landed.**
`docs/database.md:1417` and §4.4 (`:326-336`) already carry the PR #2 correction for the `APP_DATABASE` alias (added in the merged base, attributed "From PR #2, 2026-09-11"), so the rename row is fixed. But §13 step 1 at `docs/database.md:1067` still reads: *"Add `packages/boot/src/store.ts` with the parser, `dialectOf`, `withDatabase`, `asBoot`, `render`, `redactUrls` and `clientLayer` … Rename `APP_DATABASE` to `APP_STORE`. Replace `AppRecovery.filename` with `AppRecovery.store`, a `Ref`. Add the `store_identity` table … Apply `redactUrls` at `supervisor.ts:78` and `cutover.ts:263`, `:273`."* Four of those are false of what shipped: the module is `packages/storage/src/store.ts` (a separate workspace, which is an **improvement** — it is what lets the frozen editable runtime resolve it, per `docs/build-plan.md:149`); `dialectOf`/`withDatabase`/`asBoot`/`redactUrls` do not exist; `AppRecovery.store` is a plain field, not a `Ref` (`app-recovery.ts:92,101`); `store_identity` is PR #3. The design doc should get a §13-step-1 correction row matching the §17 one, splitting step 1 into what this PR does and what PR #3 does.

**Note 5 — one new lint warning, introduced by this PR.**
`packages/storage/src/store.ts:46: warning effecttsgo(flat-map-conditional-to-filter-or-fail)` — `childStore`'s `Effect.flatMap` with an identity `Effect.succeed` branch. Warnings are non-fatal (`bun run check` exits 0 and the repo already carries ~18), but this is the only one the PR adds. `Effect.filterOrFail` is a two-line change.

**Note 6 — the compatibility test synthesizes its legacy generation by regex surgery on the live source.**
`store-descriptor-compatibility.test.ts:15-20` deletes an exact import line and matches `/\tconst descriptor = yield\* Config.Redacted\("APP_STORE"\);[\s\S]*?\tconst filename = store.filename;/` against a copy of `packages/server/src`. Any reformatting of `boot-channel.ts:33-36` (tabs are literal in the pattern) silently stops producing a legacy channel. It is guarded — `:21` `expect(legacy).not.toContain("APP_STORE")` and `:22` `expect(legacy).not.toBe(channel)` make it fail loudly rather than pass vacuously — so this is a maintenance cost, not a hole.

**Improvement worth keeping — `APP_STORE` is mandatory, with no fallback to the alias alone.**
`boot-channel.ts:33` is `Config.Redacted("APP_STORE")` with no `withDefault`, while `:34` gives `APP_DATABASE` a default of `undefined`. A child that receives only the legacy alias refuses to start rather than quietly opening the aliased path, and `childStore` (`store.ts:44-51`) refuses a pair naming two different files. That asymmetry is the right direction and is stronger than the design doc's §4.4 text requires; it is also what makes `linux-ownership.ts:110`'s `Effect.die("Missing app store descriptor")` a real fence rather than a formality.
