## PR #3 — store identity, at `f4f1dca`

**Verdict: needs-changes.** Finder verdicts ['mergeable-with-changes', 'needs-changes'], skeptic verdicts ['mergeable-with-changes', 'needs-changes']. 11 findings confirmed, 0 refuted: 1 blocker, 1 major, 4 minor, 5 note. Comment posted: https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/artifactory/pull/3#issuecomment-5644334307

| id | sev | finding | where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-1 | blocker | Adoption pins the absolute store path, so moving DATA_DIR or enabling the isolated layout refuses startup forever | `packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:92` |
| B-2 | major | reserveIdentity failing aborts journal recovery, disarming every in-band repair route the identity hint points at | `packages/boot/src/index.ts:152` |
| A-1 | minor | A foreign-board backup is refused as `app_store_missing`, and the hint points at two surfaces that expose no identity | `packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:143` |
| A-2 | minor | The previous implementation's `${filename}.restore` copy is never reclaimed, and this PR removes the one thing that used to overwrite it | `packages/boot/src/app-backup.ts:33` |
| B-3 | minor | The new upgrade gate fails the boot layer, so its reason reaches no route and the operator gets a retriable 503 that never succeeds | `packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:45` |
| B-5 | minor | Refusing to adopt a legacy store that is not a comms store surfaces as a raw SqlError, not a coded identity refusal | `packages/boot/src/app-recovery.ts:29` |
| A-3 | note | The legacy-adoption shape probes run ahead of the identity check and refuse with an untyped SqlError | `packages/boot/src/app-recovery.ts:29` |
| A-4 | note | Adoption classifies a populated app store under a fresh boot store as `legacy` and adopts it with an empty seq allocator | `packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:99` |
| A-5 | note | An app-authored migration can destroy store_identity, and the restore route cannot repair it because it fences the live store first | `packages/server/src/kernel/migrations.ts:45` |
| B-4 | note | f4f1dca changes supervisor status semantics in an identity PR without disclosing it, and the downgraded state keeps error:null | `packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:88` |
| B-6 | note | Identity proves which board, not which version of it: a stale self-copy passes every check | `packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:143` |


### B-1 [blocker] Adoption pins the absolute store path, so moving DATA_DIR or enabling the isolated layout refuses startup forever

`packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:92` (write-safety)

**Claim.** The adoption record stores the realpath-resolved absolute filename of the app store and `reserve` refuses with app_store_identity_invalid whenever the current selection differs, for a ready adoption as well as a pending one. Nothing ever rewrites that field, so any change to the resolved path permanently refuses startup — and because of B-2 there is no in-band way back. This also contradicts the design, which says a name mismatch is an info log, not a refusal (§10.3: "the URL's path is reported by /_boot/status next to the live name and otherwise ignored, with an `info` log when they differ").

**Evidence.** app-store-identity.ts:28-35 `const canonical = ... const parent = yield* fs.realPath(path.dirname(filename)); const selected = path.join(parent, path.basename(filename));`; :91-93 `if (state.adoption) { if (state.adoption.filename !== selected) return yield* invalid(); return state.adoption; }`; :98 the record is created with `filename: selected`; :118 `complete` rewrites only `phase`, keeping `saved.filename`. grep over packages shows the only writers of `app_store_adoption` are :102 and :118, so the path is never updated. The store path is cwd- and mode-dependent: README:14 `DATA_DIR="$PWD/data" bun run start` with README:29 "Keep the same DATA_DIR", and index.ts:50 `const appFilename = path.join(options.dataDirectory, isolated ? "store/comms.db" : "comms.db")`.

**Scenario.** Supported layout migration: a board runs non-isolated, so its adoption record holds `<root>/comms.db`. The operator switches to the image layout (Dockerfile:57 `COMMS_ISOLATED=true`). index.ts:150 `migrateAppStore` correctly renames the store to `<root>/store/comms.db` (app-store-layout.ts:92) and records app_store_layout='ready'. Two lines later index.ts:152 `reserveIdentity` computes selected=`<root>/store/comms.db`, compares it to the recorded `<root>/comms.db`, and fails app_store_identity_invalid. Recovery aborts (index.ts:155-176), no child is ever launched, and every repair route is refused (B-2); storage.md:98 tells the operator "do not delete identity markers to bypass this refusal". Same outcome for the reference SQLite deployment when the data directory moves: clone the repo to a second path, copy `data/` across as README:29 invites, and the board is bricked where before PR #3 a `POST /api/reload` regenerated the snapshots and recovered (application.ts:22 already compares stored snapshot_dir against realPath(dataDirectory)). A relative DATA_DIR run from a different cwd, a symlinked `comms.db`, or a volume remounted at a new path all trigger it identically. No test covers identity together with migrateAppStore: app-store-layout.test.ts exercises the migration with no adoption record present.

**Suggestion.** Enforce the filename equality only while the adoption is pending — that is the window it exists to protect (a UUID reserved for store A must not be completed against store B). For a `ready` adoption, treat a differing selection as a selection change: log it, report both the recorded and the selected store on /_boot/status, and rewrite `filename` in the finalizing transaction. Concretely, guard :92 with `state.adoption.phase === "pending"` and add a `UPDATE settings ... app_store_adoption` that re-stamps the path when a ready adoption's store moves. Whatever the refusal, include the two paths in /_boot/status evidence — today the operator is told only `app_store_identity_invalid` with no indication that a path moved. Also record in storage.md that the store selection is journaled, and correct the design's §10.3 bullet, which currently specifies an info log for exactly this case.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in code. app-store-identity.ts:28-34 resolves `selected` through fs.realPath; :91-93 `if (state.adoption) { if (state.adoption.filename !== selected) return yield* invalid(); return state.adoption; }` applies to a ready adoption as well as a pending one; :98 writes `filename: selected` and :118 rewrites only `phase`, keeping `saved.filename`. grep over packages shows the only writers of app_store_adoption are app-store-identity.ts:102 and :118, so the recorded path is never re-stamped. The layout-migration scenario is reachable exactly as described: index.ts:150 `if (isolated) yield* migrateAppStore(...)` renames `<root>/comms.db` to `<root>/store/comms.db` (app-store-layout.ts:92) and app-store-layout.ts touches no settings key other than app_store_layout (:67,:72,:102), then index.ts:152 reserveIdentity compares the new selection against the recorded one and fails app_store_identity_invalid. Nothing rewrites it, and boot/docs/storage.md:92-100 (the new section) never tells the operator the store path is journaled. Blocker stands: a supported operation (non-isolated to isolated layout, or any change to the realpath of DATA_DIR) becomes a permanent refusal, and because reserveIdentity sits inside the owners block the in-band repair routes are simultaneously disarmed (B-2). The only caveat to the severity is that the trigger requires the board to have already adopted under this code at the old path; boards upgrading from master have no adoption record and migrate cleanly.


### B-2 [major] reserveIdentity failing aborts journal recovery, disarming every in-band repair route the identity hint points at

`packages/boot/src/index.ts:152` (write-safety)

**Claim.** `reserveIdentity` is the last statement of the `owners` block, and a failure there short-circuits `coordinator.recover` and `restore.recover`, so `cutover.ready` and `databaseRestore.ready` stay false and every human repair route answers `cutover_recovery_required` / `restore_recovery_required` (409, non-retriable). The identity refusal's own hint instructs the operator to "restore the matching board", which is precisely the route this ordering disables. Before this PR the same conditions (missing or foreign app store) left recovery complete and the restore route armed.

**Evidence.** index.ts:151-155 `yield* (yield* AppBackup).recoverStaging; yield* (yield* AppRecovery).reserveIdentity; })).pipe(Effect.exit)`; :156 `const source = owners._tag === "Failure" ? owners : ...`; :161-169 `const recovered = owners._tag === "Failure" ? owners : yield* coordinator.recover.pipe(Effect.andThen(restore.recover), ...)`. The gates: database-restore.ts:266-267 `if (!(yield* Ref.get(ready)) || pending.cutover) return yield* new ChildError({ code: "restore_recovery_required" })` with `ready` set only at :259, inside `restore.recover`; cutover.ts:136-137 and :471-472 the same for reload/reset/revert with `ready` set at cutover.ts:109/:121. Refusal policy: child-error-policy.ts:3-7,30,47 → 409, retriable false. The hint that contradicts it: app-store-identity.ts:153 "restore the matching board rather than initializing or replacing its identity." The behaviour change is visible in the test the PR edited: backup-inventory.test.ts:153-166 moves from `{state:"failed", attempt:3}` to `{state:"failed", attempt:0, error: ...app_store_missing}` with the comment "Identity preflight refuses the missing initialized store before launching any child."

**Scenario.** A volume snapshot restore puts another board's `comms.db` under `<DATA_DIR>` (or this board's store is replaced by a foreign file). Pre-PR: startup recovery completed, `restore.ready` was true, and a human could sign in and `POST /_boot/db/restore` with a passkey assertion — prepare cloned the wrong store as the safety copy, `install` copied the correct artifact over it (database-restore.ts:140) and the board came back in band. Post-PR: index.ts:152 fails app_store_missing, neither `cutover.recover` nor `restore.recover` runs, and every attempt to restore returns 409 restore_recovery_required whose hint says "Repair the reported condition before retrying" — with no route that can repair it. The only remaining surfaces are auth, /_boot/status and event diagnostics; the fix requires filesystem access to the data directory. Combined with B-1 this is the difference between a recoverable and an unrecoverable board.

**Suggestion.** Do not let the identity refusal veto journal recovery. Either move `reserveIdentity` out of the `owners` block so `coordinator.recover`/`restore.recover` still run (the restore path re-verifies identity on its staging copy at app-backup.ts:70 and replaces the live store by rename, so arming it is safe), or keep the refusal where it is but arm the restore route explicitly for the identity-refused state and record the condition as a distinct RecoveryPhase so /_boot/status names both the refusal and the one route that can clear it. Either way, add a server test that restores a catalogued backup while the live store is missing or foreign, which is the case the whole identity mechanism exists to detect.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. index.ts:151-155 ends the `owners` block with `yield* (yield* AppRecovery).reserveIdentity`, and :161-169 short-circuits `coordinator.recover`/`restore.recover` on `owners._tag === "Failure"`. The gates are as cited: database-restore.ts:259-267 sets `ready` only at the end of `recover` and refuses with restore_recovery_required otherwise; cutover.ts:144/:156 set `ready` only inside `recover`, and :172/:472/:494 refuse reload/revert/reset. child-error-policy maps both to 409 non-retriable. The regression is real and visible in the PR's own test edit: packages/server/test/backup-inventory.test.ts:153-166 moves from `{state:"failed", attempt:3}` (children retrying, recovery Ready, restore armed) to `{state:"failed", attempt:0, error: app_store_missing}` with the comment "Identity preflight refuses the missing initialized store before launching any child." I also confirmed the refusal is at least visible (supervisor.ts:107-113 `fail` records the redacted cause in child.status, so /_boot/status names app_store_missing), and that the author did exempt the one case where the journal itself selects a backup (app-store-identity.ts:38-55,:85-90 `selectedBackup`) — that exemption precisely covers the resume paths that call backup.restore before prepare, so the gap is the operator-initiated restore, not the journal resume. Major is correct; it is one step from blocker because the hint at app-store-identity.ts:153 points at the route this ordering disables.


### A-1 [minor] A foreign-board backup is refused as `app_store_missing`, and the hint points at two surfaces that expose no identity

`packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:143` (adoption-protocol)

**Claim.** The restore path refuses a foreign artefact with a typed error, but the error neither names the mismatch nor distinguishes itself from an absent store, and nothing boot serves lets a human resolve it. `if (row.store_id !== adoption.store_id) return yield* new EventError({ code: "app_store_missing" });` reuses the code that app-recovery.ts:21 raises when the live store file is simply gone, and EventError carries only `code` (events.ts:29-55) — no expected or found id. The 409 body (backup-http.ts:66-76, database-restore-http.ts:55-65) is the fixed string "App store identity could not be verified." plus appIdentityPolicy.hint at app-store-identity.ts:153: "Inspect /_boot/status and the backup catalog; restore the matching board". The backup catalogue cannot answer that: backup-inventory.ts:19 selects only id, reason, bytes, taken_at, published_through, generation, and the Summary schema at :5-12 has no legacy_store_id and no identity. /_boot/status cannot either: a grep for app_store_id across packages/boot/src finds only app-store-identity.ts, so neither the live identity nor the adoption phase is surfaced anywhere.

**Evidence.** app-store-identity.ts:143 `if (row.store_id !== adoption.store_id) return yield* new EventError({ code: "app_store_missing" });`; app-store-identity.ts:153 hint "Inspect /_boot/status and the backup catalog; restore the matching board rather than initializing or replacing its identity."; backup-inventory.ts:5-12 `const Summary = Schema.Struct({ id, reason, bytes, taken_at, published_through, generation })`; app-store-identity.test.ts:179 `expect(await app.run("restore")).toContain("app_store_missing")` for a backup whose store_id was set to 'foreign'.

**Scenario.** An operator copies `backups/<id>.db` from a staging board into the production data directory (or restores a snapshot that mixed two boards' volumes), catalogues it, and POSTs /_boot/db/restore. They get 409 `app_store_missing` — the identical code and body they would get if /data/comms.db had been deleted — for an artefact and a live store that are both present and intact. They then GET /_boot/db/backups as the hint instructs and see six columns, none of which says which board any artefact belongs to, and GET /_boot/status, which never prints the live app_store_id. The only way to answer "is this the wrong board, or is my store gone?" is to open the SQLite files by hand outside comms, which is exactly what the hint's first sentence tells them not to do.

**Suggestion.** Split the code: keep `app_store_missing` for an absent table or row and add a distinct `app_store_foreign` (it already has a sibling precedent in `app_store_identity_invalid`, and events.ts:34, event-http.ts:35/111 and edit-failure.ts:60 show the per-code plumbing is three lines). Carry the two ids on the error so the 409 body can name them. Then surface `app_store_id` and the adoption phase on /_boot/status, and add the artefact's identity (or a boolean 'this board') to the Summary at backup-inventory.ts:5-12, so the hint's two named surfaces can actually be inspected.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in code. app-store-identity.ts:143 `if (row.store_id !== adoption.store_id) return yield* new EventError({ code: "app_store_missing" });` is the same code raised for an absent table/row at :130 and :140 and for an absent file at app-recovery.ts:21, and EventError carries only `code`. The hint at app-store-identity.ts:153 names two surfaces that cannot answer the question: backup-inventory.ts:19 selects only id,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation and the Summary at :5-12 has no identity column; `grep -rn app_store_id packages --include='*.ts'` returns no src hit outside app-store-identity.ts, so no status surface prints the live identity or the adoption phase. The test at app-store-identity.test.ts:179 and :106 shows the same string for a foreign artefact and for a foreign live store. Diagnostics/operability only, no guarantee broken, so minor is right.


### A-2 [minor] The previous implementation's `${filename}.restore` copy is never reclaimed, and this PR removes the one thing that used to overwrite it

`packages/boot/src/app-backup.ts:33` (adoption-protocol)

**Claim.** `recoverStaging` sweeps only the new path: `fs.remove(`${filename}.restore-staging`, { recursive: true, force: true })`. The shipped code before this PR staged at `${filename}.restore` (visible in the diff: `-						const temporary = `${filename}.restore`; -						yield* fs.copyFile(backup, temporary);`), so any deployment SIGKILLed between that copyFile and its rename carries a full board copy at that name today. The previous review's one consolation for the old design was that it self-healed — "the next restore's fs.copyFile overwrote it". After this PR no code path writes, reads or removes that name ever again, so the leftover becomes permanent. The test at app-store-identity.test.ts:132-137 writes both `comms.db.restore` and `comms.db.restore-journal`, runs a full restore and a full start, and asserts the journal file still contains "stale journal" — confirming nothing touches either. Nothing else will: artifact-retention.ts:29 says "Only catalogued boot-owned artifacts are considered; this is not a volume scanner", and these bytes belong to no catalogued row.

**Evidence.** app-backup.ts:33 `recoverStaging: fs.remove(`${filename}.restore-staging`, { recursive: true, force: true })`; the 1ef641e..HEAD diff of app-backup.ts removing `const temporary = `${filename}.restore``; app-store-identity.test.ts:132-137 writes `comms.db.restore` and `comms.db.restore-journal` and asserts the latter is preserved after a restore and a start; artifact-retention.ts:29; storage-headroom.ts:30-46 (`requireHeadroom` fails `storage_headroom` on `available_bytes - requiredBytes`).

**Scenario.** A board on the shipped image is killed (OOM, host reboot) during a database restore, leaving a full copy of comms.db at /data/comms.db.restore. The operator upgrades to this PR. The file is now immortal: the sweep at index.ts:151 does not match it, retention is catalogue-only, and /_boot/status lists no such artefact. Its bytes count directly against `requireHeadroom`'s 5% refusal at storage-headroom.ts:46, so on a volume sized close to the board the deployment can reach `storage_headroom` refusals on writes and captures that pruning cannot clear, with nothing on the status page to delete — the same failure the previous review blocked on, now bounded to one file instead of one per attempt.

**Suggestion.** Extend recoverStaging to the legacy name in the same effect: remove `${filename}.restore` plus its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` sidecars with `{ force: true }` alongside the `.restore-staging` directory. It runs at the same post-closure point, it is a one-line change, and it makes the upgrade self-healing for the state the previous implementation could leave.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed, including the regression half that makes it worth reporting. `git show 1ef641e:packages/boot/src/app-backup.ts` and `git show dd733fe:...` both stage at `const temporary = `${filename}.restore``, so the shipped pre-PR path is a fixed name that a later restore overwrote; app-backup.ts:33 sweeps only `${filename}.restore-staging`, and app-store-identity.test.ts:132-137 writes `comms.db.restore` plus `comms.db.restore-journal`, runs a restore and a start, and asserts the journal bytes are still "stale journal" — nothing reads, writes or removes those names any more. artifact-retention.ts is catalogue-only, so an upgrade from a board SIGKILLed mid-restore strands one board-sized file forever against `requireHeadroom`. One-line fix in the same effect. Minor is correct (single bounded file, requires a pre-upgrade crash).


### B-3 [minor] The new upgrade gate fails the boot layer, so its reason reaches no route and the operator gets a retriable 503 that never succeeds

`packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:45` (write-safety)

**Claim.** `BootIdentityUpgradePending` is raised from `initializeBootSchema`, which is built as part of the layer the entire boot graph depends on. The failure therefore happens before `installed` is replaced, so the bootloader serves only the static pre-graph handler: every route except /health, /.well-known/agent.json and the static GET /_boot returns 503 `boot_unavailable` with the hint "Retry the same request; check bootloader logs if it persists". The operator cannot authenticate, cannot read /_boot/status, and is told to retry a condition that is permanent until they redeploy the previous image.

**Evidence.** boot-schema.ts:39-45 `if (version >= 9 && version < 17) { ... if (cutovers.length > 0 || restores.length > 0) return yield* new BootIdentityUpgradePending({}); }`; index.ts:59-75 wraps it in `Layer.effectDiscard(initializeBootSchema...)` merged into `initialized`, consumed by `graph` at :99-107 and provided at :255. On failure index.ts:256-261 only logs and calls `fail(cause)`; `Ref.set(installed, ...)` at :248 never runs, so the handler stays the one created at :53-56, `publicRoute.pipe(Effect.map((response) => response ?? authErrorResponse("boot_unavailable")))`. publicRoute (proxy.ts:442-463) handles only /health, /.well-known/agent.json and `GET /_boot` help text. auth-http.ts:108 `boot_unavailable: { status: 503, hint: "Retry the same request; check bootloader logs if it persists." }`.

**Scenario.** A reload is interrupted by a crash, leaving a `cutover` row with phase='working'. Crashes are a common reason to upgrade, so the operator deploys the new image. initializeBootSchema refuses; the board answers 503 on /setup, /auth/login, /_boot/status and every API route, with a hint telling them to retry. The message that explains what to do ("Finish pending database recovery with the previous compatible image", boot-schema.ts:25) exists only in the process log, which for the QEMU/image deployment is the hardest surface to reach. app-store-identity.test.ts:284-303 asserts the refusal and that boot.db bytes are untouched, but nothing asserts that the reason is reachable over HTTP.

**Suggestion.** Surface the gate through the boot HTTP surface rather than the layer graph: run the journal check after `Ref.set(installed, ...)` (or fold it into the recovery phase as a `Failed` cause), so the human sees a 409-class refusal with the BootIdentityUpgradePending message, the journal phase, and the instruction to finish recovery on the previous image. If it must stay in the layer, at minimum let the pre-graph handler answer /_boot/status with the stored boot-schema refusal so the reason is readable without container logs.

**Skeptic.** The mechanism is confirmed: boot-schema.ts:39-45 raises BootIdentityUpgradePending inside initializeBootSchema, index.ts:59-75 wraps it in the `initialized` layer that `graph` (:99-107) depends on, and on failure index.ts:256-263 only logs and calls `fail(cause)` — `Ref.set(installed, ...)` at :248 never runs, so the handler stays index.ts:53-56 (publicRoute else authErrorResponse("boot_unavailable") = 503 with the retry hint at auth-http.ts:108), and publicRoute (proxy.ts:442-463) serves only /health, /.well-known/agent.json and GET /_boot. But the severity is wrong: this is the pre-existing surfacing convention for every boot-schema refusal, not something this PR introduced — boot-schema.ts:37 `BootSchemaTooNew` (a downgrade, i.e. the symmetric operator mistake) has behaved exactly this way since before the stack, and the documented way back in for both is the previous image, which is what the message says (boot-schema.ts:25, echoed in storage.md:98). The gate is also correctly placed ahead of the journal-mode and user_version writes, which the test at app-store-identity.test.ts:299-303 pins by byte equality. A 503 whose hint says "retry" for a permanent condition is a real presentation defect worth fixing, but it breaks no core guarantee and is not a design element claimed done that is not.


### B-5 [minor] Refusing to adopt a legacy store that is not a comms store surfaces as a raw SqlError, not a coded identity refusal

`packages/boot/src/app-recovery.ts:29` (write-safety)

**Claim.** The pre-adoption probe that checks a legacy store really carries boot's three tables raises an unclassified SqlError rather than app_store_missing/app_store_identity_invalid, and it is skipped entirely when the adoption was minted in `fresh` mode, so the two halves of the same guard behave differently for the same on-disk situation.

**Evidence.** app-recovery.ts:29-33 `if (adoption.mode === "legacy" && adoption.phase === "pending") { yield* sql`SELECT singleton,epoch FROM kernel_writer LIMIT 0`; yield* sql`SELECT id,from_seq,to_seq,count FROM mutation_batches LIMIT 0`; yield* sql`SELECT seq,transaction_id,event,shipped_at FROM outbox LIMIT 0`; }` — a missing table fails the whole transaction with a `no such table` SqlError whose `isRetryable` is false, so it is not in editFailure's expected set (edit-failure.ts:187) and not mapped by appIdentityPolicy. Mode is decided once at app-store-identity.ts:99 `mode: state.initialized || exists ? "legacy" : "fresh"` and never recomputed, so a store that appears after a `fresh` reservation is adopted through the `marker.length === 0` branch (:35-40) with no table check at all. Also at app-store-identity.ts:128 `SELECT type FROM sqlite_master WHERE name='store_identity'` does not filter `type='table'`.

**Scenario.** A boot store restored without its adoption record sits next to a leftover empty or non-comms `comms.db`. reserve mints a `legacy` adoption, the probe fails, and /_boot/status reports `SqlError: no such table: kernel_writer` as the recovery cause instead of a coded refusal carrying the "preserve both stores" hint — the refusal is correct, its presentation is not. The asymmetry costs more: the same empty file arriving after a `fresh` reservation is adopted silently, with store_identity created on it, which is the behaviour the probe was added to prevent.

**Suggestion.** Wrap the three probes in a mapError to `app_store_missing` (or a new `app_store_unrecognized`) so the refusal carries the identity policy, and run them whenever `phase === "pending"` and the store file already existed at reservation time, not only when `mode === "legacy"`. Add `AND type='table'` at app-store-identity.ts:128 so a non-table object of that name is an identity error rather than a SqlError.

**Skeptic.** Both halves confirmed at app-recovery.ts:29-34. The probe (`SELECT ... FROM kernel_writer/mutation_batches/outbox LIMIT 0`) raises a bare SqlError on a non-comms store, which is neither in edit-failure's expected set nor mapped by appIdentityPolicy, so the refusal surfaces as `no such table: kernel_writer` in the /_boot/status cause instead of a coded identity refusal — presentation only, the refusal itself is correct. The substantive half is the asymmetry the finder buried in the evidence: `mode` is frozen at app-store-identity.ts:99 and never recomputed, while app-recovery.ts:34 passes `allowMissing = adoption.phase === "pending"` for either mode, so a store that appears at the journaled path after a `fresh` reservation is adopted with no table probe at all — verifyAppIdentity takes the app-store-identity.ts:129-133 branch, CREATEs store_identity and stamps boot's UUID onto it, then app-recovery.ts:35-40 creates the kernel tables and fences it. That is reachable without exotic steps (a first startup whose child never fences successfully keeps the adoption fresh+pending across restarts; an operator then drops an old comms.db into DATA_DIR to migrate), and it is the exact failure §10.3 says identity exists to prevent. The `SELECT type FROM sqlite_master WHERE name='store_identity'` at :128 indeed lacks `AND type='table'`. Minor is the right weight overall, but the fresh-mode half, not the SqlError wrapping, is what should be fixed.


### A-3 [note] The legacy-adoption shape probes run ahead of the identity check and refuse with an untyped SqlError

`packages/boot/src/app-recovery.ts:29` (adoption-protocol)

**Claim.** `verifyAppIdentity` is no longer the first statement inside the app transaction. When the adoption is legacy-and-pending, three probes run first: `SELECT singleton,epoch FROM kernel_writer LIMIT 0`, `SELECT id,from_seq,to_seq,count FROM mutation_batches LIMIT 0`, `SELECT seq,transaction_id,event,shipped_at FROM outbox LIMIT 0` (:30-32). They are read-only, so the guarantee the ordering exists to protect is intact, and they are the right idea — they are the only thing standing between a non-comms SQLite file at the app-store path and an adoption that would write store_identity into it and then CREATE TABLE the kernel tables on top (:35-40). They are also correctly gated off for a fresh adoption, where a crash can legitimately leave a zero-table file. The cost is classification: their refusal is a raw SqlError, not an EventError, so `isAppStoreIdentityError` (app-store-identity.ts:146-149) is false and none of the 409 plumbing this PR adds at backup-http.ts:64, database-restore-http.ts:53 and edit-failure.ts:59-60/201 applies — the operator gets `handler_failed`/500 or the generic 503 body, or at startup a redacted SQL string in child.sourceError, instead of the preserve-both-stores hint that was written for exactly this situation. (The column list itself is safe: `shipped_at` has existed since the first commit, `git log -S "shipped_at INTEGER"` on app-recovery.ts returns only c720f75, and server/src/ext/core/schema.ts:25 already probes the same shape.)

**Evidence.** app-recovery.ts:29-33 (the three LIMIT 0 probes, preceding `yield* verifyAppIdentity(adoption, adoption.phase === "pending")` at :34); app-store-identity.ts:146-149 `isAppStoreIdentityError` matches only app_store_missing and app_store_identity_invalid; backup-http.ts:44-62 classifies anything else as `unexpected` → `handler_failed`, 500.

**Scenario.** A deployment's /data/comms.db is present but is not a comms app store — an operator staged a file there, or a restore performed outside comms truncated it — and the boot store has no `app_store_initialized` marker, so reserve classifies the adoption as legacy (app-store-identity.ts:99). The probe at app-recovery.ts:30 fails `no such table: kernel_writer`. Boot correctly refuses to adopt and stays reachable, but the refusal is reported as an unexpected handler failure rather than as an identity refusal carrying the 409 hint, so the operator is not told to preserve both stores and restore the matching board.

**Suggestion.** Wrap the probe block in a mapError to `new EventError({ code: "app_store_identity_invalid" })` — the code already exists and already routes everywhere (events.ts:34, event-http.ts:35/111, edit-failure.ts:60) — so a file that does not carry the boot-owned schema is refused in the same vocabulary as a malformed adoption record. Worth one sentence in design §10.3 too, since the probes are an addition the design does not describe.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. app-recovery.ts:29-33 runs three `SELECT … LIMIT 0` probes before `verifyAppIdentity` at :34 when the adoption is legacy-and-pending; they raise SqlError, `isAppStoreIdentityError` (app-store-identity.ts:146-149) matches only the two EventError codes, and backup-http.ts:44-62 classifies anything else (a non-retryable SqlError included) as `unexpected` → handler_failed/500, so the 409 preserve-both-stores hint does not apply. The ordering guarantee itself is intact: the probes are read-only and precede every mutation. Classification-only cost on a rare path, so note is right.


### A-4 [note] Adoption classifies a populated app store under a fresh boot store as `legacy` and adopts it with an empty seq allocator

`packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:99` (adoption-protocol)

**Claim.** `mode: state.initialized || exists ? "legacy" : "fresh"` treats two very different situations as one. The intended one is the upgrade: the marker is present, so the store was initialised by a previous image. The other is `exists` with no marker at all — boot has never initialised an app store, yet a populated one is sitting at the path — which can only mean the boot store was lost or replaced while the app store survived. In that case boot mints a fresh identity, writes store_identity into the populated store, creates/keeps the kernel tables and takes the fence (app-recovery.ts:34-41), while its own event log and allocator start from scratch: events.ts:79 `INSERT INTO seq VALUES (1,1,…)`. The probes added two lines above are `LIMIT 0`, so they check the shape of kernel_writer, mutation_batches and outbox and never look at their contents; nothing compares the app store's high-water mark against seq.next. This is pre-existing — the old code took the same branch on `marker.length === 0` — but `mode` plus the probe block is the first place it is cheaply detectable, and the design names the hazard ("hand out sequence numbers from seq.next that already name messages nobody can see").

**Evidence.** app-store-identity.ts:99; app-recovery.ts:29-33 (probes use LIMIT 0) and :35-41 (tables created, fence taken); events.ts:79 seq starts at next=1 in the boot store; app-recovery.ts:38 `outbox (seq INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, …)`.

**Scenario.** /data/boot.db is lost, or restored from a snapshot predating the app store, while /data/comms.db survives with messages up to seq N. Boot reserves a new UUID in legacy mode, adopts the populated store and takes the fence. Because published_through is 0 in the fresh boot store, the board serves an empty view of a full store; the first mutation then reserves seq 1 and its outbox insert hits the primary key, so writes fail with a constraint error rather than overwriting anything. No acknowledged write is lost, but the board is simultaneously wrong-looking and write-broken, and boot had the information to refuse.

**Suggestion.** In the legacy-and-pending branch, replace the LIMIT 0 outbox probe with `SELECT MAX(seq) FROM outbox` (and/or read kernel_writer) and refuse `app_store_identity_invalid` when the app store's high-water mark is at or above the boot store's seq.next. It costs one statement in a path that runs once per board and converts a silent mis-adoption into the same preserve-everything refusal the rest of this module produces.

**Skeptic.** The code claim is confirmed: app-store-identity.ts:99 `mode: state.initialized || exists ? "legacy" : "fresh"` conflates "marker present" with "populated file, no marker", the probes at app-recovery.ts:29-33 are LIMIT 0 and never look at contents, and events.ts:79 `INSERT INTO seq VALUES (1,1,0,…)` starts a fresh boot allocator at 1. The scenario is pre-existing (the base took the same `marker.length === 0` branch) and requires a lost/rolled-back boot store beside a surviving pre-identity app store; the specific "outbox primary-key collision" consequence is inferred from app-recovery.ts:38 rather than observed. Cheap detection point, no guarantee broken in this PR — note.


### A-5 [note] An app-authored migration can destroy store_identity, and the restore route cannot repair it because it fences the live store first

`packages/server/src/kernel/migrations.ts:45` (adoption-protocol)

**Claim.** Adding store_identity to kernelSqlTables (protected-sql-tables.ts:7) guards the mediated route only: sql-write.ts:19 blocks the name textually and :70-75 installs abort triggers, but migrations.ts:45-50 runs app-owned migration modules as arbitrary SQL inside the writer-gated transaction with no protected-table check at all. A migration that drops or rewrites store_identity therefore commits. The consequence is worse for this table than for the other three it joins, because the repair path is blocked: POST /_boot/db/restore calls `recovery.prepare(prior?.attempt.epoch ?? (yield* freshEpoch))` at database-restore.ts:324, which fences the live store through verifyAppIdentity with allowMissing false, before the safety clone at :333 and long before backup.restore at :140. A live store that has lost its identity therefore fails the restore request that would have replaced it. Same for cutover, which prepares at cutover.ts:86 before restoring at :91. This is the shape kernel_writer already had (dropping it makes the UPDATE at app-recovery.ts:41 fail the same way), so it is not a new class — but it is a new member, and the one whose symptom, `app_store_missing` against a file that is plainly present, is the most misleading.

**Evidence.** migrations.ts:45-50 `sql.withTransaction(writerGate(sql, epoch) then Migrator.make({})({ loader, table: "migrations" }))` with no protectedSqlTables call; protected-sql-tables.ts:7 and sql-write.ts:19/70-75 (mediated route only); database-restore.ts:324 `yield* recovery.prepare(prior?.attempt.epoch ?? (yield* freshEpoch))` preceding :333 and :140; app-store-identity.ts:130/143 raise app_store_missing from inside that prepare.

**Scenario.** An agent writes migrations/003_cleanup.ts containing `DROP TABLE store_identity` (or an UPDATE of store_id) and it commits at the next child start. Every subsequent recovery.prepare fails app_store_missing. The human opens /_boot, sees 409 app_store_missing with the hint to restore the matching board, and POSTs /_boot/db/restore naming a backup that does carry the right identity — and the request fails at database-restore.ts:324 before it takes a safety copy or touches a file. There is no in-band way back: repair means putting a store file in place from outside comms.

**Suggestion.** Either run the app's migration batch through the same protected-table guard the mediated route uses (protectedSqlTables + the abort triggers, which already cover INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and would need DDL coverage), or give the restore route a way through: when the live store fails identity verification, let the restore reach backup.restore after the existing assertClosure proof instead of requiring a successful prepare on a store it is about to replace. At minimum record the asymmetry in design §10.3 beside the app_store_missing paragraph, since 'never creates an empty replacement board' currently also means 'never repairs one'.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed on both halves. migrations.ts:45-50 runs app-authored migration modules as arbitrary SQL under `sql.withTransaction(writerGate(...))` with no `protectedSqlTables` call, so the guard at sql-write.ts:19 and the BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE triggers at :70-75 (which cannot stop DDL anyway) do not cover it; database-restore.ts:324 `yield* recovery.prepare(...)` runs before the safety clone at :333 and before `backup.restore(target)` at :140, and cutover.ts prepares before restoring, so a live store that has lost `store_identity` fails the very request that would replace it. Not a new class (kernel_writer has the same shape) and requires a hostile/careless app migration, so note is right.


### B-4 [note] f4f1dca changes supervisor status semantics in an identity PR without disclosing it, and the downgraded state keeps error:null

`packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:88` (write-safety)

**Claim.** The PR's head commit changes production behaviour in the supervisor — `withdraw` now rewrites a `live` ChildStatus to `starting` with pid and port cleared — and the PR body does not mention it. The change itself is an improvement (it stops /_boot/status reporting a retired child as live with a dead pid), but it is unrelated to board identity and its residual is that the downgraded record keeps `error: null`, so an abandoned board reads as a board that is starting.

**Evidence.** supervisor.ts:88-95 `const withdraw = Ref.set(routing.route, null).pipe(Effect.andThen(Ref.set(current, null)), Effect.andThen(Ref.update(status, (value): ChildStatus => value.state === "live" ? { ...value, state: "starting", pid: null, port: null } : value)))`; git show f4f1dca confirms the previous definition was the one-line `Ref.set(routing.route, null).pipe(Effect.andThen(Ref.set(current, null)))`. The value is diagnostic only — proxy.ts:239-254 (/_boot/status) and :286-288 (the 503 body); routing reads child.traffic.route. The PR body lists the staging change, the upgrade gate and the SQL guard but nothing about supervisor status. The companion commit c390131 is test-only (restart-sql-failure.test.ts evidence capture).

**Scenario.** A cutover fails past acceptance, so cutover.ts:384 calls `supervisor.withdraw` and the subsequent `start(selected)` also fails. /_boot/status now reports `{state: "starting", pid: null, port: null, error: null}`. A human or monitor polling for `state === "failed"` sees a board that looks like it is mid-start; the real cause arrives only when the recovery loop next writes the status (supervisor.ts:311-332 or :372-377). Previously the same moment reported `state: "live"` with a dead pid, which was worse — so this is a net improvement whose remaining ambiguity is worth one more line.

**Suggestion.** Say in the PR body that supervisor status invalidation is included and why (it was found by the new evidence capture), or split it into its own commit on the base. While touching it, carry the withdrawal reason into the record rather than leaving `error: null` — e.g. set error to the withdrawing operation's code — so a withdrawn-but-not-yet-recovered board is distinguishable from a starting one.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed: `git show f4f1dca` replaces the one-line `withdraw` with supervisor.ts:88-95, which downgrades a `live` ChildStatus to `starting` with pid and port cleared, and the PR body does not mention it. I verified the value is diagnostic only — the sole readers are proxy.ts:239 (/_boot/status) and the 503 body path; routing reads child.traffic.route — and that supervisor.ts:107-113 `fail` later overwrites the record with state "failed" plus the redacted cause, so the ambiguous window is the interval between withdraw and the next status write, not a durable lie. Since the change strictly removes a false `live` pid, the residual `error: null` is cosmetic and the only genuine ask is one line of disclosure in the body; that is a note, not a minor defect.


### B-6 [note] Identity proves which board, not which version of it: a stale self-copy passes every check

`packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts:143` (write-safety)

**Claim.** verifyAppIdentity compares only `store_id`, and every clone of a board (backup artifacts, rehearsal clones) carries the same UUID. So the check catches a foreign or empty store but not an older copy of this same store, which is the case where the boot event log retains events whose app-side rows no longer exist. Not a regression — the pre-PR guard was `fs.exists` — but worth stating in the design rather than leaving implied.

**Evidence.** app-store-identity.ts:143 `if (row.store_id !== adoption.store_id) return yield* new EventError({ code: "app_store_missing" });` is the only currency-relevant comparison. Backup artifacts are `VACUUM INTO` copies (app-backup.ts:41) and therefore carry the identical store_identity row; the boot store holds no per-prepare high-water mark to compare against (fenceAppStore writes a fresh epoch at app-recovery.ts:41 and never reads the previous one). The PR body admits the neighbouring limitation ("Legacy adoption cannot retrospectively prove that preidentity data was never substituted").

**Scenario.** An operator rolls the volume back with a filesystem snapshot that captured `boot.db` and `comms.db` at different times, or copies an old `VACUUM INTO` artifact over the live store by hand instead of using the restore route. reserve and verifyAppIdentity both pass, the fence moves, and boot resumes with `seq.next` ahead of the app store's contents: /api/stream serves events for messages the app store no longer holds, and no code path reports a mismatch. The design's §10.3 wording ("the environment or the journal names a database that is not this board, including an empty one") reads as if identity closed this, and it does not.

**Suggestion.** Either state the limit explicitly in §10.3 and storage.md (identity proves board, not currency; a stale self-copy is indistinguishable and must go through the restore route, which records restored_to_seq), or close it cheaply: stamp a monotonically increasing fence counter into store_identity in the same app transaction that moves the epoch, mirror it in boot `settings`, and refuse a store whose counter is behind — with the restore path setting both sides to the artifact's published_through.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed as a correctly-scoped note. app-store-identity.ts:143 compares only `store_id`; backup artifacts are VACUUM INTO copies (app-backup.ts:41) and so carry the identical store_identity row; fenceAppStore writes a fresh epoch (app-recovery.ts:41) and never reads the previous one; and grep for published_through shows no boot-side high-water mark is ever compared against the app store's contents. So a stale self-copy passes. Not a regression (the pre-PR guard was fs.exists at app-recovery.ts:19-21), and the PR body admits the neighbouring legacy limitation, so this is documentation work on §10.3 and storage.md rather than a code defect.


### Item status


#### adoption-protocol

Dimension: the identity adoption protocol and restore, at f4f1dca in /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-cryogenicplanet-general-comms/f370fb0c-0b48-4731-a18c-45a3703a3b43/scratchpad/wt-pr3 (note: the worktree is at scratchpad/wt-pr3, not scratchpad/stack/wt-pr3).

THE TWO PREVIOUS BLOCKING ASKS ARE BOTH FIXED, and I verified them in code rather than taking the body's word.

(a) The staging leak is gone. Current lifecycle: one fixed path, `${appStoreFilename}.restore-staging` (app-backup.ts:59). Each `restore` removes it recursively with `force` (:60), recreates it (:61), registers a scope finalizer that removes it again (:62, `Effect.orDie`), copies the artefact to `<dir>/store.db` (:63-65), verifies identity on that copy inside its own transaction (:66-72), fsyncs the copy (:74), removes the live `-wal`/`-shm` (:76), renames onto the live store (:77) and fsyncs the store's parent (:78). Three things reclaim it: the scope finalizer on every exit including failure and interrupt; the same-path removal at the start of the next restore; and `AppBackup.recoverStaging` (app-backup.ts:33) called at index.ts:151 on every recovery cycle, inside `supervisor.operationGate` after `supervisor.recoverClosure` and `release` — i.e. after positive owner closure, as boot/docs/storage.md:100 claims. A SIGKILL therefore leaves at most one directory holding at most one board copy, and the next start removes it. Pinned by app-store-identity.test.ts:262-282 (two consecutive killed attempts, then sweep, then a successful restore). The unbounded, in-band-unreclaimable leak the previous review blocked on no longer exists. One residue remains (A-2): nothing reclaims the *old* implementation's `${filename}.restore` file, and the test at :132-137 deliberately leaves it in place.

(b) The backup stamp moved into the finalizing transaction with the `IS NULL` guard: app-store-identity.ts:114-115, `if (saved.phase === "pending" && saved.mode === "legacy") UPDATE backups SET legacy_store_id=… WHERE legacy_store_id IS NULL`, inside the same `boot.withTransaction` as the id, the marker and the ready flip (:116-118). This is load-bearing, not cosmetic: a boot store rolled back to a pre-adoption snapshot now mints a new UUID, hits the surviving `store_identity` row with the old id at :143, fails `app_store_missing`, and never reaches the stamp — so the catalogue is not re-provenanced against an identity that was never adopted. app-store-identity.test.ts:219-239 pins both halves (`prior-board` preserved, a later backup left null).

(c) The two unexplained Linux failures are still unexplained. The body says "Exact-head Linux/image/QEMU checks are pending" and does not mention them.

KILL-POINT ANALYSIS (mine, not the previous review's). Adoption: before `reserve` commits → nothing durable, fresh mint next start. After `reserve` → `app_store_adoption` pending; next start resumes the same UUID (:91-93), and `read`'s cross-check (:76) makes "pending with an `app_store_id`" and "ready without one" both `app_store_identity_invalid` rather than ambiguous. After the app transaction commits `store_identity`, before `complete` → next start resumes the same UUID and `verifyAppIdentity` finds the matching row (app-store-identity.test.ts:87-103 "stamp" mode, a real SIGKILL). After the boot marker, before the app transaction → unreachable by construction: `complete` runs at app-recovery.ts:94, strictly after the store and its parent directory are fsynced at :92-93. After `complete` → normal. Restore: before `copyFile` → nothing; after `copyFile`, after the staging-copy identity transaction, or after the `-wal`/`-shm` removal → the live file is still the pre-restore store and the journal drives a redo, the staging directory swept on the next start; after `fs.rename` but before the parent fsync → either the rename is visible (done) or it is not (journal redoes it), both safe. No state in either sequence needs out-of-band repair that did not already need it before this PR.

EVERY SPECIFIC CLAIM I WAS ASKED TO CHECK HOLDS, with one qualification. The identity check is ahead of the recovery-table creation (app-recovery.ts:35-40) and the fence update (:41), and ahead of every mutation in the transaction — but it is no longer literally the *first* statement: three non-mutating `SELECT … LIMIT 0` shape probes precede it at :29-33 when the adoption is legacy-and-pending (A-3). Those probes are an improvement — they are what stops boot from writing `store_identity` into an arbitrary SQLite file and then creating kernel tables on top of it — but their refusal is an untyped `SqlError` outside the `EventError` vocabulary this PR extends everywhere else. `allowMissing` is `adoption.phase === "pending"` at :34 and, on the restore path, `backup.legacy_store_id === adoption.store_id` at app-backup.ts:70, which cannot both be true because the stamp is only written by `complete`. A foreign identity aborts inside the app transaction before the fence, and `events.append`/`events.abort` (app-recovery.ts:125-126) run only after `fenceAppStore` returns, so no reserved range is resolved or discarded (test:105-117 asserts the epoch is unchanged). A legacy store is adopted once: `reserve` early-returns an existing adoption and refuses one naming a different filename (:92), and nothing in src ever deletes `app_store_adoption`. Two boot processes cannot both adopt: the bare `INSERT INTO settings` at :102 runs inside `boot.withTransaction` against `settings(key TEXT PRIMARY KEY)` (boot-schema.ts:62), so the loser gets a conflict rather than a second UUID. The restore verifies identity on the disposable copy before the `-wal` removal and the rename, and refuses a foreign artefact with a typed `EventError` — but the error does not name the mismatch and reuses `app_store_missing` (A-1).

TWO UNDOCUMENTED IMPROVEMENTS BEYOND WHAT THE DESIGN ASKS. First, `BootIdentityUpgradePending` (boot-schema.ts:20-27, 39-46): the rung-17 migration refuses before any PRAGMA or schema change when a cutover or restore journal is in flight, because a first upgrade cannot authorize an identity-free artefact before adoption completes. It is correctly version-gated (`cutover.phase` exists from rung 9, `db_restore_requests` from 13) and leaves the board reachable — the layer failure is caught at index.ts:256-262 and `/_boot` keeps serving, the same shape generations.test.ts:273-288 pins for `BootSchemaTooNew`. Tested byte-for-byte at app-store-identity.test.ts:284-303. Second, the deterministic staging path is also a symlink fix: the old `fs.copyFile(backup, `${filename}.restore`)` would follow a planted symlink, while the new code removes the path before creating the directory. Both deserve a line in the design doc (§10.3 already carries the two-phase protocol and the `legacy_store_id` correction, so §17's table is current on those).

No finding in this dimension touches a core guarantee. The core-guarantee questions I chased and cleared: the staging copy's identity write survives the rename (proven by test:119-143, which restores a legacy copy and then starts with `allowMissing` false); the `DELETE FROM public_paths` that precedes every `backup.restore` self-heals because `reconstructPublicPages` is keyed on the child epoch (public-page-policy.ts:36) and republishes the full grant set at each admission; and the `recoverStaging` sweep cannot race an in-flight restore because both hold `supervisor.operationGate`.

#### write-safety

From the acknowledged-writes/fencing angle the two-phase adoption protocol itself holds up: `reserve` commits the UUID in its own boot transaction, `verifyAppIdentity` is the first statement inside the app transaction and precedes the `CREATE TABLE`/fence update (app-recovery.ts:34 before :36-41), the pending-batch evidence is read in that same transaction and resolved only after it commits, and `identity.complete` runs after an explicit fsync of the store and its parent. I could not construct a SIGKILL interleaving, a cutover, a restart or a restore that loses or double-resolves an acknowledged write, nor a comms-internal path that stamps or authorizes a foreign backup. Both previous asks that were in scope are genuinely fixed: the `.restore-*` staging leak is now one deterministic `<store>.restore-staging` directory, removed before use, scope-finalized, and swept at startup after closure proof (app-backup.ts:59-62, index.ts:151); the legacy backup stamp moved into the finalizing transaction with an `IS NULL` guard (app-store-identity.ts:114-115). Rung 17 migrates forward atomically, refuses 18+, and does not collide with master (16). The new error surface is correct and leak-free: 409/400 non-retriable everywhere, no paths, UUIDs or credentials in any body or event. The protected-sql-tables line belongs here. What does not hold up is the *selection* half of the adoption record. The record pins the realpath-resolved absolute filename of the app store and `reserve` refuses forever when it changes (app-store-identity.ts:92), which turns the documented operations of moving `DATA_DIR` (README:14 uses `$PWD/data`) and the supported non-isolated-to-isolated layout migration (app-store-layout.ts:92) into a permanent `app_store_identity_invalid`. That refusal is amplified by where `reserveIdentity` sits: inside index.ts's `owners` block, so any identity refusal skips `coordinator.recover` and `restore.recover`, leaves both `ready` refs false, and makes every in-band repair route (reload, revert, reset, database restore) answer `*_recovery_required` — including the restore the policy hint tells the operator to perform. A third, smaller way-in problem: the new `BootIdentityUpgradePending` gate fails the boot layer, so its reason reaches no HTTP route and the operator gets a retriable 503 that never succeeds. c390131 is test-only; f4f1dca is a genuine but undisclosed supervisor fix.

### Skeptic corrections


**adoption-protocol.** I agree with every item_status row's verdict; two need a wording correction, and one needs a caveat.

1. Row "§10.3: allowMissing true only while an adoption is pending — fixed". The code is as described (app-recovery.ts:34 `verifyAppIdentity(adoption, adoption.phase === "pending")`; app-backup.ts:70 `verifyAppIdentity(adoption, backup.legacy_store_id === adoption.store_id)`), and the finder's non-overlap argument holds — while pending, every catalogue row's legacy_store_id is still NULL because the stamp is only written by complete (app-store-identity.ts:114-115). But the bullet as stated is not what the code does: on the restore path allowMissing is true precisely when the adoption is *ready* (a stamped legacy artefact). That is deliberate and the design already carves it out (database-design.md:941, "A restore may create store_identity on its private staging copy only when that column equals the live identity"), so the status should read "fixed, with the design's legacy-artefact exception" rather than "only while pending".

2. Row "(a) previous staging-leak ask — fixed". I verified the whole lifecycle (app-backup.ts:59-62, :33; index.ts:145-151, the sweep inside the same `withPermit` block as `recoverClosure`/`release`, and sequenced before `restore.recover` at index.ts:159-160, so the sweep cannot race a resumed restore). One thing the row does not mention: `restore` still takes a full board copy at app-backup.ts:65 with no `headroom.check` on the staging path (contrast `clone` at :38). Pre-existing — 1ef641e had the same unchecked copyFile — so not a PR defect, but it is why A-2's stranded copy has a concrete cost.

3. Row "Two boot processes cannot both adopt — fixed". Accurate as far as it goes: app-store-identity.ts:102 is a bare `INSERT INTO settings(key,value) VALUES('app_store_adoption',…)` with no ON CONFLICT inside `boot.withTransaction`, against `settings(key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, …)` (boot-schema.ts:62), so a second reserver loses on the write lock or the primary key. Worth noting the transaction is SQLite-deferred, so the loser's failure is SQLITE_BUSY/constraint, i.e. the same untyped-SqlError classification A-3 describes — it cannot mint a second UUID, but it does not surface as an identity refusal either.

4. Row "(c) two unexplained Linux failures — unchanged". Confirmed against the PR body; honest-but-silent is the right characterisation.

All other rows I spot-checked in the worktree and they hold: the store_identity shape and validation (app-store-identity.ts:131, :135-143), the two-phase reserve/complete wiring (:81-105, :109-120; app-recovery.ts:17, :92-94), the ready/mirror cross-check (:76), the once-only legacy upgrade (:91-93, :114-118, and no src path deletes `app_store_adoption` — grep over packages/boot/src and packages/server/src returns only the five reads/writes in app-store-identity.ts), the pre-fence refusal, the restore-before-replace ordering (app-backup.ts:64-78), `store_identity` in kernelSqlTables (protected-sql-tables.ts:7), the BootIdentityUpgradePending gate (boot-schema.ts:20-27, 38-46, correctly ahead of the PRAGMAs at :47-50 and version-gated 9/13), and the app_store_database deferral (no occurrence anywhere in packages). Also confirmed the staging identity write cannot be lost in a WAL sidecar the rename leaves behind: storage/src/client.ts:12 opens with `disableWAL: true` and the inner scope closes before the fsync at app-backup.ts:74.


**write-safety.** I agree with the substance of every row; four need correction or sharpening.

1. "Secret or path leakage in new error bodies and events" — overstated as written. protected-sql-tables.ts:7 is consumed only by the write guard (sql-write.ts:11,19,61-66: statement-text refusal plus temp ABORT triggers, and sql-write.ts:66 filters to tables that actually exist, so the new entry cannot brick a child whose store predates the table). There is no equivalent guard on the read path — grep for kernelSqlTables/protectedSqlTables across packages/server/src returns only sql-write.ts and protected-sql-tables.ts itself — so any agent with SQL read can `SELECT * FROM store_identity` and read the board UUID. Error bodies and events are clean as claimed; "no UUID anywhere" is not.

2. "BootIdentityUpgradePending upgrade gate" — the predicate judgment is right but the citations are wrong. The backup.restore call in the cutover path is cutover.ts:91 (`yield* backup.restore({ path: saved, legacy_store_id: rows[0]?.legacy_store_id ?? null })`), reached from cutover.ts:108 (`if (record.phase !== "accepted") yield* restore(record)`), not cutover.ts:113; database-restore.ts:126 and :140 are correct. I also confirmed the gate's `version >= 9` floor is exactly right: the cutover table is created at boot-schema.ts:96-99 (version < 9) and db_restore_requests at version < 13, so no pre-9 store can hold a pending journal of either kind.

3. "§10.3 two-phase adoption" — fixed is right, and the ordering is stronger than the row credits. database-restore.ts:324 runs `recovery.prepare` before the safety clone at :333 (`backup.clone(filename)`), so adoption is always finalized and store_identity is always present in the live store before a pre-restore safety artifact is taken. That is what keeps the rollback path (database-restore.ts:126 → app-backup.ts:70) authorizable, and it closes the otherwise-fatal corner where a pending-adoption board could take an identity-free safety copy and then be unable to roll back to it. Worth recording as a deliberate improvement rather than leaving implied.

4. "Explain the two unexplained Linux failures" — agreed that this is unchanged/disclosed. The cited test is packages/server/test/backup-inventory.test.ts:150-166 (the row omits the package); I confirmed the diff is exactly the behaviour change from B-2, not a timing tweak.

Everything else I checked independently and agree with: rung 17 migrates forward inside one transaction (boot-schema.ts:137-138) and master ships 16 (`git show dd733fe:packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts` → `user_version = 16`, `supported: 16`); the legacy stamp is inside complete's boot transaction with an IS NULL guard and is skipped once phase is ready (app-store-identity.ts:114-118); the staging leak is genuinely fixed for the steady state (app-backup.ts:33,59-62 plus index.ts:151 after recoverClosure at :144), with the test at app-store-identity.test.ts:262-282 killing two restores and showing one directory; restore authorizes on a disposable copy and `fs.rename` publishes it (app-backup.ts:63-78), and the staging copy is opened with disableWAL (packages/storage/src/client.ts:12) so no committed identity write can be stranded in a sidecar left behind by the rename; `fail` is non-fatal (supervisor.ts:107-113), so an identity refusal is reported rather than crash-looping.


### What the skeptics found that the finders missed


**adoption-protocol.** A durable adoption record is never cleared or re-authorized, so any identity divergence that is not a crash becomes a permanent refusal with no in-band way out — and this is a behaviour change from the base, not just a new refusal. `reserve` early-returns the stored adoption (app-store-identity.ts:91-93), nothing in packages/boot/src or packages/server/src ever deletes `settings.app_store_adoption` (grep: only the five sites in app-store-identity.ts), and `transferred_to` is declared at :131 and validated at :141 but is never written or honoured as an authorization anywhere.

Concretely: restore the boot store from a snapshot taken before adoption (or lose it and keep /data), with the app store intact. `read` finds no adoption, so `reserve` mints a *new* UUID with `mode: "legacy"` (marker present) and `phase: "pending"` and commits it at :102. `verifyAppIdentity` then finds the surviving `store_identity` row carrying the old id and fails at :143 `app_store_missing`, so `complete` at app-recovery.ts:94 is never reached and the wrong-UUID pending adoption is now durable. Every subsequent start takes the same path forever. The restore route cannot help either: every catalogued artefact was stamped with the *old* id by the earlier `complete` (:115), so `backup.legacy_store_id === adoption.store_id` is false at app-backup.ts:70, `allowMissing` is false, and each artefact's own `store_identity` row carries the old id — refused at :143. On the base (1ef641e), the same rolled-back boot store simply re-fenced the existing app store on `marker.length > 0` and the board ran.

Refusing is arguably the right call — a rolled-back boot store also rolls back `seq`, which is the hazard the section exists for — but the design explicitly frames the two-phase protocol as the fix for "the surviving half names an identity the other half will never agree with, and every subsequent start fails `app_store_missing` with both stores intact" (database-design.md:933), and that exact state is still reachable here, now made permanent by the durable reservation. Nothing in this PR gives a human a lever: no operator-authorized re-adoption, no clear of the pending record, no use of `transferred_to`, and (per A-1) nothing served that even names the two ids being compared. The cheap fix is to not persist a fresh reservation when the app store already carries an identity the new UUID contradicts — refuse first, mint nothing — so the board's next start is diagnosable rather than wedged by boot's own write; and §10.3 should say how an operator legitimately re-authorizes an existing app store under a new boot store.


**write-safety.** The staging-directory fix does not survive the layout migration, so the previous review's ask (a) is closed only in the steady state. `recoverStaging` and `restore` both derive the directory from the *current* store filename — app-backup.ts:33 `recoverStaging: fs.remove(`${filename}.restore-staging`, { recursive: true, force: true })` and :59 `const directory = `${filename}.restore-staging``— and the layer is built with the layout-dependent path (index.ts:105 `backupLayer(appFilename)`, with appFilename chosen at index.ts:50 by COMMS_ISOLATED). A restore killed on the non-isolated layout leaves `<root>/comms.db.restore-staging/store.db`, a full-board-sized copy (exactly what app-store-identity.test.ts:275-276 asserts exists after `app.crash("restore")`). If the operator then moves to the image layout, index.ts:150 `migrateAppStore` renames the store to `<root>/store/comms.db` — app-store-layout.ts only inspects `legacy`, `filename` and their `-wal/-shm/-journal` sidecars (app-store-layout.ts:53-63), never the staging directory — and from then on index.ts:151 sweeps `<root>/store/comms.db.restore-staging` only. The pre-migration copy is orphaned permanently: no other sweep touches it (retention prunes only `backups/` and generation snapshots), and because it is a stale clone of the board's database it is also the worst thing to leave lying around next to the live store, given B-6 (a stale self-copy passes every identity check if anyone ever drops it back over `comms.db`). The cheap fix is to sweep both candidate paths in `recoverStaging` (or remove `<dataDirectory>/comms.db.restore-staging` inside `migrateAppStore`, which already owns the pre-migration layout and already has positive closure evidence), and to note in boot/docs/storage.md:100 that the staging path follows the store.


### Claims check

Claims-check complete. Everything ran clean; working tree unmodified.
