### A-2 [major] Most of the step-2 `DbOps` surface is absent, and `readOnlySession`, `capacity`, `tableExists`, `dropClone`, `reapClones` and the rehearsal-copy budget are deferred silently

`packages/boot/src/db-ops.ts:33` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** The PR is the stack's step-2 vehicle but delivers only a rename plus descriptor threading; the operations design 5.1/5.2 names and design step 2 explicitly lists as "Changes" are not present, and the PR body's deferral list does not mention them.

**Evidence.** `db-ops.ts:33-83` returns exactly four members — `recoverStaging`, `estimatedBytes`, `clone`, `prepareClone`, `restoreInto` — and `:85` tags that object as the service. Repo-wide grep over `packages` and `scripts` returns **zero** occurrences of each of: `cloneForRehearsal`, `dropClone`, `reapClones`, `readOnlySession`, `tableExists`, `DbOpsError`, `dialect`, `REHEARSAL_COPY_BUDGET`, `SCRATCH_DATABASE_LIMIT`, `rehearsal_copy_timeout`, `scratch_limit`, `clone_load_failed`, `app_store_database`. There is no `backup` method and no `capacity` member. Design step 2's change list reads: "Add `capacity` delegating to `storage-volume.ts`, `tableExists`, `readOnlySession`, `dropClone` and `reapClones` ... Add the rehearsal-copy budget (section 5.3) and the success-path `dropClone` (section 5.4)." The PR body's deferral sentence is "Remote engines, provisioning, native restore and cross-engine transfer remain later stack work" — none of these names appear. Measured delta +117 −74 against step 2's stated +280 −80.

**Scenario.** Each omission becomes a step-5 blocker instead of an inert SQLite change, and one of them is load-bearing today. `readOnlySession` is the mechanism design 11 requires for `POST /api/sql`; `packages/server/src/kernel/sql-read.ts` is untouched in this delta, so the route still builds a second `readonly: true` SQLite connection directly and the seam the design puts on `DbOps` does not exist — meaning the first Postgres deployment has an unenforced read-only route until that file is rewritten under time pressure. Without the `REHEARSAL_COPY_BUDGET` of design 5.3, `database-backup.ts:109`'s single `Effect.timeout("10 seconds")` around the whole capture and the cutover's total absence of a clone budget (`cutover.ts:219`) mean a slow dump on a remote engine is reported to the editing agent as a self-test failure, sending it to debug code that is fine — the precise confusion 5.3 exists to prevent. Without `dropClone` on the success path (5.4) and `reapClones` (10.5), a remote deployment accumulates one copy of the board per edit with nothing to reap them.

**Suggestion.** Either land the named members now with SQLite bodies (`capacity` delegating to `readStorageVolume`, `tableExists` as the `sqlite_master` probe, `readOnlySession` as the existing second read-only connection, `dropClone` as `fs.remove`, `reapClones` as a stale-`*.rehearsal.db` sweep, and the two budgets) — each is a few lines and all are inert on SQLite — or state them explicitly in the PR body as deferred to a named later PR, so the step-2 gap is a recorded status rather than an omission a reader has to grep for.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. db-ops.ts:33-83 exposes only recoverStaging, estimatedBytes, clone, prepareClone, restoreInto, and repo-wide greps return zero for cloneForRehearsal, dropClone, reapClones, readOnlySession, tableExists, DbOpsError, dialect, REHEARSAL_COPY_BUDGET, rehearsal_copy_timeout, scratch_limit, clone_load_failed, app_store_database. design step 2 (database-design.md:1079) explicitly lists 'Add `capacity` ... `tableExists`, `readOnlySession`, `dropClone` and `reapClones`', 'returning a descriptor, journaling the name in the same boot transaction', 'the rehearsal-copy budget (5.3) and the success-path `dropClone` (5.4)'; its acceptance at :1085 ('restoreInto returns the same descriptor it was given, the journal names comms.db before and after') is unachievable because restoreInto returns void (db-ops.ts:55-82) and writes no settings row. pr4-body.md's deferral sentence names only 'Remote engines, provisioning, native restore and cross-engine transfer'. Major stands: a step the stack presents as step 2 has its own acceptance criteria unmet and the gap is undeclared.


### B-1 [major] Schema version bumped to 18 without extending the in-flight-recovery refusal, so a v17 store mid-restore is migrated and locked out of its rollback image

`packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:39` (scope-and-regressions)

**Claim.** PR #3 introduced the BootIdentityUpgradePending refusal with its bound equal to the then-new target version (`if (version >= 9 && version < 17)` at 0658fe7, target 17). This PR raises the target to 18 (`if (version > 18) ... supported: 18` at :37, `if (version === 18) return` at :51, `PRAGMA user_version = 18` at :140) but leaves the refusal bound at `< 17`. A store already at 17 therefore escapes the refusal entirely and is migrated to 18 with an in-flight cutover or restore outstanding.

**Evidence.** boot-schema.ts:37-46 at HEAD: `if (version > 18) return yield* new BootSchemaTooNew({ found: version, supported: 18 });` then `// Refuse before schema or journal-mode changes so the previous image can finish recovery.` / `if (version >= 9 && version < 17) { const cutovers = yield* sql`SELECT singleton FROM cutover WHERE phase!='accepted' LIMIT 1`; ... if (cutovers.length > 0 || restores.length > 0) return yield* new BootIdentityUpgradePending({}); }` and boot-schema.ts:138-140 `if (version < 18) yield* sql`ALTER TABLE backups ADD COLUMN engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'sqlite' ...`` / `yield* sql`PRAGMA user_version = 18``. Verified against git: `git show 0658fe7:packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts` has `version > 17 ... supported: 17`, `version >= 9 && version < 17`, `if (version === 17) return` — the bound matched the target there; `git show dd733fe:...` (merged base, target 16) has no such guard at all, confirming PR #3 added it for exactly this purpose. BootIdentityUpgradePending's own message (boot-schema.ts:25) is `"Finish pending database recovery with the previous compatible image before upgrading board identity. Boot schema and recovery journals were preserved."`

**Scenario.** Operator runs the PR #3 image; boot.db is at user_version=17. A database restore is interrupted mid-flight (db_restore_requests.phase='restoring') or a cutover is left at phase!='accepted', and the container restarts. The operator deploys the PR #4 image. Line 39's guard does not fire (version is 17, not < 17), so the ALTER TABLE runs and user_version becomes 18. The PR #4 image now owns the recovery. If its restore path is the thing that is broken — the exact reason ask (b) asked for QEMU recovery acceptance on this rename — rolling back to the PR #3 image no longer works: that image raises BootSchemaTooNew{found:18,supported:17} inside `initialized`, the whole service graph fails, and index.ts:53-56 leaves the fallback handler in place so every /_boot/* route answers 503 boot_unavailable. The in-flight recovery can only be finished by the image the operator is trying to leave.

**Suggestion.** Change line 39 to `if (version >= 9 && version < 18)` so the refusal tracks the target version, and add a test that a v17 store with `cutover.phase='working'` (and one with `db_restore_requests.phase='restoring'`) refuses with BootIdentityUpgradePending rather than stamping 18. Better still, derive the bound from a single `const supported = 18` so a future rung cannot forget it.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in code. boot-schema.ts:37 `if (version > 18) ... supported: 18`, :39 `if (version >= 9 && version < 17)`, :138-140 `if (version < 18) ALTER TABLE backups ADD COLUMN engine ...` / `PRAGMA user_version = 18`; `git show 0658fe7:packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts` has `version > 17 ... supported: 17` with the same bound `version < 17`, so the bound did equal the target one commit earlier, and the comment above it (:38) is generic: 'Refuse before schema or journal-mode changes so the previous image can finish recovery.' Two corrections to the framing. (1) It is not a blocker and no core guarantee breaks: the 18 migration is additive (one ALTER with a DEFAULT) and leaves cutover/db_restore_requests journals intact, so the PR #4 image can finish the in-flight recovery and /_boot stays reachable; what is lost is the option to roll back to the PR #3 image in that window. (2) PR #3's own framing scoped the refusal to pre-identity stores ('Pending preidentity database recovery refuses before WAL or schema changes', pr3-body.md:3) and the error class is BootIdentityUpgradePending, so 'version < 17' is at least arguably intentional; the generic comment and the one-character fix still make tracking the target the right call. Kept at major on the strength of the comment and the cheapness of the fix, not because it blocks.


### A-1 [minor] `backups.engine` is still never written by any production insert; the previous review's ask is unfixed while the body claims it is done

`packages/boot/src/cutover.ts:289` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** All three production `INSERT INTO backups` statements still omit the `engine` column and depend entirely on the schema default, so the provenance value is never asserted at the one place it can be wrong — exactly the change the previous review asked for while it is provably inert.

**Evidence.** `cutover.ts:289`: `yield* sql`INSERT INTO backups(id,path,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation) VALUES(${id},${saved},'pre-flip',${bytes},${frozenAt},${fence},${generation.n})``. `database-backup.ts:92-93`: `INSERT INTO backups(id,path,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation)\n VALUES(${id},${saved},${options.reason},${bytes},${taken},${published},${active.generation.n})`. `database-restore.ts:336-337`: the same column list with `'pre-restore'`. The value comes only from `boot-schema.ts:139`: `ALTER TABLE backups ADD COLUMN engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'sqlite' CHECK(engine IN ('sqlite','pg','mysql'))`. There is no `DbOps.dialect` to stamp from (zero occurrences of `dialect` in `packages/*/src`), which is what design 10.2 specifies: "stamped at capture from `DbOps.dialect`". The PR body's first sentence says the change "record[s] each artifact's engine".

**Scenario.** Step 5 adds the Postgres `DbOps`. `pg_dump` writes `backups/<id>.dump`, and any of these three inserts that is carried forward unchanged — or any new capture path that copies the existing column list — produces a row with `engine='sqlite'` for a Postgres dump. Every guard that exists to catch this then passes, because they all compare the stored column to the literal `"sqlite"`: `database-restore-auth.ts:45`, `database-restore.ts:56`, `cutover.ts:74` and `db-ops.ts:58`. The restore proceeds to `fs.copyFile(backup.path, temporary)` (`db-ops.ts:67`) and then opens the dump as SQLite at `:70-73`, so the human gets an opaque decode/SqlError instead of the 409 `backup_engine_mismatch` with its hint, and the live store is saved only by the accident that the rename at `:79` comes after the identity check. Worse, the drill in design 10.4 step 1 picks "the newest artefact whose engine matches" and will pick that row, and `app-store-identity.ts:129`'s `AND engine='sqlite'` would stamp a legacy identity onto it.

**Suggestion.** Add a `dialect: "sqlite"` field to the `DbOps` service object in `db-ops.ts` and name the column in all three inserts from it: `INSERT INTO backups(id,engine,path,...) VALUES(${id},${dbOps.dialect},${saved},...)`. On SQLite this is provably inert (the default already produces the same value), which is why it should land now rather than with the second engine.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in code. cutover.ts:289 `INSERT INTO backups(id,path,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation) VALUES(${id},${saved},'pre-flip',...)`, database-backup.ts:92-93 and database-restore.ts:336-337 share that exact column list with no `engine`; the only source is boot-schema.ts:141 `ALTER TABLE backups ADD COLUMN engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'sqlite' CHECK(engine IN ('sqlite','pg','mysql'))`, and `dialect` has zero occurrences under packages/ and scripts/. So the previous review's ask (a) is genuinely unfixed. Severity corrected down: on SQLite the default records the correct value on every row, so the body's 'record each artifact's engine' is not false today and there is no live defect — the cost is that step 5 inherits three unlabelled inserts. Minor, not major.


### A-3 [minor] Retention protects foreign-engine artefacts forever while still counting their bytes, permanently deadlocking the 20% artefact budget

`packages/boot/src/artifact-retention.ts:160` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** Foreign-engine rows are excluded from pruning but included in the budget total, so once they exceed the 20% limit every prune call fails `backup_budget` with no in-band remedy — and the new test pins that as the expected behaviour.

**Evidence.** `artifact-retention.ts:151-152` sums every catalogued row: `backupBytes += backup.bytes;`. `:157` `if (limit === null || backupBytes + requiredBackupBytes <= limit) break;`. `:160` adds `backup.engine !== "sqlite" ||` to the skip condition whose body at `:170` is `continue`. `:179-180`: `if (limit !== null && backupBytes + requiredBackupBytes > limit) return yield* new ArtifactRetentionRejected({ code: "backup_budget" });`. The new test at `packages/boot/test/artifact-retention.test.ts:327-336` asserts exactly this: `expect(await app.prune({ capacity: 100 })).toMatchObject({ failure: { code: "backup_budget" } })`, that the catalogue is byte-identical afterwards, and that `backups/foreign.db` still holds `"retained-data"`. Every caller is on a critical path: `cutover.ts:173` (`requiredBackupBytes` 0, i.e. an ordinary cutover), `database-backup.ts:79` (hourly and manual), `database-restore.ts:298` and `:330` (restore). Design 10.2 says "`bytes` is the artefact's size ... the artefacts are what occupy the volume", and design 15.5 says an engine change "makes every prior artefact non-restorable".

**Scenario.** A deployment's boot store travels to a Postgres server and later a SQLite board is stood up from that catalogue, or any path that leaves `pg`/`mysql` rows in a SQLite deployment's `backups` table. Those artefacts are non-restorable by design 15.5, so they have no recovery value, yet `:160` makes them unprunable and `:151` keeps them in the total. As soon as they alone exceed `capacity_bytes * backup_percent / 100`, `prune` returns `backup_budget` unconditionally — so `cutover.ts:173` refuses every cutover, `database-backup.ts:79` refuses every hourly and manual backup, and `database-restore.ts:298` refuses every restore. The board can no longer accept an edit or take a safety copy, and the only fix is a human running `DELETE FROM backups` by hand, which is the opposite of the "a human can always get back in" guarantee the budget is supposed to serve. Unreachable today because nothing writes a non-`sqlite` row, but the policy and its test are now baked in.

**Suggestion.** Pick one of the two consistent policies and state it in the design doc, which currently rules on neither. Either exclude foreign-engine artefacts from `backupBytes` (they are not this deployment's budget to defend) and keep them unprunable, or — better, since they are non-restorable — prune them *first*, before any sqlite row, and keep them in the total. Protecting and counting them simultaneously is the one combination with no exit. Design section 10.2 or 10.6 should gain the ruling, and the test at `artifact-retention.test.ts:327` should assert the chosen behaviour rather than the deadlock.

**Skeptic.** Code confirmed exactly as quoted: artifact-retention.ts:151 `backupBytes += backup.bytes;` counts every row, :160 `backup.engine !== "sqlite" ||` routes foreign rows to `continue` at :170, :179-180 then fails `backup_budget`, and artifact-retention.test.ts:327-336 pins that outcome for pg and mysql. The combination genuinely has no in-band exit. Severity corrected down: unreachable in this PR — nothing writes a non-'sqlite' row (A-1 is the proof: the column only ever takes its default), so the test reaches it only via raw `UPDATE backups SET engine=...`. It is a latent policy choice the design does not rule on, not a live bug, and nothing regressed since the column did not exist before. Minor.


### A-4 [minor] `backupPath` centralizes the artefact-name validations but is not engine-aware, and two of three write sites bypass it

`packages/boot/src/backup-metadata.ts:24` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** Design 10.2's step-2 item "the three extension-aware validations" is half delivered: the three read-side checks are centralized into one helper, but the helper hardcodes `.db` and three of the five sites that derive the same name do not use it.

**Evidence.** `backup-metadata.ts:24-25`: `export const backupPath = (path: Path.Path, dataDirectory: string, id: string) => path.join(dataDirectory, "backups", `${id}.db`);` — no engine parameter. Readers use it: `database-restore.ts:61-62`, `cutover.ts:78-80`, `app-store-identity.ts:61`, `database-restore.ts:327`. Writers mostly do not: `cutover.ts:280` `const saved = path.join(directory, `${id}.db`);` and `database-backup.ts:77` `const saved = path.join(directory, `${id}.db`);`. Retention has a fourth derivation in relative form: `artifact-retention.ts:172` `const relative = path.join("backups", `${backup.id}.db`);` followed by the `:173` equality check against `path.join(directory, relative)`. Design 10.2: "All three become extension-aware by engine: `${id}.db`, `${id}.dump` or `${id}.sql`."

**Scenario.** Step 5 makes the name engine-dependent. Whoever adds the `engine` argument to `backupPath` updates the four call sites that use it and the validators start expecting `<id>.dump`; the two inline writers at `cutover.ts:280` and `database-backup.ts:77` keep emitting `<id>.db`, so every row they write fails its own validator on the next restore with `restore_backup_invalid` / `cutover_backup_invalid` — a fail-closed refusal of an artefact that is actually intact, discovered only when a human tries to restore. Retention's independent copy at `:172` fails differently and more quietly: `:173`'s path comparison stops matching, `continue` fires for every row, and pruning silently stops reclaiming anything until the budget trips (A-3).

**Suggestion.** Give `backupPath` an `engine` parameter now, returning `.db` for `"sqlite"`, and route all five sites through it: `cutover.ts:280`, `database-backup.ts:77`, `database-restore.ts:327`, and `artifact-retention.ts:172` (a `backupRelativePath(engine, id)` sibling, or pass both roots). On SQLite the change is inert, and it leaves exactly one place for step 5 to edit.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. backup-metadata.ts:24-25 `backupPath = (path, dataDirectory, id) => path.join(dataDirectory, "backups", `${id}.db`)` has no engine parameter; readers route through it (database-restore.ts:61-62, cutover.ts:78-80, database-restore.ts:345ish), while the two writers build the name inline — cutover.ts:311 `const saved = path.join(directory, `${id}.db`)` (quoted line 280 in the finding is off, the inline join is in the same pre-flip block) and database-backup.ts:77 `const saved = path.join(directory, `${id}.db`)` — and retention keeps a third form at artifact-retention.ts:172. Design 10.2's 'extension-aware by engine' is not delivered. Inert today; the cost is that the same PR that introduced the helper left three sites off it. Minor stands.


### A-6 [minor] `isDescendant` is still step-3 work landed inside step 2, now half-applied inside the one statement it edits, and still puts `@comms/storage` on the editable app's import surface

`packages/server/src/ext/core/topics.ts:30` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** The previous review's third ask is unchanged, and the partial application is now visible within a single SQL string: six fragments in one statement go through the new helper while a seventh beside them stays raw, so the step-3 convention and the old one coexist line by line.

**Evidence.** `packages/storage/src/descendant.ts:5`: `export const isDescendant = (sql: SqlClient, child: Fragment, ancestor: Fragment) => sql`substr(${child},1,length(${ancestor})+1)=${ancestor}||'/'`;`, exported at `packages/storage/package.json:9` `"./descendant": "./src/descendant.ts"`. Nine routed call sites: `public-paths.ts:52`, `:59`, `topics.ts:26`, `:27`, `:28`, `:29`, `:30`, `:31`, `:69`. In the same statement as six of them, `topics.ts:30` keeps the raw form: `AND (${path}='' OR t.path=${path} OR substr(t.path,1,length(${path})+1)=${path + "/"})`. About 25 further raw descendant fragments remain under `packages/server/src/ext/core` — `topic-move.ts:70`, `:83`, `:86`, `:94`, `:115`, `:117`, `:119`, `:120`, `messages.ts:68`, `:71`, `:165`, `topic-operations.ts:51`, `:60`, `message-operations.ts:64`, `:73`, `pages.ts:48`, `:100`, `published-messages.ts:15`, `public-page-policy.ts:47`, `topic-page-continuation.ts:17-18`, plus `boot/src/events.ts:170` and `public-paths.ts:58`. The editable app imports the boot-side workspace at `topics.ts:1` `import { isDescendant } from "@comms/storage/descendant";`. Design 6.2 scopes this to step 3 and sizes it at 41 sites; design step 3 says "Do `isDescendant` first (41 sites)".

**Scenario.** A reviewer of step 3 opens `topics.ts` and must diff two conventions inside one 6-line SQL template to work out which fragments were already converted and why one was not (its ancestor is a JS-concatenated bound literal, not a column), then repeat that judgement across the ~25 remaining sites. Meanwhile the boundary commitment — the hot-reloadable app now depends on `@comms/storage` — is made by a PR whose stated subject is backup operations, so the decision is not reviewed where it belongs. The SQL itself is byte-identical on SQLite, so nothing breaks today; the cost is entirely reviewability and the premature dependency.

**Suggestion.** Move `packages/storage/src/descendant.ts`, its package export, its two tests and the `public-paths.ts` / `topics.ts` edits into the step-3 PR and revert them here, as the previous review asked. If they stay, convert `topics.ts:30` too (bind the ancestor as `sql`${path}`` so the helper applies) so that at least no single statement mixes the two forms, and say in the body that the app's dependency on `@comms/storage` is being taken now.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. packages/storage/src/descendant.ts:5 ships here, exported at packages/storage/package.json:9 `"./descendant": "./src/descendant.ts"`; 9 routed call sites across boot/src/public-paths.ts and server/src/ext/core/topics.ts (which imports `@comms/storage/descendant` at topics.ts:1); topics.ts:30 keeps the raw `substr(t.path,1,length(${path})+1)=${path + "/"}` inside the same statement as six routed fragments; 25 raw `substr(...length(...` fragments remain under packages/*/src. design:1091 scopes isDescendant to step 3 at 41 sites. This is the previous review's ask (c), unchanged, and a genuine scope/reviewability cost plus an early dependency commitment by the hot-reloadable app. Minor stands.


### B-2 [minor] All three backup capture sites still omit `engine` and rely on the column default, which the design says must be stamped at capture

`packages/boot/src/cutover.ts:289` (scope-and-regressions)

**Claim.** The PR adds `engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'sqlite'` and reads it in five places, but none of the three INSERTs names it. Design §10.2 is explicit: "The `backups` schema gains one column, `engine`, stamped at capture from `DbOps.dialect`." This is the previous review's ask (a), unchanged at 7d47607.

**Evidence.** cutover.ts:289 `yield* sql`INSERT INTO backups(id,path,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation) VALUES(${id},${saved},'pre-flip',${bytes},${frozenAt},${fence},${generation.n})``; database-restore.ts:336-337 `INSERT INTO backups(id,path,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation) VALUES(${id},${filename},'pre-restore',...)`; database-backup.ts:92-93 `INSERT INTO backups(id,path,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation) VALUES(${id},${saved},${options.reason},...)`. `grep -rn "INSERT INTO backups"` over packages/boot/src and packages/server/src returns exactly these three.

**Scenario.** After the §15 SQLite-to-Postgres transfer the boot store's `backups` rows are carried across and the `engine` column's DDL (and its 'sqlite' default) comes with them. The first hourly backup on the Postgres deployment writes a `pg_dump` artifact through database-backup.ts:92 and the row is stamped `engine='sqlite'` by the default. database-restore-auth.ts:45 then refuses it — `if (backup.engine !== "sqlite") return yield* new AuthError({ code: "backup_engine_mismatch" })` inverted for a pg deployment — so every artifact the deployment has just produced is unrestorable, and §15.5's promise that "the first pre-flip or hourly backup on the target is the first restorable one" is false. The fix is one word per statement and costs nothing while the change is inert.

**Suggestion.** Name the column in all three statements (`INSERT INTO backups(id,engine,path,...) VALUES(${id},'sqlite',${saved},...)`) now, while 'sqlite' is the only correct value, so that step 5 replaces a literal with `DbOps.dialect` rather than hunting for three statements that silently depend on a DDL default.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. cutover.ts:289, database-restore.ts:336-337 and database-backup.ts:92-93 are the only three `INSERT INTO backups` in packages/boot/src and packages/server/src and none names `engine`; the column is `engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'sqlite' CHECK(engine IN ('sqlite','pg','mysql'))` at boot-schema.ts:139. docs/database.md:919 is explicit: 'The `backups` schema gains one column, `engine`, stamped at capture from `DbOps.dialect`.' This is the previous review's ask (a), unaddressed. Inert today because 'sqlite' is the only correct value, so minor is right.


### B-3 [minor] isDescendant migration leaves one prefix match un-converted inside the same SQL statement it migrated

`packages/server/src/ext/core/topics.ts:30` (scope-and-regressions)

**Claim.** Six of the seven prefix matches in `makeTopics`'s main query were routed through the new helper; the seventh, in the same statement, was left on the raw form. The statement now emits two different spellings of the same predicate, and the one left behind is the one that will not be fixed when the dialect branches land.

**Evidence.** topics.ts:30 `FROM visible_topics t WHERE NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM visible_topics a WHERE a.deleted_at IS NOT NULL AND (t.path=a.path OR ${isDescendant(sql, sql`t.path`, sql`a.path`)})) AND (${path}='' OR t.path=${path} OR substr(t.path,1,length(${path})+1)=${path + "/"})` — the converted call and the leftover `substr(...)` are in one line. Counts: 9 `isDescendant(` call sites at HEAD, 34 remaining `substr(` occurrences in packages/boot/src + packages/server/src (43 at f4f1dca); design §6.2 scopes the helper at 41 sites.

**Scenario.** Step 5 adds the per-dialect branches from design §6.2, whose MySQL form is `substr(${child},1,char_language(${ancestor})+1)=CONCAT(${ancestor},'/')` — specifically `char_length`, because MySQL's `LENGTH()` counts bytes. Every site routed through the helper gets that fix; topics.ts:30 does not, so on MySQL a topic whose path contains a multibyte character (the test fixture's own `日本語/é` case) silently stops matching its own subtree in the topic-list filter, returning an empty or truncated topic list with no error. A reviewer auditing "is the prefix matching portable?" sees `isDescendant` in the statement and has no signal that one branch of the same WHERE clause bypasses it.

**Suggestion.** Convert topics.ts:30's remaining match in this PR (it is the same one-line edit as the six beside it), or, if the intent is to hold the helper back as step-3 work per the previous review's ask (c), revert all nine call sites and the storage module out of this PR so the 41 sites land together and are counted once.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed by reading topics.ts:30, which holds both spellings in one line: `... (t.path=a.path OR ${isDescendant(sql, sql`t.path`, sql`a.path`)})) AND (${path}='' OR t.path=${path} OR substr(t.path,1,length(${path})+1)=${path + "/"})`. The six siblings at :26-:31 and :69 are converted; this one is not, and the helper accepts a bound-parameter ancestor (storage/test/fixtures/descendant.ts:36-40 exercises exactly that form), so the conversion is the same one-line edit. The MySQL divergence the finder cites is real: docs/database.md:505 gives `substr(${child},1,char_length(${ancestor})+1)=CONCAT(${ancestor},'/')`, and `length()` counts bytes on MySQL. Minor, since the whole migration is step-3 work that arguably should not be here at all.


### B-4 [minor] The new backupPath helper is used by the four validators but bypassed by both capture sites that create the names

`packages/boot/src/database-backup.ts:77` (scope-and-regressions)

**Claim.** `backupPath` (backup-metadata.ts:24) is introduced as the single definition of a SQLite artifact's name and adopted at the four validation sites, but the two places that actually mint the name still hand-roll it. Design §10.2 requires these to change together ("All three become extension-aware by engine: `${id}.db`, `${id}.dump` or `${id}.sql`").

**Evidence.** backup-metadata.ts:23-25 `/** SQLite artifact names are rooted in DATA_DIR, never derived from the live store location. */ export const backupPath = (path: Path.Path, dataDirectory: string, id: string) => path.join(dataDirectory, "backups", `${id}.db`);`. Adopted at cutover.ts:78 and :80, database-restore.ts:61-62 and :327, app-store-identity.ts:61. Bypassed at database-backup.ts:75-77 `const directory = path.join(recovery.dataDirectory, "backups"); ... const saved = path.join(directory, `${id}.db`);` and cutover.ts:278-280 `const directory = path.join(options.dataDirectory, "backups"); ... const saved = path.join(directory, `${id}.db`);` (artifact-retention.ts:172 builds the same name a third way, relative to its root).

**Scenario.** Step 5 makes `backupPath` extension-aware by engine. The four validators start expecting `backups/<id>.dump` on Postgres; database-backup.ts:77 and cutover.ts:280 keep writing and cataloguing `backups/<id>.db`. Every subsequent restore of a backup taken after that change fails the path comparison at database-restore.ts:61 with `restore_backup_invalid`, and cutover rollback fails at cutover.ts:78 with `cutover_backup_invalid` — i.e. the pre-flip safety backup becomes unusable precisely when a cutover needs to roll back. The failure is silent until someone tries to restore.

**Suggestion.** Route both capture sites through `backupPath(path, recovery.dataDirectory, id)` / `backupPath(path, options.dataDirectory, id)` in this PR, and make artifact-retention.ts:172 derive its relative name from the same helper, so there is exactly one place that knows the extension.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. backup-metadata.ts:24-25 defines `backupPath`; it is used at cutover.ts:78/:80, database-restore.ts:61/:62/:327 and app-store-identity.ts:61, while database-backup.ts:75-77 and cutover.ts:278-280 still build `path.join(directory, `${id}.db`)` by hand and artifact-retention.ts:172 builds `path.join("backups", `${backup.id}.db`)` a third way. One correction to the supporting claim: the DATA_DIR rooting itself is not new — `git diff f4f1dca..HEAD` shows the validation sites already read `recovery.dataDirectory` / `options.dataDirectory` at base, so this is a pure seam extraction whose only cost is the two capture sites left outside it. Minor; the drift only bites when step 5 makes the helper extension-aware.


### A-5 [note] `POST /_boot/db/backup` returns a record without `engine` while the list route now shows it on every row

`packages/boot/src/database-backup.ts:82` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** The capture response omits the provenance field the catalogue now carries, so the object the API hands back no longer matches `BackupRecord` and an agent cannot tell from the capture alone whether the artefact it just made is restorable by this deployment.

**Evidence.** `database-backup.ts:82-89` builds the returned value as `const record = { id, reason: options.reason, bytes, taken_at: taken, published_through: published, generation: active.generation.n };` with no `engine`, and `backup-http.ts:40-41` serializes it directly: `service.capture(...).pipe(Effect.map((record) => HttpServerResponse.jsonUnsafe(record, ...)))`. The schema that describes a catalogued backup requires the field: `backup-metadata.ts:7` `engine: Schema.Literals(["sqlite", "pg", "mysql"])`, and the list route emits it: `backup-inventory.ts:7`, `:20`, pinned at `packages/server/test/backup-inventory.test.ts:67-83`. Design 10.2 states the contract as "`GET /_boot/db/backups` shows the engine on every row".

**Scenario.** The app-side drill of design 10.4 captures a backup, then has to decide whether it can restore it. The `POST /_boot/db/backup` body it just received has no `engine`, so it must issue a second `GET /_boot/db/backups` and page to find its own row — and any client that decodes the capture response with `BackupRecord` fails on a missing required field rather than getting a typed record. The two shapes also drift as soon as a second engine exists: the list says `pg`, the capture says nothing.

**Suggestion.** Add `engine` to the `record` object at `database-backup.ts:82` from the same `DbOps.dialect` field A-1 introduces, so the capture response and the catalogue row are the same shape and both decode as `BackupRecord`.

**Skeptic.** Omission confirmed: database-backup.ts:82-89 builds `record` with id, reason, bytes, taken_at, published_through, generation and no `engine`, and backup-http.ts:40-41 serializes it unchanged, while backup-inventory.ts:7/:20 emit engine on the list route. But the claim's sharper half is unsupported: BackupRecord is boot-internal only (grep shows uses solely in backup-metadata, cutover, db-ops, database-restore, artifact-retention — nothing in packages/protocol or packages/server decodes an API response with it), so no client 'fails on a missing required field'. Design 10.2 specifies engine on the GET rows only, not the POST body. Real but cosmetic asymmetry: note.


### A-7 [note] Rung 18 does not extend the pending-recovery upgrade refusal, so a store at 17 with an in-flight cutover or restore is migrated past the image that can finish it

`packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:39` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** The ladder ceiling moved 17 → 18 but the `BootIdentityUpgradePending` window was left at `version < 17`, so the one rung this PR adds is the one rung the guard does not cover.

**Evidence.** `boot-schema.ts:37` `if (version > 18) return yield* new BootSchemaTooNew({ found: version, supported: 18 });` and `:138-140` add rung 18 and `PRAGMA user_version = 18`. The guard immediately above is unchanged from PR #3: `:38-46` `// Refuse before schema or journal-mode changes so the previous image can finish recovery.` / `if (version >= 9 && version < 17) { ... if (cutovers.length > 0 || restores.length > 0) return yield* new BootIdentityUpgradePending({}); }`. Git history confirms the bound was introduced with rung 17 in `0658fe7` and not touched by `db0e92d`. The existing test only covers the 16 → 17 case: `test/app-store-identity.test.ts:311-313` asserts `user_version` stays 16 and `legacy_store_id` is absent.

**Scenario.** A board running the PR #3 image is at `user_version` 17 with `cutover.phase != 'accepted'` or a `db_restore_requests` row in `authorized/restoring/working/rollback`. The PR #4 image starts, skips the guard because 17 is outside `[9, 17)`, and commits rung 18. The operator who then wants to roll back to the PR #3 image to finish that recovery — the scenario the guard's own message describes — is refused with `BootSchemaTooNew { found: 18, supported: 17 }`, and the in-flight restore can only be completed by the newer image. Benign in practice because PR #3 and PR #4 ship as one stack so no deployment ever runs a 17-only image, and rung 18 is additive with a default the restore path reads correctly, which is presumably why the bound was left alone.

**Suggestion.** Either extend the window to `version >= 9 && version < 18` and add the 17-with-pending-recovery case to `test/app-store-identity.test.ts` alongside the existing 16 case, or add one comment at `boot-schema.ts:38` stating that the window is deliberately scoped to the identity rung and that purely additive rungs above it are safe to apply under a pending recovery — so the next rung author knows which rule they are following.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. boot-schema.ts ceiling is 18 (`if (version > 18) return yield* new BootSchemaTooNew({ found: version, supported: 18 })`) and rung 18 plus `PRAGMA user_version = 18` are added, while the BootIdentityUpgradePending window immediately below is still `if (version >= 9 && version < 17)`, so a v17 store with a pending cutover or restore is migrated to 18 and can no longer be completed by the PR #3 image. Real but benign — PR #3 and PR #4 ship as one stack, and rung 18 is purely additive with a correct default. Note stands.


### A-8 [note] The repo's own status records at this head contradict the PR body on the Linux result and still carry the corrected line figure

`docs/codex-scratchpad.md:105` (dbops-surface)

**Claim.** No documentation was updated in this delta, so two in-repo records still describe the superseded revision 2b3e365, still assert the "+38 production lines" figure the previous review rejected, and still record the Linux suite as failing two tests with no diagnosis — while the PR body says Linux, image and QEMU all pass at 7d47607.

Linux34614609950 finishes732/734: storage-startup restored-policy message returns507, and server cutover slow-body test reaches its20s aggregate timeout. Both lack diagnostic cause ... Production delta+38lines." `docs/build-plan.md:131` repeats the same revision and "Linux 34614609950 finishes 732/734, with an unexplained restored-policy 507 and a slow-body cutover aggregate timeout". The PR body says "At `7d47607`, Linux tests, image acceptance and QEMU recovery pass." `git diff --name-only f4f1dca..HEAD` outside `packages/` and `scripts/` is empty — no docs, workflow or Dockerfile change. Measured production delta is +117 −74, net +43 across `packages/*/src` and `scripts` (+187 −144 with `--no-renames`), not +38.

**Scenario.** A reviewer or the owner reads the two in-repo records — which are the durable artefact, where the PR body is not — and concludes the Linux suite still has two undiagnosed failures in the freeze-and-drain machinery at this head, which is the exact condition the previous review named as a merge blocker for a PR that renames the service the restore path depends on. There is no way to tell from the repo which statement is current, and the stale line figure is the one the previous review already asked to be corrected.

If they merely did not recur, say so — a flake that stopped reproducing is a different status from a fixed failure.

Production delta+38lines', docs/build-plan.md:131 repeats it, `git diff --name-only f4f1dca..HEAD` has nothing outside packages/ and scripts/, and the measured delta is +117 -74 src/scripts and +308 -78 test, not +38. ### B-5 [note] Foreign-engine artifacts are made permanently unprunable while still counting against the 20% budget, so the budget refusal becomes unsatisfiable

`packages/boot/src/artifact-retention.ts:160` (scope-and-regressions)

**Claim.** The new `backup.engine !== "sqlite"` condition puts foreign artifacts on the `continue` branch of the prune loop, but their `bytes` are still summed into `backupBytes` at :151 before the loop. Once foreign artifacts alone exceed the limit, no sequence of prunes can satisfy :179, so `ArtifactRetentionRejected{backup_budget}` is permanent. The PR's own test asserts this as the intended behaviour rather than flagging it.

**Evidence.** artifact-retention.ts:144-151 sums every catalogued row: `for (const backup of catalog.backups) { ... backupBytes += backup.bytes; }`. The prune loop at :156-170 skips foreign rows: `if (protectedBackups.has(backup.id) || backup.engine !== "sqlite" || backup.published_through === null || ...) continue;`. The refusal at :179-180: `if (limit !== null && backupBytes + requiredBackupBytes > limit) return yield* new ArtifactRetentionRejected({ code: "backup_budget" });`. The new test at artifact-retention.test.ts:327-336 codifies it: `await app.sql(`UPDATE backups SET engine='${engine}' WHERE id='foreign'`); ... expect(await app.prune({ capacity: 100 })).toMatchObject({ failure: { code: "backup_budget" } }); expect(await app.sql("SELECT * FROM backups")).toEqual(before);`

**Scenario.** A deployment transfers SQLite to Postgres per §15. The carried-over catalogue holds, say, 3 GiB of `.db` artifacts now stamped non-sqlite, on a volume whose 20% backup budget is 2 GiB. `retention.prune` is called before every clone: cutover.ts:282, database-backup.ts:79, database-restore.ts:298 and :330. All of them now fail `backup_budget` forever. Every edit cutover, every hourly backup and every restore is blocked, with no operation the software offers that can free the space — the artifacts it refuses to delete are also the ones §15.5 says are non-restorable. Design §10.6 puts dumps squarely in the volume part of the budget ("Dumps are still files on the volume, catalogued in `backups` with `bytes`"), so something must be able to reclaim them.

**Suggestion.** Either exclude foreign-engine bytes from `backupBytes` (they are not this deployment's restore history and should not consume its budget), or give retention an explicit, age-or-count-bounded path to delete non-restorable foreign artifacts. Whichever is chosen, design §10.2/§10.6 needs the correction written down — it currently says foreign artifacts are refused for restore but never says who deletes them or how the budget survives them.

**Skeptic.** The code is as described: artifact-retention.ts:144-151 sums every row's bytes into `backupBytes`, :160 adds `backup.engine !== "sqlite"` to the skip list, :179-180 refuses `backup_budget`, and artifact-retention.test.ts:327-336 codifies the permanent refusal. But the scenario is wrong in a way that matters: `engine` records who wrote the artifact, so after a SQLite-to-Postgres transfer the carried rows stay `engine='sqlite'` (docs/database.md:919, 925) and remain prunable; the rows this skips on a Postgres deployment are the new `.dump` rows, which the hardcoded relative name at :172 (`backups/<id>.db`) would already exclude from pruning with or without the new condition. The sharper latent issue is that :160 compares against the literal `"sqlite"` rather than the deployment dialect, so on a remote engine the loop prunes exactly the artifacts that are non-restorable and preserves exactly the ones that are. Today every row is sqlite, so there is zero behaviour change, and the cross-engine catalogue the scenario needs does not exist yet. Note, not minor; the request for a design correction in §10.2/§10.6 about who reclaims foreign artifacts is fair.


### B-6 [note] cutover.ts hardcodes engine: "sqlite" into restoreInto, making DbOps' own engine refusal unreachable on the cutover-rollback path

`packages/boot/src/cutover.ts:89` (scope-and-regressions)

**Claim.** The cutover restore path reads the full `BackupRecord` (including `engine`) but then constructs a synthetic argument with the engine literal instead of forwarding the row's value. The guard at db-ops.ts:58 — the second check the design asks for on this exact path — can therefore never fire from cutover, and the single guard at cutover.ts:74 is load-bearing alone.

**Evidence.** cutover.ts:70-74 `const rows = yield* sql`SELECT * FROM backups WHERE id=${record.backup}`.pipe(Effect.flatMap(Schema.decodeUnknownEffect(Schema.Array(BackupRecord)))); const artifact = rows[0]; if (artifact && artifact.engine !== "sqlite") return yield* new ChildError({ code: "backup_engine_mismatch" });` then cutover.ts:89 `yield* backup.restoreInto({ path: saved, legacy_store_id: artifact?.legacy_store_id ?? null, engine: "sqlite" });`. Compare database-restore.ts:125 and :139, which pass the decoded row straight through (`yield* backup.restoreInto(yield* saved(record.safety_backup))`). db-ops.ts:58 `if (backup.engine !== "sqlite") return yield* new ChildError({ code: "backup_engine_mismatch" });`. Design §10.2 names "the cutover rollback" as one of the three places that must refuse a foreign artefact.

**Scenario.** Someone later reorders or narrows the check at cutover.ts:74 — for example folding it into the combined `if (!saved || saved !== backupPath(...) ...)` block below it, which returns `cutover_backup_invalid` rather than `backup_engine_mismatch` — and the hardcoded literal at :89 means `restoreInto` accepts the artifact as SQLite and `fs.copyFile(backup.path, temporary)` copies a Postgres dump into the app store's staging, where `verifyAppIdentity` fails on a file that is not a SQLite database and the operator gets a decode error instead of `backup_engine_mismatch`. Type-checking will not catch the removal because the literal satisfies the parameter type.

**Suggestion.** Pass `engine: artifact.engine` (narrowing `artifact` first so the existing guard still types), so the literal cannot outlive the check that justifies it.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed verbatim. cutover.ts:70-74 decodes the full `BackupRecord` and refuses `artifact.engine !== "sqlite"`, then :89 calls `backup.restoreInto({ path: saved, legacy_store_id: artifact?.legacy_store_id ?? null, engine: "sqlite" })`, while database-restore.ts:125 and :139 pass the decoded row through unchanged. db-ops.ts:58's guard is therefore dead on the cutover path and the literal satisfies the `Pick<BackupRecord, "path"|"legacy_store_id"|"engine">` parameter type, so removing the :74 check would not fail typecheck. Defence-in-depth only today; note is the right severity.


### B-7 [note] Boot-executed SQL text moved into @comms/storage, where check-invariants' app-domain-table guard does not look

`scripts/check-invariants.ts:52` (scope-and-regressions)

**Claim.** `checkDomainSql` only inspects SQL literals under `packages/boot/src/`. Putting a SQL-emitting helper that boot executes into `packages/storage/src/` moves that text outside the only directory the R12 guard covers. The rename exception was updated correctly, but this coverage gap is new.

**Evidence.** check-invariants.ts:51-52 `function checkDomainSql(node: ts.Node, text: string) { if (!collectTables && !relative(root, path).startsWith(`packages${sep}boot${sep}src${sep}`)) return;`. The new helper is storage/src/descendant.ts:5-6 `export const isDescendant = (sql: SqlClient, child: Fragment, ancestor: Fragment) => sql`substr(${child},1,length(${ancestor})+1)=${ancestor}||'/'`;` and it is called from boot at public-paths.ts:52 and :59. Design §6.2 places this helper in `packages/server/src/kernel/dialect.ts`, not in the storage package. check-invariants.ts:41 already allowed `boot -> storage`, so nothing in the script objects.

**Scenario.** A later helper added beside `descendant.ts` — say a portable `topicSubtree(sql)` fragment that names `topics` or `messages` — is imported and executed by boot, and `bun scripts/check-invariants.ts` reports "Workspace import boundaries passed" because the literal lives in packages/storage. R12's stated definition of done ("Boot knows no app domain table name... The CI grep from R-habit 2 is part of the definition of done for every step") is satisfied by the script while being false in fact.

**Suggestion.** Extend `checkDomainSql`'s path predicate to cover every package boot depends on for SQL text (at minimum `packages/storage/src/`), or keep the helper where design §6.2 puts it and widen the predicate to that file too. Either way the script should fail if boot-reachable SQL names a collected app table regardless of which package the literal sits in.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. check-invariants.ts:51-52 returns early unless the file is under `packages/boot/src/`, and the new SQL-emitting helper is storage/src/descendant.ts:5-6, executed by boot via public-paths.ts:52 and :59, with boot -> storage already allowed at :41. docs/database.md:1091 does put the section 6 helpers in the server kernel's dialect.ts, not in storage. The gap is latent, not live: descendant.ts names no table, only `substr`/`length` over interpolated fragments, so R12 is not actually violated today. Note.


### Item status


#### dbops-surface

Scope reviewed: `packages/boot/src/db-ops.ts` in full against `app-backup.ts` at f4f1dca, plus backup-metadata, backup-inventory, database-backup, database-restore, database-restore-auth, restore-generation, artifact-retention, cutover, index, boot-schema, child-error-policy, child-process, auth, app-store-identity, public-paths, server/ext/core/topics and storage/descendant at 7d47607.

What the rename actually changed is small and mostly good. `db-ops.ts` is the old `AppBackup` object with four edits: the service is retagged `comms/boot/DbOps` (:85), `make` now takes `(store: FileStore, dataDirectory: string)` instead of a bare filename (:12), `clone`/`prepareClone` take `FileStore` descriptors instead of path strings (:36, :48), and `restore` becomes `restoreInto`, refusing a non-sqlite artefact with a typed error before anything is copied (:55-58). Rooting `storageHeadroom` in `dataDirectory` rather than `path.dirname(filename)` (:19) is a genuine improvement over the base: the clone destinations all live under DATA_DIR, so that is the volume the budget should measure, and it matches design 10.2.

Engine provenance is read back and enforced correctly on every path I could find: authorize (`database-restore-auth.ts:45`), restore selection (`database-restore.ts:56`), cutover rollback (`cutover.ts:74`), the copy itself (`db-ops.ts:58`), and identity adoption's backup authority (`app-store-identity.ts:60`). The error is typed, 409 and `retriable: false` (`child-error-policy.ts:46-50`), it is spread into the auth policy table, and it is pinned by tests. Rung 18 is correct, additive and idempotent, with a v17→v18 fixture asserting exact provenance preservation. Retention cannot delete an artefact a pending restore depends on (`artifact-retention.ts:104-110`, :123-126), and inventory and retention agree that `id` identifies a backup.

Three things are wrong from this dimension's point of view. First, `backups.engine` is still never written by any of the three production inserts (`cutover.ts:289`, `database-backup.ts:92`, `database-restore.ts:336`) — the value comes only from the column default — which is exactly the previous review's ask (a), unfixed, while the PR body says the change "record[s] each artifact's engine". Second, the step-2 `DbOps` surface is almost entirely absent and the body does not list it as deferred: `dialect`, `store`, `DbOpsError`, `cloneForRehearsal`, `backup`, `dropClone`, `reapClones`, `capacity`, `readOnlySession`, `tableExists`, the `REHEARSAL_COPY_BUDGET` and the `scratch_limit`/`clone_load_failed`/`rehearsal_copy_timeout` codes all have zero occurrences repo-wide; `readOnlySession` in particular is deferred silently. Third, retention now permanently protects foreign-engine artefacts while still counting their bytes, which the new test at `artifact-retention.test.ts:327-336` pins as "prune fails with `backup_budget`" — a state in which no cutover, hourly backup, manual backup or restore can ever complete again.

Measured production delta at 7d47607 over f4f1dca: **+117 −74, net +43** across `packages/*/src` and `scripts` (+187 −144 without rename detection). The body no longer claims a line figure, so the previous review's "38 production lines" complaint is moot for the body — but `docs/codex-scratchpad.md:105` and `docs/build-plan.md:131` in this same worktree still say "Production delta+38lines", still describe 2b3e365, and still record Linux as 732/734 with two unexplained failures, directly contradicting the body's "At 7d47607, Linux tests, image acceptance and QEMU recovery pass". Step 2's own budget is +280/−80, consistent with the missing operations.

One smaller note not worth a finding: the test file and fixture for the renamed service are still `packages/boot/test/app-backup.test.ts` and `test/fixtures/app-backup.ts` even though `scripts/check-invariants.ts:13` was updated for the rename, so the only tests for `db-ops.ts` are not discoverable by name.

#### scope-and-regressions

Note: the worktree is at scratchpad/wt-pr4, not scratchpad/stack/wt-pr4; I read only that detached checkout at 7d47607.

Accounting for the diff. The src delta over f4f1dca is +119 -75 across 19 files and is textually identical to PR #4's own commit delta over 0658fe7 — the two merges contribute nothing. Of the five files that are not obviously backup concerns, three are: auth.ts:57, child-process.ts and child-error-policy.ts:46-50 each add the new `backup_engine_mismatch` code, and the policy entry is not optional — child-error-policy.ts's map is `satisfies Readonly<Record<ChildError["code"], ...>>` and auth-http.ts:37 spreads it to cover AuthError codes, so omitting it would not compile. Two are not: public-paths.ts and server/src/ext/core/topics.ts are the `isDescendant` consumers the previous review asked to split out, 9 call sites of ~40. Everything else is a mechanical consequence of the AppBackup -> DbOps rename plus the descriptor-shaped call sites.

The merges are clean. Both are byte-identical to a fresh three-way recompute with zero manual resolution: `git merge-tree --write-tree db0e92d af25db0` emits tree f95719f3 with no conflict section and diffs empty against 7d6c737; the same holds for 7d6c737+f4f1dca against 7d47607. PR #3's four post-0658fe7 files (supervisor.ts and three server tests) are blob-identical at f4f1dca and HEAD. Nothing was dropped and nothing appears in the merge that is in neither parent.

Regression lens. The freeze and drain sequences, the keeper closure proof (`assertClosure` at all seven sites, `recoverClosure` before `recoverStaging`), the close-handle protocol and the staging finalizers are untouched by the rename; the new engine refusal is correctly placed before the first destructive call on every path and is tested for non-destructiveness. The DATA_DIR rooting of both `backupPath` and the DbOps headroom is an improvement on the base that the design only implies, and needs no doc correction. Two real seams are left half-migrated: the capture sites bypass `backupPath` (B-4) and one prefix match inside an otherwise-migrated statement bypasses `isDescendant` (B-3).

One finding blocks. boot-schema.ts:39 raises the schema target to 18 but leaves the `BootIdentityUpgradePending` refusal bound at `version < 17` — the refusal PR #3 added one commit earlier, with its bound deliberately equal to that PR's new target. A store already at 17 with an in-flight cutover or restore is therefore migrated to 18 without refusal, and the PR #3 image can no longer open it (`BootSchemaTooNew` fails the whole service graph, leaving every /_boot route at 503). That is the exact window the refusal exists for, and the fix is one character. Two further minors carry concrete post-transfer failures: the three `INSERT INTO backups` still omit `engine` against a design that says "stamped at capture" (B-2, the previous review's unaddressed ask (a)), and foreign-engine artifacts are made permanently unprunable while still counted against the 20% budget, which the PR's own new test codifies as intended (B-5) — design §10.2/§10.6 needs a correction saying who reclaims them. Previous ask (b) is now verifiably satisfied: image, recovery, verify (1) and verify (2) all pass at 7d47607.

### Skeptic corrections


**dbops-surface.** Two rows I disagree with; everything else I checked matches the code.

1. "Design 10.2: cross-engine restore refused with `backup_engine_mismatch`" — marked **fixed** with "four independent points". It is **partly**. The cutover rollback path does not pass the row's engine into the service at all: `cutover.ts:89` is `yield* backup.restoreInto({ path: saved, legacy_store_id: artifact?.legacy_store_id ?? null, engine: "sqlite" })` — a fabricated literal, not `artifact.engine`. So the in-`restoreInto` guard at `db-ops.ts:58` (`if (backup.engine !== "sqlite") return yield* new ChildError({ code: "backup_engine_mismatch" })`) is dead on this path; only the outer check at `cutover.ts:74` protects it. The other two call sites do thread the real record (`database-restore.ts:125` and `:139` pass the `saved(...)`/`target` row). Three independent points, not four, and one of them is structurally bypassed.

2. "Design 10.5/10.6: retention and the artefact budget agree on foreign-engine artefacts" — marked **regressed**. The combination at `artifact-retention.ts:151` (counts every row) and `:160` (`backup.engine !== "sqlite" ||` → `continue`) is real, but nothing regressed: before this PR the column did not exist, and after it no production insert can produce a non-`sqlite` row (`cutover.ts:289`, `database-backup.ts:92`, `database-restore.ts:336` all omit the column, per the same reviewer's A-1). The honest status is **partly / new unruled policy**, reachable only by hand-written SQL as `artifact-retention.test.ts:331` does.

Rows I re-verified and agree with: previous asks (a) unchanged and (c) unchanged; rung 18 fixed (`boot-schema.ts:137-142`); DATA_DIR rooting fixed and an improvement (`db-ops.ts:12`, `:19`, vs base `storageHeadroom(path.dirname(filename))`; `storageHeadroom` samples lazily at `storage-headroom.ts:54-62`, so moving the root is not a behaviour hazard); legacy stamping scoped to SQLite an improvement; `restoreInto` partly (returns void, `db-ops.ts:55-82`); `app_store_database` absent; every zero-occurrence grep in the DbOps-surface rows reproduced exactly; measured delta reproduced at src/scripts +117 −74 and test +308 −78.


**scope-and-regressions.** Four rows I read differently, plus one framing correction.

1. Summary line "One finding blocks" (and the item row "Schema bump refusal ... regressed"). Overstated. boot-schema.ts:138-140 is one additive `ALTER TABLE backups ADD COLUMN engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'sqlite'` plus `PRAGMA user_version = 18`; nothing in the cutover or db_restore_requests journals is touched, so the PR #4 image can finish the in-flight recovery it inherits and every /_boot route stays up. The loss is the rollback option to the PR #3 image, in a window that only exists if PR #3 shipped as its own image (the merged base dd733fe is at target 16 and is still caught by the `version < 17` bound). Also, PR #3's own body scoped the refusal to "pending preidentity database recovery", and the class is named BootIdentityUpgradePending, so "regressed" is stronger than the evidence: the bound was plausibly written as "stores below the identity rung", not "stores below the target". Real and worth fixing; not a blocker.

2. Row "Design §6.2: isDescendant ... only 9 of ~40 sites are converted" — the counts are partly wrong. At HEAD `grep -rn "substr(" packages/boot/src packages/server/src` returns 26, not 34, and there are exactly 9 `isDescendant(` call sites (public-paths.ts:52, :59; topics.ts:26, :27, :28, :29, :30, :31, :69). The 9-of-~40 framing survives; the 34 figure does not. The step-ordering objection is stronger than the row states: docs/database.md:1091 opens step 3 with "Do `isDescendant` first (41 sites)", so this is named step-3 work inside a step-2 PR, which is the previous review's ask (c) verbatim.

3. Row "Design §10.2: the backups directory comes from DATA_DIR ... improvement / fully done ... All five derivations now root in a data directory". Overcredited. `git diff f4f1dca..HEAD -- packages/boot/src/cutover.ts packages/boot/src/database-restore.ts packages/boot/src/app-store-identity.ts` shows the base already read `path.join(recovery.dataDirectory, "backups", `${id}.db`)` (database-restore.ts:61 at base), `path.join(options.dataDirectory, ...)` (cutover.ts:79 at base) and `dataDirectory ?? path.dirname(filename)` (app-store-identity.ts:49 at base). The only behavioural moves in this PR are db-ops.ts:19 `storageHeadroom(dataDirectory)` replacing `storageHeadroom(path.dirname(filename))` and db-ops.ts:17 threading `dataDirectory` into `appStoreIdentity`; the rest is a pure seam extraction into `backupPath`. Still an improvement (in the isolated layout `<DATA_DIR>/store` may not exist when `df` runs), just a much smaller one than "all five derivations".

4. Row "The storage descendant helper is not used for a security check whose failure mode is silent — fixed". The label contradicts its own evidence ("It IS used for such a check", public-paths.ts:51-52). The substance is right and I verified it: the rewrite is literal (`=${value.path + "/"}` becomes `=${value.path}||'/'`), and storage/test/fixtures/descendant.ts:10-28 covers strict descendant vs equality, empty and leading-slash roots, `日本語/é`, literal `%` `_` `*`, an embedded quote and the NOCASE/BINARY case, in both the column-column and bound-parameter forms. Mark it "unchanged-and-tested" rather than "fixed".

Rows I checked and agree with: the two merge-cleanliness rows (`git merge-tree --write-tree db0e92d af25db0` -> f95719f3 with no conflict section, and `git diff -M f4f1dca HEAD` textually identical to `git diff -M 0658fe7 db0e92d`); auth.ts:57 / child-process.ts:31 / child-error-policy.ts:46-50 being forced by `satisfies Readonly<Record<ChildError["code"], ...>>`; asks (a) and (c) unchanged; the freeze/drain, assertClosure, close-handle, staging-finalizer and failed-restore rows (I re-read db-ops.ts whole, database-restore.ts:40-70 and :355-390, cutover.ts:55-100 and :210-300); backup-inventory.ts:7/:20; app-store-identity.ts:129's `AND engine='sqlite'`; and the check-invariants exception rename at :13 (db-ops.ts:52 touches only kernel_writer and no stale app-backup.ts key remains).


### What the skeptics found that the finders missed


**dbops-surface.** `packages/boot/src/cutover.ts:89` fabricates the engine instead of forwarding it: `yield* backup.restoreInto({ path: saved, legacy_store_id: artifact?.legacy_store_id ?? null, engine: "sqlite" })`, even though the full row is in hand two lines earlier (`cutover.ts:71` decodes `SELECT * FROM backups WHERE id=${record.backup}` as `BackupRecord`, and `cutover.ts:74` reads `artifact.engine`). The other two callers pass the real record (`database-restore.ts:125`, `:139`). This is the one place in the PR where provenance is asserted rather than read, and it is the rollback path — the path whose whole job is to get a broken board back to a known-good store.

Concrete cost: the defence-in-depth check this PR adds inside the service (`db-ops.ts:58`) is unreachable from cutover rollback, so the guarantee here rests on a single check in a different file. If step 5 (or any refactor) moves the `cutover.ts:74` validation — e.g. folds it into `backupPath`-style centralization, or relaxes it to "engine matches this deployment" — the literal at :89 silently keeps telling the service "this is SQLite", and `db-ops.ts:67` `fs.copyFile(backup.path, temporary)` then copies a `pg_dump` over the staging store and opens it as SQLite at `:70-73`. The fix is one token (`engine: artifact.engine`, with the existing `!artifact` refusal at `:77-83` already narrowing it), and it also removes the `artifact?.` optionality that hides the non-null case from the type checker. Related smaller instance in the same pattern: the reviewer's "four independent refusal points" claim rests on this call, which is why the status row above needs correcting too.


**scope-and-regressions.** Step 2's engine-neutral budget work is absent and the body's deferral sentence does not cover it. docs/database.md:1079 lists, as step 2 changes, "Add `capacity` delegating to `storage-volume.ts`, `tableExists`, `readOnlySession`, `dropClone` and `reapClones`" and "Add the rehearsal-copy budget (section 5.3) and the success-path `dropClone` (section 5.4)". None of those names exists anywhere in packages/boot/src at HEAD (`grep -rn "capacity\|tableExists\|readOnlySession\|dropClone\|reapClones\|REHEARSAL" packages/boot/src` returns only storage-volume.ts/event-storage.ts `capacity_bytes` hits), and db-ops.ts:33-82 exposes exactly `recoverStaging`, `estimatedBytes`, `clone`, `prepareClone`, `restoreInto`.

The §5.3 gap is the one with a cost today, because it is the inconsistency the design was written to fix and it is purely SQLite. cutover.ts:218 `yield* backup.clone({ _tag: "file", filename: clone })` has no budget at all — the `Effect.timeout("30 seconds")` at cutover.ts:226 is piped onto `rehearsed.process.health`, i.e. the child's self-test, exactly as docs/database.md:448 describes — while database-backup.ts:109 wraps the whole hourly capture, `backup.clone` at :80 included, in `Effect.timeout("10 seconds")`. So on a multi-GB board a `VACUUM INTO` that needs more than ten seconds fails every hourly backup (the 10s budget is not the clone's own), and the same copy inside an edit can block the cutover indefinitely with no budget to trip. docs/database.md:450 specifies the fix (`REHEARSAL_COPY_BUDGET`, 30s on SQLite, `rehearsal_copy_timeout` surfaced verbatim by the edit route) and assigns it to this step.

The PR body's deferral line — "Remote engines, provisioning, native restore and cross-engine transfer remain later stack work" — honestly covers `dropClone`/`reapClones` (§5.4 notes the SQLite clone dies with the proposal tree) and plausibly `readOnlySession`, but it does not cover §5.3 or `capacity`/`tableExists`, which are engine-neutral seam items. Status, not bug, for the ones the body covers; for §5.3 it is an unlisted step-2 omission that the next rung will inherit silently.

Full verification complete. Here is my report.

### Findings

**1. Minor — previous review change (a) is still unaddressed: no INSERT names `engine`.** All three writers still rely on the column default.
- `packages/boot/src/cutover.ts:289` — `INSERT INTO backups(id,path,reason,bytes,taken_at,published_through,generation) VALUES(${id},${saved},'pre-flip',…)`
- `packages/boot/src/database-backup.ts:92-93`
- `packages/boot/src/database-restore.ts:336-337`

Cost: correct today only because `boot-schema.ts:139` declares `ADD COLUMN engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'sqlite'`. The moment step 3 adds a non-SQLite writer, or a future migration drops/changes that default, these three rows silently get stamped `sqlite` and then pass the very `engine !== "sqlite"` gates this PR installed — a Postgres-era pre-flip safety backup would be accepted as a SQLite restore source by `db-ops.ts:58` and copied over the live app store. The fix is three tokens per statement and is free while the column is still inert; it is the cheapest it will ever be in exactly this PR.

**2. Minor — previous review change (c) is still unaddressed: `packages/storage/src/descendant.ts` remains in this backup PR.** 6 new source lines plus a `./descendant` subpath export (`packages/storage/package.json:9`) plus 7 call sites — 5 in `packages/server/src/ext/core/topics.ts:26-69`, 2 in `packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:52,59`. 26 `substr(` sites remain unconverted in `boot/src` + `server/src`, so this is a partial migration landing inside a backup PR.

**Correction to the previous review on this point:** it said this "commits the editable app to importing `@comms/storage` as a side effect of a backup PR." That overstates it — `git diff f4f1dca..HEAD -- packages/server/package.json packages/boot/package.json` is **empty**, and both already declared `"@comms/storage": "workspace:*"` (`packages/server/package.json:20`, `packages/boot/package.json:15`) before this PR. The only new coupling is one subpath export. The scope objection stands on its own (step-3 work, partial migration); the dependency-creation part of it does not.

### Bottom line
The suite, check and build are all green at this tip with zero failures and zero flakes, CI confirms the Linux/image/QEMU acceptance the body claims, the measured source delta matches the stated one, and the previously-flagged "38 production lines" claim no longer exists in the body. Of the previous review's three required changes, **(b) is done**; **(a) and (c) are still open** and both are minor-severity as they stand. Nothing here breaks a core guarantee, and nothing claimed done is undone.
