### A-1 [major] The ledger/mirror equality check refuses instead of repairing the derived value forward, so one wrong literal makes a store that no generation can start and no in-band surface can repair

`packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:49` (ledger-design)

**Claim.** `migrate` treats `user_version` and the row count as two independent authorities and refuses startup whenever they differ (`(tables.length > 0 && applied.length !== version)`), even when the ledger is a valid, name-matching prefix that is merely one row ahead of the mirror it derives from. Because the mirror is stamped by the *caller* from a hand-written literal (`boot-schema.ts:224`, `core/schema.ts:130`) while the rows are written by `migrate` itself, the two can be committed out of step by a single authoring slip, and the resulting state is unrecoverable by fixing the source.

**Evidence.** migrations.ts:47-51 `if (applied.some((row, index) => row.migration_id !== index + 1 || row.name !== steps[index]?.name) || (tables.length > 0 && applied.length !== version)) return yield* invalid();` — while the mirror is written only by the caller: core/schema.ts:130 `if (version !== 10) yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version = 10\`;` and boot-schema.ts:224 `if (currentVersion !== 18) yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version = 18\`;`. There is no repair surface: server.ts:101-102 runs `initialize` (this validator) before `migrate(...)` and before extensions, so neither an agent-authored migration nor `api.migrate` ever executes; sql-write.ts:19 refuses any `/api/sql` statement mentioning `core_migrations` (protected-sql-tables.ts:8); boot has no SQL route at all (`grep -rln "unsafe(" packages/boot/src` returns only `source-schema.ts`), and `initializeBootSchema` failing fails the layer at boot/src/index.ts:60, so the container exits with no `/_boot`.

**Scenario.** An agent (or PR #9) appends core rung 11 to `ext/core/schema.ts` and forgets one of the three hand-written `10`s. First start: `version=10`, `applied.length=10`, names match, step 11 runs, row 11 is inserted, and `if (version !== 10)` skips the mirror write — the transaction commits with ledger=11, mirror=10. Every subsequent start: `tables.length > 0 && 11 !== 10` → `core_migrations: migration_ledger_invalid`. Correcting the source to bump all three literals does **not** help: the check still compares 11 rows against a mirror of 10. The child dies before any repair route exists, so every generation — including the previously live one — is broken, and the only escape is a boot-side restore of an older backup, which discards writes acknowledged since it. The same slip on `boot-schema.ts`'s three `18`s produces a boot.db that refuses on every existing deployment with no HTTP surface and no way back in.

**Suggestion.** Treat the mirror as derived, not as a second authority. Either have `migrate` stamp it (pass the SQLite mirror write into the helper so the row count and the marker can never be written by different code paths), or keep the caller's write and relax the check: when the ledger rows are a contiguous, name-matching prefix, repair the mirror forward to `applied.length` instead of refusing; keep the hard refusal only for `version > applied.length` with a ledger present (rungs that ran without receipts) and for the `too_new` cases. Add a fixture whose ledger is one name-matching row ahead of the mirror and assert it starts and self-heals.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed mechanically from the code. packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:47-51 is `if (applied.some((row, index) => row.migration_id !== index + 1 || row.name !== steps[index]?.name) || (tables.length > 0 && applied.length !== version)) return yield* invalid();` and the mirror is written only by the caller (packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:224 `if (currentVersion !== 18) yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version = 18\``, packages/server/src/ext/core/schema.ts:130 `if (version !== 10) yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version = 10\``). I traced the scenario step by step: with steps.length=11 and version=10, the too-new gates at migrations.ts:30 and :45 both pass, names match, `applied.length(10) !== version(10)` is false, step 11 runs, row 11 is inserted, and the caller's `!== 10` guard skips the mirror write, so the transaction commits ledger=11/mirror=10. Every later start then fails `11 !== 10` -> migration_ledger_invalid, and bumping the three literals does not help (11 rows vs mirror 10), while reverting to the 10-step image trips `applied.some(row.migration_id > steps.length)` -> migration_ledger_too_new. So the state is unreachable from any image, in either direction. Repair surfaces: packages/server/src/server.ts:101-102 runs `initialize` before `migrate` and before extensions, so nothing agent-authored ever executes; packages/server/src/kernel/sql-write.ts:19 plus protected-sql-tables.ts:8 refuse any /api/sql mentioning core_migrations; packages/boot/src/index.ts:60-63 makes initializeBootSchema a layer dependency, so on the boot side the process dies before any route is installed. Note also that the storage fixture only covers the mirror-ahead direction (packages/storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts:104-106 `mirror` mode: 1 row, user_version=2) and never the ledger-ahead direction this finding is about, which supports the suggested fixture. Major is right: it is a design defect (a derived value treated as a second authority) with an unrecoverable failure mode, but it needs an authoring slip to trigger, so it is not a blocker on its own.


### B-2 [major] The adoption fixture is synthesised by the new ladder, so it cannot detect the one divergence the design warns about

`packages/boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:43` (ledger-crash-safety)

**Claim.** Design §7.3 says of the adopt step: "This is where a mistake is expensive, because it touches a user's existing volume. Test it against a real v14 fixture, not a synthesised one", and §13 step 4's acceptance is "A fresh store builds the same schema as before, proved by comparing `sqlite_master` output before and after." The pre-ledger store in this PR is produced by running the *new* `initializeBootSchema` and then dropping the ledger, so the baseline and the code under test are the same code. Neither the old-vs-new `sqlite_master` comparison nor a checked-in fixture exists.

**Evidence.** boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:43-50: `if (mode === "legacy") { yield* initializeBootSchema; ... yield* sql\`DROP TABLE boot_migrations\`; assert.deepEqual(yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version\`, [{ user_version: 18 }]); }`. The same circular shape is used by every legacy fixture updated in this PR, e.g. boot/test/fixtures/backup-metadata.ts:12-15 (`initializeBootSchema` then `DROP TABLE boot_migrations; PRAGMA user_version=17`) and server/test/topics-http.test.ts:41 (`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS core_migrations`, `PRAGMA user_version=1`).

**Scenario.** Change the body of any rung during a future refactor of boot-schema.ts — reorder two `ALTER TABLE`s in rung 15, change a `CHECK` in rung 9, drop an index from `eventFilterSchema`. Every test stays green, because the "legacy" baseline is rebuilt with the changed code. On a real volume written by the current image, adoption stamps all 18 receipts without running anything, so the store keeps the *old* shape while the ledger asserts all 18 rungs were applied; no later rung can repair it and the divergence is permanent and silent. That is exactly the failure §7.3 told the implementer to test against a real fixture.

**Suggestion.** Either check in a binary boot.db and comms.db captured from 7d47607 (and from master's v16) and run `adopt` against copies of them, or — cheaper and version-controlled — check in the `SELECT type,name,tbl_name,sql FROM sqlite_master ORDER BY type,name` text produced by 7d47607 for a fresh boot store and a fresh app store, and assert the new ladder's fresh output equals it. For the record, I verified the equivalence out of band for this revision: extracting every `sql`-tagged literal from both revisions of boot-schema.ts and ext/core/schema.ts gives 42 and 46 literals respectively, byte-identical and in identical order, the only removals being core's eight redundant intra-transaction `PRAGMA user_version = N` writes. The property holds today; nothing in the suite protects it.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed, and the unmet acceptance text is verbatim in the design. boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:43-49 builds the "legacy v18" baseline by running the code under test (`yield* initializeBootSchema`) and then `DROP TABLE boot_migrations`, asserting uv 18; every other pre-ledger baseline in the sweep has the same shape (backup-metadata.ts:12-15, topics-http.test.ts:40-41, retired-reactions.test.ts:34, search-faults.ts:86). database-design.md:707 says "Test it against a real v14 fixture, not a synthesised one" and §13 step 4's acceptance at database-design.md:1109 requires "proved against a checked-in fixture database" plus "comparing sqlite_master output before and after". Neither exists, so this is a step-4 acceptance criterion claimed exercised by the adoption tests and not actually exercised — major is right. The divergence risk is real precisely because adoption stamps all 18 receipts without running anything, after which no rung can repair a wrong shape.


### A-3 [minor] The supported rung count is hand-copied in three places per ladder while the authority is the step array

`packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:38` (ledger-design)

**Claim.** Each ladder's length now appears four times: once implicitly as the last `id` in the `steps` array, and three times as a literal in the too-new gates and the mirror stamp. Nothing derives one from the other and nothing asserts they agree, which is exactly the slip that A-1 turns into an unrecoverable store.

**Evidence.** boot-schema.ts:38 `if (version > 18) return yield* new BootSchemaTooNew({ found: version, supported: 18 });`, :56 the same check inside the transaction, :217 `id: 18,`, :224 `if (currentVersion !== 18) yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version = 18\`;`. Core is identical: ext/core/schema.ts:17, :24, :122 (`id: 10`), :130. `migrate` already knows the real count — it uses `steps.length` for both `too_new` tests at migrations.ts:30 and :45 — but never exposes it.

**Scenario.** PR #8 does not add a rung (checked: `wt-pr8/packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts` still ends at `id: 18` with `> 18` and `!== 18`), so the first rung added after this lands is the first chance to desynchronise four places by hand. A reviewer reading a future diff sees `id: 19` added and must remember three unrelated lines, two of which are forty lines above the array and one ninety lines below it.

**Suggestion.** Bind the array first and derive everything: `const steps = [...] as const; const supported = steps.length;` then use `supported` in both gates and in the mirror stamp, or return the applied count from `migrate` and stamp from that. One binding per ladder removes the class.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed by reading: packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:38 `if (version > 18) ...supported: 18`, :56 the same inside the transaction, :217 `id: 18,`, :224 `if (currentVersion !== 18)`; packages/server/src/ext/core/schema.ts:17, :24, :122 `id: 10`, :130. `migrate` does derive the count internally (`steps.length` at migrations.ts:30 and :45) and never exposes it. The outer gate at :38 genuinely must precede the PRAGMA writes at :49-51, so it cannot simply call migrate — but hoisting `const steps = [...] as const` above and using `steps.length` removes all four copies, so the suggestion is sound. Minor on its own; it is the enabling condition for A-1, which is why it is worth taking.


### A-4 [minor] Five distinct corruption causes, one opaque message, on a code path whose only human-visible output is a dead container

`packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:8` (ledger-design)

**Claim.** `MigrationLedgerError`'s message is `${this.ledger}: ${this.code}` with only two codes, and `migration_ledger_invalid` covers five unrelated conditions: a malformed `steps` array shipped in the image, a non-table object occupying the ledger name, a non-contiguous id, a name mismatch, and a ledger/mirror length disagreement. It reports neither the offending row nor the expected and found values, on the one failure path where the operator has no HTTP surface to interrogate.

**Evidence.** migrations.ts:8-10 `override get message() { return \`${this.ledger}: ${this.code}\`; }`, with `invalid()` raised from four separate places: :23-29 (bad `steps` input or bad `version`), :34 (`tables[0].type !== "table"`), :48 (id gap or name mismatch), :49 (mirror disagreement). Contrast the sibling refusal on the same file's caller, boot-schema.ts:16-18: `return \`Boot schema ${this.found} is newer than supported schema ${this.supported}\`;`. Boot surfaces nothing else — `initializeBootSchema` is a layer dependency at boot/src/index.ts:60, so the failure kills the process before `publicRoute`/`/_boot` is installed.

**Scenario.** An operator upgrades, the container exits, and the log says `boot_migrations: migration_ledger_invalid`. They cannot tell whether the image itself is malformed (report the bug, roll back the image), whether row 9's name differs (one UPDATE on the volume fixes it), or whether the ledger has 19 rows and the mirror says 18 (one PRAGMA fixes it). All three require mounting the volume with an external sqlite3 to find out, while the board is down.

**Suggestion.** Split the code per cause — e.g. `migration_ledger_malformed_steps`, `migration_ledger_not_a_table`, `migration_ledger_incomplete`, `migration_ledger_name_mismatch`, `migration_ledger_mirror_mismatch` — or carry fields (`migration_id`, `expected`, `found`, `rows`, `mirror`) and print them, the way `BootSchemaTooNew` already does. The malformed-`steps` case is a programmer error, not store corruption, and should be `Effect.die` rather than a code that tells the operator their data is invalid.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. migrations.ts:8-10 `override get message() { return \`${this.ledger}: ${this.code}\`; }` with only two codes (`:5`), and `invalid()` is raised from four sites covering five causes: :23-29 (malformed steps or a non-integer/negative version — a programmer error in the shipped image, not store corruption, so Effect.die is the right call there), :34 (a non-table object holding the ledger name), :48 (id gap or name mismatch), :49 (mirror disagreement). No row id, expected or found value is carried, unlike the sibling refusal at boot-schema.ts:16-18 which prints found and supported. The cost is concrete and lands exactly where diagnosis is hardest: packages/boot/src/index.ts:60 makes this a layer dependency, so the container exits before publicRoute/_boot is installed. The boot fixture itself has to distinguish the causes by tag string (boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:80-86), which is evidence the single code is too coarse. Minor: diagnosability, no guarantee broken.


### A-5 [minor] Of the four histories this PR enumerates, the editable `migrations` ledger is the one left unprotected from POST /api/sql

`packages/server/src/kernel/protected-sql-tables.ts:8` (ledger-design)

**Claim.** This PR adds `core_migrations` to `kernelSqlTables` and leaves `migrations` — the agent-authored ledger it explicitly distinguishes itself from — absent, so `/api/sql` may freely `DELETE FROM migrations` or `DROP TABLE migrations`. The word-match guard is per-name, and no protected name matches the bare word.

**Evidence.** protected-sql-tables.ts:5-14 lists `kernel_writer, store_identity, core_migrations, mutation_batches, outbox, idempotency, extension_migrations, protected_sql_tables` — no `migrations`. sql-write.ts:19 tests `new RegExp(\`\\b${name}(?:\\b|_)\`, "i")` per protected name, and neither `\bcore_migrations\b` nor `\bextension_migrations\b` matches `DELETE FROM migrations`; writeShape's verb allow-list at :15 admits `DELETE` and `DROP`. The ledger itself is real (`kernel/migrations.ts:48` `Migrator.make({})({ loader, table: "migrations" })`) and `server/test/protected-sql-tables.test.ts:30-41` adds cases for `core_migrations` but none for `migrations`.

**Scenario.** A deployment has written `002_add_column.ts`. An agent runs `POST /api/sql` with `DROP TABLE migrations` — allowed, 200. Nothing breaks while the child is live, because `migrate` already ran at server.ts:102. At the next child start `Migrator` re-creates an empty ledger and replays `002_add_column.ts`, whose `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` now fails; the batch transaction rolls back and the child dies at startup, so the previously live generation can no longer start either and recovery is a boot-side restore. The default image ships no migration files (`packages/server/src/migrations/` contains only README.md), so this bites only deployments that have used the feature — i.e. the ones with the most to lose.

**Suggestion.** Add `"migrations"` to `kernelSqlTables` beside the entry this PR just added, and extend `protected-sql-tables.test.ts`'s list with `DELETE FROM migrations` / `DROP TABLE migrations`. One line, in a list this PR already edits, and it makes the README's "four histories" claim true at the boundary as well as in the schema.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. protected-sql-tables.ts:5-14 lists eight names and `migrations` is not among them; sql-write.ts:19 builds `new RegExp(\`\\b${name}(?:\\b|_)\`, 'i')` per protected name, and neither `core_migrations` nor `extension_migrations` matches the bare token in `DROP TABLE migrations`; the verb gate at :15 admits DROP and DELETE and the keyword blocklist at :16 does not mention it. The ledger is real (kernel/migrations.ts:48 `Migrator.make({})({ loader, table: 'migrations' })`) and protected-sql-tables.test.ts:30-41 adds core_migrations cases but none for `migrations`. The replay consequence is plausible and the default image ships no migration files (packages/server/src/migrations/ contains only README.md), so it bites only deployments that used the feature. Severity held at minor rather than raised: the gap pre-exists this PR (base already lacked the entry), so it is an adjacent one-line fix in a list this PR edits, not a defect this PR introduces.


### B-1 [minor] The "byte-preservation" validation claim has no corresponding test; the unchanged-marker guard is unasserted

`packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:224` (ledger-crash-safety)

**Claim.** The PR body says "the subsequent version-marker fix passes 31/31 focused cases, including byte-preservation checks". No test in this PR inspects bytes, file size, mtime, WAL state, or anything else that distinguishes "wrote the marker" from "did not write the marker". The guard that the final commit (aaf6396, "Avoid rewriting unchanged SQLite schema mirrors") exists to add is therefore untested, and so is the equivalent guard in the core store.

**Evidence.** boot-schema.ts:224 `if (currentVersion !== 18) yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version = 18\`;` and server/src/ext/core/schema.ts:130 `if (version !== 10) yield* sql\`PRAGMA user_version = 10\`;`. Every assertion that could see this compares a *logical* snapshot in which the version is identical on both sides: boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:34-42 returns `{schema, version, receipts, settings, sessions}` and boot-schema.test.ts:23-24 compares `run("fresh")` to `run("fresh")` (user_version 18 both times); storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts:14-26 returns `{schema, version, data, editable, extensions, receipts}` and :96-97 runs `initialize()` a second time and asserts `snapshot` equality (user_version 3 both times). `grep -n "byte\|\.size\|statSync\|stat(" ` over boot/test/boot-schema.test.ts, boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts, storage/test/migrations.test.ts and storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts returns nothing.

**Scenario.** Delete the `currentVersion !== 18` condition at boot-schema.ts:224 and the `version !== 10` condition at schema.ts:130, restoring the unconditional `PRAGMA user_version = N`. The full ledger and upgrade suites still pass, because no assertion can observe the difference. The regression that returns is the one base avoided with `if (version === 18) return;`: every plain restart of a steady-state board commits a page-1 write to boot.db and to the app store. On a volume sitting at the headroom floor that startup write is in the path before `headroomPolicyLayer` is built (boot/src/index.ts:20-37 builds `initialized` first), so the failure mode is boot refusing to start rather than a refused mutation.

**Suggestion.** Add one assertion that can see the write. Cheapest: in boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts's snapshot, include `stat(filename).size` plus `stat(filename + "-wal").size` (or a SHA-256 of the main file after a `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`), and assert equality across the second `adopt`/`fresh` run. Do the same for the core store in the storage fixture, which already has a two-run comparison at :96-97. Until then, drop "including byte-preservation checks" from the body.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in code and body. boot-schema.ts:224 `if (currentVersion !== 18) yield* sql`PRAGMA user_version = 18`` and ext/core/schema.ts:130 `if (version !== 10) yield* sql`PRAGMA user_version = 10`` are the whole content of the final commit, and nothing asserts them: boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:34-42 snapshots {schema,version,receipts,settings,sessions} with user_version 18 on both sides of the comparison at boot-schema.test.ts:24/31, and storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts:14-26 snapshots with user_version 3 on both sides at :96-97 — and that fixture writes `PRAGMA user_version=3` unconditionally at :52, so it does not even exercise the production guard's shape. pr7-body.md:5 does say "the subsequent version-marker fix passes 31/31 focused cases, including byte-preservation checks", and no test in the diff reads a byte, size, mtime or WAL frame. Severity lowered to minor: the guard itself is correct, the regression is hypothetical (it returns only if someone deletes the condition), and the concrete defect is an inaccurate validation sentence plus one missing assertion — both cheap to fix. One citation in the scenario is wrong: the layer ordering is boot/src/index.ts:59-76 (`initialized` at :59, `headroomPolicyLayer.pipe(Layer.provideMerge(initialized))` at :76), not :20-37, which is an import block. The ordering conclusion holds.


### B-4 [minor] The sibling `migrations` ledger is still unprotected while this PR protects `core_migrations`

`packages/server/src/kernel/protected-sql-tables.ts:8` (ledger-crash-safety)

**Claim.** This PR adds `core_migrations` to `kernelSqlTables` and tests that `/api/sql` refuses to touch it, but leaves `migrations` — the vendored `Migrator` ledger that `kernel/migrations.ts:48` creates with `table: "migrations"` — off the list. The route this PR just hardened against one ledger is still open against the other, and the design (§7.1) wanted the core rungs to live in that very table.

**Evidence.** protected-sql-tables.ts:5-14 lists `kernel_writer, store_identity, core_migrations, mutation_batches, outbox, idempotency, extension_migrations, protected_sql_tables` and no `migrations`. sql-write.ts:19 refuses a statement only when `protectedTables.some((name) => new RegExp(`\\b${name}(?:\\b|_)`, "i").test(input.sql))`, and `\bextension_migrations\b` does not match `DELETE FROM migrations`. sql-write.ts:66 computes `guarded` as the intersection of `protectedTables` with existing tables, so no `comms_sql_guard_migrations_DELETE` temp trigger is created either. kernel/migrations.ts:45-50 wraps `Migrator.make({})({ loader, table: "migrations" })` in the epoch-gated transaction.

**Scenario.** An authenticated agent posts `{"sql":"DELETE FROM migrations"}` to `/api/sql`. It is accepted and acknowledged. On the next child start, `Migrator` reads an empty ledger and re-runs every agent-authored migration from 0001; the first one containing a `CREATE TABLE` fails with "table already exists", the migration transaction aborts, and the child refuses to start — for every generation, because the failure is in the store. Recovery is a boot-side restore, the same dead end as B-3, reached through a plain API call rather than a source edit.

**Suggestion.** Add `"migrations"` to `kernelSqlTables` at protected-sql-tables.ts:8 and extend the refusal list in server/test/protected-sql-tables.test.ts:34-38 (where `"DELETE FROM core_migrations"` and `"DROP TABLE core_migrations"` were just added) with the two equivalents. One array entry; the PR is already editing this file and this test.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. protected-sql-tables.ts:5-14 lists eight names with `core_migrations` newly at :8 and no `migrations`; kernel/migrations.ts:48 is `Migrator.make({})({ loader, table: "migrations" })`; sql-write.ts:19's `new RegExp(`\\b${name}(?:\\b|_)`)` over those names does not match `DELETE FROM migrations`, and sql-write.ts:66's `guarded` intersection therefore creates no temp trigger for it either, so the indirect path is open too. Kept at minor rather than major: the gap predates this PR, and the brick outcome is conditional on the board having agent-authored migrations whose replay fails (with no `src/migrations` directory kernel/migrations.ts:13 returns `[]` and the delete is harmless). The fix is one array entry and two strings in a test the PR is already editing.


### A-6 [note] docs/database.md, the authority for this track, still specifies the superseded design in four places and names the wrong ledger set for a transfer

`docs/database.md:641` (ledger-design)

**Claim.** All four of this PR's deviations from §7 are improvements and all four match `docs/build-plan.md:159`, but the design doc is unchanged, so the authority now contradicts the implementation — the precise repeat of the previous review's ask ("three of the four deviations here are improvements on the design, and none of them reached docs/database.md"). Section 15.3's transfer procedure is additionally wrong in a way that will mislead step 8.

**Evidence.** docs/database.md:641 "the app-side ladder joins an existing ledger rather than creating one" (implementation creates `core_migrations`); :709-711 "It becomes a max-applied-id comparison" (implementation keeps `version > 18` / `version > 10` as the primary gate); :739 "after step 4 there is no `user_version` and no separate bootstrap" and :1103 "Delete the `user_version` ladders" (implementation keeps the mirror deliberately, per build-plan.md:159 "Keep `PRAGMA user_version` so older images retain their newer-schema refusal"); :1310 "The source's `boot_migrations` and `migrations` rows are compared" — a transfer must now compare `boot_migrations`, `core_migrations` and `migrations`, plus `extension_migrations` receipts. server/src/migrations/README.md:23 also still describes only the `user_version` bootstrap.

**Scenario.** Step 4's remote half, or the step 8 transfer tool, is written from §7 and §15.3 as published: it deletes the mirror that older images depend on for their newer-store refusal, looks for core rungs in the `migrations` table, and compares two ledgers instead of three — each a silent divergence from the store shape this PR has already shipped to volumes.

**Suggestion.** Amend §7.1, §7.3, §7.4, §7.6, §13 step 4 and §15.3 step 5 to describe what landed: a comms-owned `migrate` helper, four histories, real rung names rather than `legacy_vN`, the retained SQLite mirror and what it is for, and the three-ledger comparison. Add the new sentence to `packages/server/src/migrations/README.md` too, since that is where an agent authoring a migration will look for "do not touch `core_migrations`".

**Skeptic.** All five citations verified verbatim in the worktree: docs/database.md:641 'so the app-side ladder joins an existing ledger rather than creating one'; :709-711 'It becomes a max-applied-id comparison'; :739 'after step 4 there is no user_version and no separate bootstrap'; :1103 'Delete the user_version ladders at packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:38-90'; :1310 'The source\'s boot_migrations and migrations rows are compared against what step 4 applied.' docs/build-plan.md:159 does hold the contrary decisions ('Separate core and editable migration ledgers', 'Keep PRAGMA user_version so older images retain their newer-schema refusal'), so the authority and the decision log now disagree. packages/server/src/migrations/README.md:23 is also stale as quoted. The §15.3 step-5 point is the most consequential for step 8 and is correctly identified. Note, not minor: documentation only, and packages/storage/docs/README.md:11 does record the shipped behavior accurately.


### B-3 [note] A ledger shorter than the mirror refuses forever, while a missing ledger self-heals — and the short state survives rehearsal

`packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:49` (ledger-crash-safety)

**Claim.** `migrate` treats "ledger absent" as adoptable and "ledger present but shorter than the mirror" as unrecoverable corruption. Because app-owned code can empty the core ledger without making the running child unhealthy, the bad state is created during a generation that rehearsal passes and cutover accepts, and only surfaces on the next restart.

**Evidence.** migrations.ts:47-51: `if (applied.some((row, index) => row.migration_id !== index + 1 || row.name !== steps[index]?.name) || (tables.length > 0 && applied.length !== version)) return yield* invalid();` versus :52-53 `if (tables.length === 0) yield* sql\`CREATE TABLE ${sql(ledger)} (...)\`` followed by the receipt-only loop at :54-58. The write path is open: server/src/kernel/extension-migrations.ts:20-26 rejects only oversize, NUL-bearing, multi-statement or non-DML statements and never consults `kernelSqlTables`/`protectedSqlTables`, then executes `sql.unsafe(statement)` at :54 inside the writer-gated transaction. `DELETE FROM core_migrations` passes every one of those filters.

**Scenario.** An agent adds `packages/server/src/migrations/0005-cleanup.ts` (or any ext module) whose statement is `DELETE FROM core_migrations`. The candidate child starts: `initialize` runs with the ledger intact, then the extension migration commits the delete. The child is healthy, so the rehearsal copy passes and cutover accepts the generation; the previous good generation is retired. On the next restart — a deploy, a restart request, a crash — `initialize` sees `tables.length = 1`, `applied.length = 0`, `version = 10` and fails `migration_ledger_invalid`. Every generation including the retained ones fails identically, since they all read the same app store. The board is app-down until an operator runs a boot-side database restore. `DROP TABLE core_migrations` instead re-adopts cleanly on the next start, so the two corruptions of the same table have opposite outcomes.

**Suggestion.** Close the write path rather than relax the check: route `makeExtensionMigrate` through `writeShape(input, yield* protectedSqlTables(sql))` the way `writeSql` does at sql-write.ts:61-62, so neither ledger is reachable from app-authored migrations. If that is out of scope here, at minimum classify `tables.length > 0 && applied.length < version && every applied row matches its step` as adoptable (re-stamp the prefix up to `version`, running nothing) so the short state heals the same way the absent state does, and keep the hard refusal for `applied.length > version` and for name mismatches.

**Skeptic.** The code behaves as described: migrations.ts:49 `(tables.length > 0 && applied.length !== version)` → `migration_ledger_invalid` forever, versus :52 `if (tables.length === 0)` → clean re-adoption, so DELETE bricks and DROP heals. The write path is open as claimed — extension-migrations.ts:20-26 filters only size, NUL, `;|--|/*` and the leading verb, then :54 `sql.unsafe(statement)`, and never consults kernelSqlTables (server/test/fixtures/extension-migrations.ts:81-84 shows the author enumerating rejected statements, with `DELETE FROM core_migrations` not among the rejected shapes). Severity lowered to note because the hazard class is pre-existing and not introduced here: the same path already accepts `DROP TABLE topics`, which is recorded in extension_migrations and then fails ext/core/schema.ts:26's `SELECT path,parent,... FROM topics LIMIT 1` on every subsequent child start, producing the identical all-generations-down state with the identical boot-side-restore recovery. The new asymmetry is worth the one-line classification fix, not a merge condition.


### B-5 [note] Nothing enforces the SQLite-only precondition the together-commit claim rests on

`packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:13` (ledger-crash-safety)

**Claim.** `migrate` documents two preconditions it cannot check — that the caller holds one transaction, and that the dialect is SQLite — and `packages/storage/docs/README.md:11` states the together-commit property as a property of the package. The only SQLite-specific statement in the body is the `sqlite_master` probe, which is precisely the line PR #8 will branch. Nothing then makes the documented invariant false-at-compile-time or false-at-run-time on MySQL, where DDL implicitly commits.

**Evidence.** migrations.ts:13-14: `/** The caller owns one transaction around adoption, every step, receipts and the user_version mirror. SQLite only: editable and extension migration namespaces are deliberately independent. */`. migrations.ts:31: `const tables = yield* sql\`SELECT type FROM sqlite_master WHERE name=${ledger}\``. storage/docs/README.md:11: "Adoption, schema changes and the legacy `user_version` mirror commit together; mismatched or newer histories refuse startup." The design's own §14.4 item 1 records that MySQL's implicit DDL commit is what defeats this class of guarantee, and §13 step 6 is where MySQL lands.

**Scenario.** PR #8 or later adds a dialect branch at :31 (`information_schema.tables` for pg/mysql) because that is the single line that fails to compile or to run. Everything else in the function is portable SQL and keeps working. On MySQL, `CREATE TABLE boot_migrations` at :53 commits implicitly, so a SIGKILL after step 17's `ALTER TABLE` and before the receipt insert leaves a ledger that is missing its last row with the schema already changed — the `applied.length !== version` state of B-3, now reachable by a crash rather than by app code. The contract comment and the README still assert the opposite, and the SIGKILL tests at storage/test/migrations.test.ts:22-52 only ever run against SQLite.

**Suggestion.** Make the precondition executable: open the function with a dialect refusal, e.g. `yield* sql.onDialectOrElse({ orElse: () => invalid(), sqlite: () => Effect.void })` (design §17 records `onDialectOrElse` at `Statement.ts:526-533`), so a pg/mysql caller fails loudly at the one place the assumption lives. Rename it `migrateSqlite`, or keep the name and let the dialect guard carry the contract. Also state in README.md:11 that the together-commit property is the caller's transaction, not the helper's.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed as written. migrations.ts:13-14 states both preconditions as a comment, storage/docs/README.md:11 asserts "Adoption, schema changes and the legacy `user_version` mirror commit together" as a package property, and the function body contains no dialect check — the only SQLite-specific statement is the `sqlite_master` probe at :31, with everything else portable. Note is the right severity: nothing is wrong today, the scoping is disclosed honestly in three places, and the cost is a trap laid for PR #8.


### B-6 [note] Step-4 work landed inside a steps-1-to-2 stack, skipping step 3, and the ledger freezes 18 rung names that the stack has not finished renumbering

`packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:57` (ledger-crash-safety)

**Claim.** This PR is design §13 step 4 ("Both schema bootstraps to `Migrator`") landing in a stack whose stated scope is steps 1 and 2, with step 3 (`dialect.ts`, 41 `isDescendant` sites of which PR #4 routed 7) not done. It also converts the rung ladder into a name-and-id ledger, which freezes rungs 1 to 18 and their names for every deployment that starts under it — the standing review concern about rungs 17 and 18 needing a renumber if the base lands migrations first. The consequence is now loud rather than silent, which is better, but it is nowhere documented and it changes the cost of the renumber decision.

**Evidence.** boot-schema.ts:57 `yield* migrate(sql, "boot_migrations", currentVersion, [ ... ])` with ids 1..18 and names `generations` … `backup_engine` (:60, :72, :81, :98, :115, :120, :125, :130, :135, :144, :151, :156, :164, :182, :190, :204, :211, :218), pinned in the fixture's `names` array at boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:13-32. The name check is migrations.ts:48 `row.name !== steps[index]?.name` → `migration_ledger_invalid`, and `initializeBootSchema` runs inside the layer graph at boot/src/index.ts:20-24, so its failure aborts layer construction and boot does not reach the `/_boot/*` handler installed at index.ts:14-17.

**Scenario.** The base lands a boot migration and rungs 17 and 18 renumber to 18 and 19 with the ledger already shipped. A deployment that adopted the ledger under the current numbering has rows `17 store_identity, 18 backup_engine`; the new image's step 17 is the base's new rung. `applied.some((row, index) => ... row.name !== steps[index]?.name)` fires and boot refuses to start. Recovery is an image rollback — the board keeps serving on the previous image — which is a better outcome than the base's silent "rung 17 now means something else", but it means the renumber becomes a release that cannot be rolled forward without an explicit ledger-rewrite step.

**Suggestion.** Say in packages/storage/docs/README.md that a shipped `(id, name)` pair is permanent and that renaming or renumbering a rung requires an explicit ledger-rewrite migration, not a source edit. Settle the rung 17/18 renumber question before this merges, because afterwards it is a release-blocking change rather than a textual one. And land step 3 before step 4 as §13 orders it, or say in the body that the order was changed and why.

**Skeptic.** Both halves confirmed. database-design.md:1101 is "Step 4. Both schema bootstraps to `Migrator`" and :1089 is step 3 (`dialect.ts`), so this is step-4 work landing inside a steps-1-and-2 stack ahead of step 3 — the exact habit the previous review flagged. The freeze is real: boot-schema.ts:57's `migrate(sql, "boot_migrations", currentVersion, [...])` pins ids 1..18 to names, migrations.ts:48 `row.name !== steps[index]?.name` turns any later renumber into `migration_ledger_invalid`, and `initializeBootSchema` runs inside the layer graph at boot/src/index.ts:59-60 so the failure aborts layer construction. Nothing in the diff documents that a shipped (id, name) pair is permanent. Note is right — the failure is loud and rollback-recoverable, which is an improvement on base, but it changes the cost of the pending rung 17/18 renumber decision.


### Item status


#### ledger-design

PR #7 replaces both hand-written `PRAGMA user_version` ladders with named, row-recorded histories and a shared 59-line validator. Counted from code it is exactly what the body claims: boot keeps 18 identities (`boot-schema.ts` ids 1..18, names `generations`..`backup_engine`) and core keeps 10 (`ext/core/schema.ts` ids 1..10, `messages`..`mention_punctuation`), contiguous, uniquely named, with no renumber — step N runs iff `version < N`, which is exactly the base's `if (version < N)` mapping, so the standing "do not renumber rungs 17/18" concern is honored. Four histories now exist and stay separate: `boot_migrations` (boot.db, owned by the immutable image), `core_migrations` (app store, owned by the built-in core extension), `migrations` (app store, the editable TypeScript ledger via the vendored `Migrator`, `kernel/migrations.ts:48`), and `extension_migrations` (per-extension receipts keyed `(extension,name)`); each is a distinct table in a distinct namespace, so no two can collide, and the storage fixture asserts the other two ledgers are byte-for-byte untouched by an adoption. The ledger DDL at `migrations.ts:53` is the vendored `Migrator` sqlite branch verbatim (`Migrator.ts:145-150`), so these tables are wire-compatible with a later switch to `Migrator` — a good, deliberate choice. Adoption is correct and atomic: with no ledger present, rungs at or below the mirror are stamped but not run, the rest run, and everything commits with the mirror in one caller-owned transaction; the SIGKILL test proves rollback of adoption, DDL, receipts and mirror together. "An unchanged version is validated without rewriting its marker" is real and narrow: `aaf6396` only guards the `PRAGMA` write (`boot-schema.ts:224`, `core/schema.ts:130`), so a current store now does three reads inside a transaction and no write where the base returned early. Protection against `POST /api/sql` is genuinely done for the new table: `core_migrations` is in `kernelSqlTables` (`protected-sql-tables.ts:8`), and `protected-sql-tables.test.ts:37-54` proves both the direct statement and the trigger-mediated path are refused, across restart. Deviations from `docs/database.md` §7 (hand-rolled validator instead of `Migrator` modules; `core_migrations` instead of joining the `migrations` ledger; real names instead of `legacy_vN`; the `user_version` mirror retained rather than deleted) are all improvements and all match `docs/build-plan.md:159`, but none of them reached `docs/database.md`, which still specifies the superseded design in four places — the exact repeat miss the previous review called out. Two new design hazards are worth fixing before this lands: the validator demands `applied.length === version` and refuses rather than repairing the derived mirror forward (`migrations.ts:49`), which turns a one-literal authoring slip into an app or boot store that no generation can start and no in-band surface can repair; and the step *names* silently became a validated key frozen at each deployment's adoption moment, so a future rename refuses startup only on stores that adopted earlier — a break no test in this PR can catch, because both "legacy" fixtures are generated by the current code. On portability the ledger *shape* travels fine, but the invariant does not: `sqlite_master`, `PRAGMA user_version` and "adoption + DDL + receipt + mirror commit together" are all SQLite-only, and MySQL's non-transactional DDL makes the equality rule unimplementable there — the same relaxation A-1 asks for is what step 4's remote half will need. That is deferred honestly (remote URLs are still refused at `storage/src/store.ts:23`).

| status | item | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| improvement | Design §7.1: both user_version ladders become a recorded migration history | Implemented as a shared 59-line validator `packages/storage/src/migrations.ts` called from `boot/src/boot-schema.ts:57` and `server/src/ext/core/schema.ts:32`, not as vendored `Migrator` modules. The ledger DDL at `migrations.ts:53` is `Migrator.ts:145-150`'s sqlite branch verbatim (`migration_id integer PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL, created_at datetime NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp, name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL`), so a later switch to `Migrator` reads the same table. Hand-rolling buys the adoption/validation semantics the design's `Migrator` path could not express and avoids §7.1's Postgres `ensureMigrationsTable` hazard; it forgoes `Migrator`'s pg `LOCK TABLE` and `Locked` classification, which step 4's remote half will have to supply itself. |
| improvement | Design §7.1: the app-side ladder joins the existing `migrations` ledger | PR7 creates a separate `core_migrations` (`ext/core/schema.ts:32`) instead of stamping core rungs into the agent-authored `migrations` table (`kernel/migrations.ts:48`). Correct: the editable ledger's ids come from agent filenames, so sharing it would let an agent's `002_*.ts` collide with a future core rung. `docs/database.md:641` still states the superseded intent and needs the correction; `docs/build-plan.md:159` already records the four-history decision. |
| fixed | Design §7.3: one-time adopt step, no-op when already stamped, no-op on a fresh store, atomic with the ladder | `migrations.ts:52-58`: with no ledger (`tables.length === 0`) every step at or below the mirror is stamped without running (`if (step.id > version) yield* step.run`), the rest run, and the caller's single `sql.withTransaction` covers adoption, DDL, receipts and the mirror. Verified no-op on a stamped ledger (steps skipped via `step.id <= applied.length`, no inserts, mirror write skipped at `boot-schema.ts:224`), and full run on a fresh store (`version === 0`). `storage/test/migrations.test.ts:22-52` SIGKILLs mid-transaction and asserts the snapshot is unchanged and a retry succeeds. |
| partly | Design §7.3 acceptance: prove adoption against a real pre-ledger fixture, not a synthesised one | `boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:43-49` builds its "legacy v18" store by running the *current* `initializeBootSchema` and then `DROP TABLE boot_migrations`; `backup-metadata.ts:14` does the same for v17. Correct for the DDL question, but it makes the names in the ledger agree with the current code by construction, so the fixture family cannot detect a name drift between releases (see A-2). `storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts` is a genuinely independent three-step ladder and covers `empty`, `gap`, `wrong-name`, `mirror`, `newer-ledger`, `newer-version`. |
| unchanged | Design §7.4: "refuse a newer store" becomes a max-applied-id comparison | The primary refusal is still the mirror: `boot-schema.ts:38` and `:56` (`version > 18` → `BootSchemaTooNew`), `ext/core/schema.ts:17` and `:24` (`version > 10` → `app_schema_unsupported`). `migrations.ts:30,45` add a second, ledger-based `migration_ledger_too_new`. Deliberate and consistent with keeping the mirror for older-image compatibility, but `docs/database.md:709-711` still specifies the replacement. |
| improvement | Design §7.6: after step 4 there is no `user_version` | The mirror is kept and is load-bearing: it is the too-new gate, the pending-recovery range gate (`boot-schema.ts:40`), the adoption input, and a cross-check (`migrations.ts:49`). Keeping it is right — an older image reads only `user_version`, so deleting it would make a boot-image rollback unsafe, and `docs/build-plan.md:159` records exactly this decision ("Keep `PRAGMA user_version` so older images retain their newer-schema refusal"). `docs/database.md:739` and `:1103` still say the opposite. |
| deferred-honestly | Design §7.1: create each ledger with CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS before any outer transaction (the Postgres 42P01/25P02 hazard) | `migrations.ts:31` probes `sqlite_master` and `:53` creates the table *inside* the caller's transaction. SQLite-only by construction and labelled so at `migrations.ts:14`; unreachable on a remote engine because `storage/src/store.ts:23` still refuses `postgres:`/`mysql:` descriptors with `store_engine_unsupported`. The PR body lists remote schema execution as subsequent work. |
| fixed | build-plan.md:159: preserve all four migration histories independently | Four distinct tables, no shared keyspace: `boot_migrations` (boot.db), `core_migrations` (`protected-sql-tables.ts:8`), `migrations` (`kernel/migrations.ts:48`), `extension_migrations` (`kernel/extension-migrations.ts:38`, PK `(extension,name)`). `storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts:19-20,94-95` asserts the editable and extension ledgers are unchanged by an adoption; `server/test/topics-http.test.ts:51-64` asserts the ten core names after a v1→v10 upgrade. |
| fixed | Ledger row keying and cross-history collision | One row per rung keyed `migration_id integer PRIMARY KEY` within a per-history table (`migrations.ts:53`); the two new histories live in different databases, and within the app store the three tables have distinct names. An extension cannot squat the name: `extension-migrations.ts:49-53` refuses a `protect:true` CREATE whose target already exists under `COLLATE NOCASE`. |
| partly | Ledger item 48 / the mediated SQL boundary protects the ledger | `core_migrations` added to `kernelSqlTables` (`protected-sql-tables.ts:8`) and enforced by the word-match in `sql-write.ts:19` plus the temp ABORT triggers at `:70-75`; `server/test/protected-sql-tables.test.ts:37-54` proves direct statements get 501 and a trigger that deletes the ledger makes the outer write fail 400 with the ledger intact, across restart. Item 48's second half is still open upstream — `api.migrate` never consults the set (`extension-migrations.ts:54` runs any `DROP`/`DELETE`), and for this table the consequence is worse than for the others (A-1). |
| fixed | Previous review standing concern: do not renumber boot rungs; PR #3 and #4 hold 17 and 18 | Counted from code: boot ids 1..18 with rung 17 = `store_identity` (`backups.legacy_store_id`) and rung 18 = `backup_engine` (`backups.engine`), identical DDL and identical thresholds to base `7d47607:boot-schema.ts:137-139`. Core ids 1..10 likewise map one-to-one onto the base's `if (version < N)` blocks, including the preserved `if (version > 0)` guards that keep retired `reactions`/`agents` out of fresh stores (`ext/core/schema.ts:73,86`). |
| fixed | PR body: "an unchanged version is validated without rewriting its marker" | `aaf6396` guards only the mirror write: `boot-schema.ts:224` `if (currentVersion !== 18)`, `ext/core/schema.ts:130` `if (version !== 10)`. On a current store `migrate` performs three reads and no write, so no marker and no ledger row is rewritten; `boot/test/boot-schema.test.ts:21-32` compares full `sqlite_master` + `user_version` + data snapshots across repeated starts. Note the base returned before the transaction entirely (`7d47607:boot-schema.ts:51`), so a current store now opens a read-only transaction it previously skipped; under WAL that costs nothing. The body's "byte-preservation" is asserted logically, not at file level. |
| fixed | PR body: "Pending preidentity recovery still refuses before schema or WAL changes" | `boot-schema.ts:40-47` is unchanged from base and still precedes `PRAGMA auto_vacuum`/`journal_mode`/`synchronous` at `:49-51` and the transaction at `:52`; `boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts` refusal modes assert the snapshot is unchanged after every refusal. |
| fixed | Previous review ask: stop starting the next step inside the current one | The diff is confined to the two ladders, the shared validator, one protected-table entry, two README lines and tests; no dialect helpers, no pg/mysql client code, no new rung. Source delta 309/-124 matches. |

| deferred-honestly | Portability: does the ledger work on Postgres and MySQL | Shape yes, invariant no. The table matches `Migrator`'s per-dialect variants on the only column that matters (`migration_id` PK). But `migrations.ts:31` reads `sqlite_master`, `:53` emits SQLite-flavoured types, both callers read and write `PRAGMA user_version`, and the central rule `applied.length === version` (`:49`) has no remote analogue; on MySQL the `CREATE TABLE` at `:53` and every DDL rung auto-commit, so "adoption, DDL, receipts and the mirror commit together" is unavailable and a crash mid-ladder leaves a committed ledger/DDL skew that the validator can only refuse. Honest for this PR (remote descriptors are rejected upstream), but step 4's remote half needs a different rule (A-7). |


#### ledger-crash-safety

Note on setup: the worktree named in my brief did not exist (only `.../stack/wt-pr4`, a detached worktree at 7d47607, was present). I materialized the tree at aaf6396 into `.../stack/wt-pr7` with `git archive aaf6396 | tar -x`, which reads only the object store and writes nothing to the repository, and read every file from there. All citations are repo-relative paths in that tree.

From the crash-safety, upgrade and downgrade angle this PR is the strongest work in the stack, and the one thing I most expected to be wrong is right. There is exactly one commit point per store and everything is inside it: `sql.withTransaction` at boot-schema.ts:52 wraps the in-transaction version re-read, the too-new recheck, the `sqlite_master` probe, the ledger `CREATE TABLE`, every `step.run`, every receipt `INSERT`, and the `PRAGMA user_version = 18` mirror at :224; ext/core/schema.ts:20-141 has the same shape with `writerGate` first. That is not just read off the page — storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts pauses inside the transaction after the mirror write and storage/test/migrations.test.ts:29-47 SIGKILLs it for real and asserts the fresh-process snapshot, `PRAGMA user_version` included, is unchanged. So the body's together-commit claim is true on SQLite, the kill-point state space is three states and all three are classifiable with no out-of-band repair, and durability is backed by `synchronous = FULL` set before the transaction opens. The pending-preidentity-recovery refusal from PR #3 is byte-identical to base and still precedes `journal_mode = WAL` and the transaction. A newer store refuses two ways, mirror and ledger, both tested. Adoption of a v17 and a v18 store is fixtured, downgrade to PR #4's image is safe because that image returns early at 18 and never reads the ledger, and I verified mechanically that the refactor changed no DDL at all: extracting every `sql`-tagged literal from both revisions gives 42 boot and 46 core literals, byte-identical and in identical order, the only removals being core's eight redundant intra-transaction `PRAGMA user_version` writes. The 28-site fixture sweep for the new ledger is complete; the two sites not updated are correctly untouched. Three deviations from the design are improvements that the design doc should absorb: a bespoke 59-line `migrate` avoids the Postgres `regclass`-probe hazard §7.1 worries about and so can create the ledger inside the one transaction, a separate `core_migrations` avoids the id collision §7.1's "join the existing `migrations` ledger" would have caused with agent-authored migration 0001, and keeping the `user_version` mirror is what preserves downgrade to the previous image.

Two things need changing before merge, both in the tests rather than the mechanism. The body claims the version-marker fix was validated "including byte-preservation checks" and nothing in this PR reads a byte, a size, an mtime or a WAL frame; the guards at boot-schema.ts:224 and schema.ts:130 — the entire content of the final commit — can be deleted with the suite green, and the regression that returns is a page-1 write on every steady-state restart, in a code path that runs before the headroom policy layer exists. And the pre-ledger fixture is circular: boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:43-50 builds its "legacy v18" store by running the new ladder and then dropping the ledger, which is exactly what design §7.3 warned against ("Test it against a real v14 fixture, not a synthesised one"), so the suite cannot detect a divergence between the old ladder's schema and the new one — the failure where adoption stamps all eighteen receipts onto a store that has the old shape, permanently and silently. I verified out of band that no divergence exists today; nothing in the suite protects that. Beyond those, three smaller items: the ledger refuses forever when it is shorter than the mirror but self-heals when it is absent, and the short state is reachable from an app-authored migration that rehearsal cannot catch because it does not make the running child unhealthy; the sibling `migrations` ledger is still missing from `kernelSqlTables` while this PR adds `core_migrations` to it, leaving `DELETE FROM migrations` open through `/api/sql`; and the SQLite-only precondition the whole together-commit claim rests on is a comment rather than a guard, with `sqlite_master` at migrations.ts:31 the single line PR #8 will branch. Sequencing is the one genuine repeat: this is design step 4 landing inside a steps-1-and-2 stack, ahead of step 3, which is the habit the previous review asked Codex to stop.

| --- | --- | --- |
| fixed | Adoption, schema changes, receipts and the user_version mirror commit together (PR body + storage/docs/README.md:11) | The transaction boundary is boot-schema.ts:52-226 (`sql.withTransaction`) and ext/core/schema.ts:20-141. Inside it, in order: the re-read of `user_version` (:54 / :23), the too-new recheck (:56 / :24), `migrate` — which itself contains the sqlite_master probe (migrations.ts:31), the ledger `CREATE TABLE` (:53), every `step.run` and every receipt `INSERT` (:54-58) — and finally the mirror write (boot-schema.ts:224 / schema.ts:130). Nothing in the adoption path sits outside. Empirically confirmed, not just read: storage/test/fixtures/migrations.ts pauses at :53-56 with `process.stdout.write("READY\n")` followed by `Effect.never` after `PRAGMA user_version=3` and still inside `sql.withTransaction`, and storage/test/migrations.test.ts:29-47 SIGKILLs there and asserts the fresh-process snapshot — which includes `PRAGMA user_version`, `sqlite_master`, the data table and the receipts — is byte-identical to before, then that `success` still works. ### Refuted, not posted


**A-2 [major] Rung names silently became a validated key frozen at each deployment's adoption moment, with no guard, no documentation, and fixtures that cannot catch a rename** — `packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:48`. The mechanism is real (migrations.ts:48 validates names; rows are written only when `tables.length === 0` at :52, so stored names are frozen at adoption), but two load-bearing claims are false. First, name validation is not a silent drift: docs/build-plan.md:159 is the owner's governing decision and says explicitly 'validate complete contiguous IDs/names and the legacy version before adoption', so validating names is requested behavior, not an accident. Second, 'no guard' and 'every CI suite passes' are wrong. packages/boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:13-32 hard-codes all eighteen boot names and asserts the ledger equals them (`:53-56`), driven by packages/boot/test/boot-schema.test.ts:21-25; packages/server/test/topics-http.test.ts:51-64 hard-codes all ten core names after a v1->v10 upgrade. Because those stores are built by the current source, renaming a rung in source without editing the pinned list fails CI immediately — so a rename cannot reach rollout unnoticed. The boot fixture also has a dedicated `name` corruption mode (boot-schema.ts:71 `UPDATE boot_migrations SET name='wrong_name'`) asserted to refuse without touching schema, mirror or data, contradicting 'no test in this PR exercises a name that differs from the current source'. What survives is a documentation ask: nothing states the names are permanent identifiers, and packages/server/src/migrations/README.md:23 still describes only the user_version bootstrap. That is a note, and it overlaps A-6.


**A-7 [note] The ledger's central invariant is the one part that cannot travel to Postgres or MySQL, and nothing yet refuses a non-SQLite dialect with a clear error** — `packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:31`. The code facts check out (migrations.ts:31 `SELECT type FROM sqlite_master WHERE name=${ledger}`, :53 the SQLite-flavoured CREATE TABLE inside the caller's transaction, :49 the mirror equality, both callers reading PRAGMA user_version), but none of it is a defect of this PR. packages/storage/src/store.ts:23 refuses `postgres|postgresql|mysql` descriptors with store_engine_unsupported before any client exists, so no remote engine can reach this helper, and the helper's docstring at migrations.ts:13-14 states 'SQLite only' as a deliberate scope. Remote schema execution is listed as subsequent work, and by the standing rule an honestly deferred item is a status, not a bug. The dialect-guard suggestion is cheap but would be dead code today, and the substantive half of the ask — record in §7 that the mirror equality is a SQLite-only cross-check and the portable rule is prefix validation plus CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS outside the outer transaction — is already covered by A-6. Refuted as a finding; kept as a note.


### Skeptic corrections


**ledger-design.** Four rows I disagree with, all verified in the worktree.

1. "Design §7.3 acceptance: prove adoption against a real pre-ledger fixture" — status `partly` is right, but its evidence is wrong where it says the fixture family "cannot detect a name drift between releases (see A-2)". packages/boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:13-32 hard-codes all eighteen names as a literal array and asserts the ledger equals it at :53-56, and packages/server/test/topics-http.test.ts:51-64 does the same for the ten core names. Those stores are built by the current source, so a rename in source with the list unchanged fails CI. The fixture also has a dedicated name-drift mode at boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:71 (`UPDATE boot_migrations SET name='wrong_name' WHERE migration_id=9`), exercised by boot/test/boot-schema.test.ts:34-40. The row should say the synthesis weakness is confined to the *DDL* question (the pre-ledger store is produced by running the current initializeBootSchema then `DROP TABLE boot_migrations` at :44-47), not to names.

2. "Ledger item 48 / the mediated SQL boundary protects the ledger" — status `partly` is right but understates the consequence badly. The row says "api.migrate never consults the set (extension-migrations.ts:54 runs any DROP/DELETE), and for this table the consequence is worse than for the others (A-1)". It is worse than that and asymmetric: see "missed" below. This row should carry a finding, not a parenthetical.

3. "Ledger row keying and cross-history collision" — status `fixed` is right, but "An extension cannot squat the name: extension-migrations.ts:49-53 refuses a protect:true CREATE whose target already exists under COLLATE NOCASE" is narrower than stated. That existence check is gated on `options?.protect` (extension-migrations.ts:27-31 computes `table` only when `protect` is set, and :50 tests `table !== undefined`), so a plain `api.migrate("x", "CREATE TABLE ...")` without `protect` bypasses it entirely; it is only `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` semantics plus SQLite's own duplicate-name error that stop a squat there. The keying conclusion still holds.

4. "PR body: an unchanged version is validated without rewriting its marker" — status `fixed` agreed, with one correction to the evidence: the cited proof is boot/test/boot-schema.test.ts:21-25 (two `fresh` runs compared) and :27-32 (two `adopt` runs compared), not "boot/test/boot-schema.test.ts:21-32" as one assertion; the full-snapshot comparison lives in the fixture at boot/test/fixtures/boot-schema.ts:34-42. The substance (three reads, no write, on a current store) is correct.

Rows I checked and agree with as written: §7.1 improvement (hand-rolled validator, DDL byte-identical to Migrator's sqlite branch), §7.1 app-ladder improvement, §7.3 adopt/no-op/atomicity (storage/test/migrations.test.ts:22-52 is a genuine SIGKILL-at-a-pause-barrier test with a byte-compared snapshot), §7.4 unchanged, §7.6 improvement, §7.1 Postgres-hazard deferral, build-plan four-histories fixed, renumber concern fixed (boot 17=store_identity/18=backup_engine confirmed at boot-schema.ts:210-222, core ids 1..10 confirmed), "stop starting the next step" fixed, docs/database.md claimed-but-not, and portability deferred-honestly.


**ledger-crash-safety.** Three rows I would change, one I would qualify.

1. "No runtime behaviour change for a board that never upgrades" — marked `fixed`, should be `partly`, and the row's own evidence says so. Two steady-state changes ship: boot now always opens a write transaction (the base's `if (version === 18) return;` before `sql.withTransaction` at packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts:52 is gone), and on the app side `core_migrations` joins `kernelSqlTables` at packages/server/src/kernel/protected-sql-tables.ts:8, so `guarded` at packages/server/src/kernel/sql-write.ts:66 gains a table and every `/api/sql` write now creates and drops three extra temp triggers (sql-write.ts:70-75 and :86-88). Both are acceptable costs, neither is "no change".

2. "A store written by master (user_version 16, no ledger) upgrades exactly once" — marked `fixed`; I would mark it `partly`. The row itself concedes no fixture adopts at 16, and the reasoning it substitutes (ids 1-16 receipted without running, 17-18 run, second start writes nothing) is exactly the property B-2 says the suite cannot protect, because every baseline in the suite is produced by the new ladder. boot/test/app-store-identity.test.ts:299 does set uv=16 but only to reach the pending-recovery refusal. The upgrade from the one version users actually run is argued, not tested.

3. "MySQL's lack of transactional DDL..." — marked `partly`; agreed, but the row understates one thing. `migrate` also has no dialect parameter and no caller-transaction assertion (packages/storage/src/migrations.ts:15-21), so on a non-SQLite client it would fail at the `sqlite_master` probe with a raw driver error rather than `MigrationLedgerError`, i.e. not even a classifiable refusal. That is the argument for B-5's suggested guard, and it belongs in this row.

4. "Test-fixture sweep: every legacy downgrade fixture was updated" — marked `fixed`; I re-enumerated and agree. 31 `PRAGMA user_version=` sites across 20 files; every site that re-runs a ladder drops its ledger first (boot/test/fixtures/agent-presence.ts:54, backup-metadata.ts:14 and :55, child-attempts.ts:47, combined-restore-migration.ts:16, enrollment-run.ts:51, tokens-run.ts:290, boot/test/{app-store-identity.test.ts:299, edit-lock.test.ts:327, events.test.ts:148/320/350, generations.test.ts:324/347/369/409, public-paths.test.ts:191, source-tree-publication.test.ts:125}, server/test/{retired-reactions.test.ts:34, mention-read-marks.test.ts:129, topics-http.test.ts:40, fixtures/search-faults.ts:86}), and the two exceptions are correctly untouched: server/test/fixtures/extension-migrations.ts:83 is a string inside a rejected-statement list, and boot/test/fixtures/app-backup.ts:78-83 opens a hand-built store with `new Database(filename)` and runs no ladder.

Everything else I checked line by line and agree with, including the three `improvement` rows (bespoke 59-line `migrate` avoiding the regclass hazard so the ledger CREATE can sit inside the caller's transaction at migrations.ts:52-53; a separate `core_migrations` at ext/core/schema.ts:32 avoiding the id-1 collision with agent migration 0001 that §7.1's shared `migrations` table would have caused, given kernel/migrations.ts:48; and retaining the `user_version` mirror, which is what keeps downgrade to PR #4's image safe). The design doc corrections those rows propose are the right ones.


### What the skeptics found that the finders missed


**ledger-design.** The new ledger is writable through `api.migrate`, and the DELETE case permanently bricks the app store while the DROP case silently self-heals — an asymmetry nobody reported. packages/server/src/kernel/extension-migrations.ts:24 admits `DELETE` in its verb gate (`/^\s*(?:CREATE|ALTER|DROP|INSERT|UPDATE|DELETE|WITH)\b/i`) and :54 runs `yield* sql.unsafe(statement)` with no consultation of `kernelSqlTables`/`protectedSqlTables` at all — the protected set is referenced only from `sql-write.ts:19` for `/api/sql`. So an extension migration `api.migrate("x", "DELETE FROM core_migrations")` commits (extension-migrations.ts:35-57, inside its own `sql.withTransaction`). On the next child start, `migrate` sees `tables.length > 0` with `applied.length === 0` and `version === 10`, so migrations.ts:49 `(tables.length > 0 && applied.length !== version)` fires: `core_migrations: migration_ledger_invalid`, forever. By contrast `DROP TABLE core_migrations` through the same API is harmless — `tables.length === 0` takes the adoption branch at migrations.ts:52-57, stamps rungs 1..10 without running them (`if (step.id > version)` is false for all), and the store is healthy again; packages/server/test/topics-http.test.ts:40 even relies on that (`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS core_migrations`). The difference between a no-op and an unrecoverable store is one SQL verb on the same table through the same API.

Why this matters more than A-1: it needs no authoring slip. It is reachable today by the product's normal mechanism — an agent editing an extension in the hot-reloadable source — and there is no in-band repair, because packages/server/src/server.ts:101-102 runs `initialize` before `migrate(...)` and before `extensionsLayer` is built (:110-111), so a corrective extension migration can never load. The only remedy is a boot-side restore of the app store, which discards every write acknowledged since the backup — the core guarantee at stake. And it is new in this PR: before it, there was no `core_migrations` row set to empty, and `PRAGMA` was already blocked at sql-write.ts:16.

The fix is one line in the list this PR already edits plus one check: gate `extension-migrations.ts:54` on `protectedSqlTables(sql)` the way `sql-write.ts:62` does, and/or relax migrations.ts:49 so an empty-or-prefix ledger with a valid name sequence is re-adopted rather than refused — the same relaxation A-1 asks for, which would make this path self-heal like the DROP path already does.


**ledger-crash-safety.** The boot ladder has no post-adoption shape verification, while the core ladder does — so B-2's divergence is silent on the boot store and self-detecting on the app store, and the fix is a handful of lines the PR already has a template for.

packages/server/src/ext/core/schema.ts:131-138, immediately after `migrate` and the mirror write and still inside the one transaction, probes the shape it just claimed: `SELECT deleted_at FROM topics LIMIT 1`, `SELECT ns,key,value,updated_seq,previous FROM kv LIMIT 1`, `SELECT message_id,body,previous_body FROM messages_fts LIMIT 1`, `SELECT edited_at,deleted_at,updated_seq,previous FROM messages LIMIT 1`, `SELECT instance,key,kind,input_hash,outcome FROM idempotency LIMIT 1`, `SELECT archived_at FROM topics LIMIT 1`, `SELECT instance,topic,seq FROM reads LIMIT 1`, plus the pre-migrate probes at :25-30. If adoption stamps ten receipts onto a store whose shape does not match what those receipts assert, one of these SELECTs fails, the whole transaction rolls back, and the child refuses to start with the ledger never created — loud and recoverable by rolling back the image.

packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts has no equivalent. `grep -n "LIMIT 1"` over the file returns only lines 41 and 44, both inside the pre-existing pending-recovery check that runs before the transaction; after `migrate(...)` at :57-223 the transaction contains exactly one statement, `if (currentVersion !== 18) yield* sql`PRAGMA user_version = 18`` at :224, and then commits. So on the store that matters most — the one holding credentials, the seq allocator, the event log and the generation table — adoption commits eighteen receipts asserting all eighteen rungs ran, with no check that any of the eighteen rungs' tables or columns are actually present. The store then carries a permanently unrepairable lie: every future rung is gated on `step.id > version` (migrations.ts:56) or skipped by `step.id <= applied.length` (:55), so nothing ever revisits rungs 1-18, and the first symptom is a runtime query failure in `events`, `backups` or `cutover` long after the adoption that caused it.

Concrete failure: a deployment running a hand-patched or partially-restored boot.db at uv 18 that is missing `backups.engine` (rung 18's column, boot-schema.ts:218-221). Today it fails loudly and immediately at the first `backups` query; after this PR it adopts cleanly, reports a complete eighteen-rung history, and keeps failing at the same runtime query with the ledger now asserting the column was added. The cheap fix is to mirror the core pattern: after the mirror write at :224, add the boot equivalents of the core probes — one `SELECT` per rung-added column or table that later code depends on, `backups.engine` and `backups.legacy_store_id` first since they are the two newest rungs and the ones a pre-ledger store is most likely to be missing. That also gives the adoption path the shape check §13 step 4's acceptance asks for in a form the existing circular fixture can still exercise, which is why it is worth doing even if the checked-in fixture from B-2 lands.


### Claims check

All checks complete. Findings below.

Green on every run.

Passes.

## One code observation worth the PR author's attention (not a test-claim issue)

`packages/boot/src/boot-schema.ts` removes the base's `if (version === 18) return;` early exit, so **every** boot start now opens a write transaction against the boot store and re-reads `PRAGMA user_version` inside it. That is required for adoption of a pre-ledger v18 store, and the in-transaction re-read plus `BootSchemaTooNew` recheck is a genuine improvement over the base (it closes a TOCTOU between the outer version read and the migration). I flag it only because the steady-state cost moved from "no transaction" to "one transaction plus two queries" on every start, and no test measures that. The design doc does not need a correction for this; it is the in-transaction recheck the doc's §12.4 asks for.
