### A-1 [major] Published-image CTE drops the §6.10 `ELSE tags::text` casts, so every Postgres read fails to parse

`packages/server/src/ext/core/published-messages.ts:10` (dialect-and-queries)

**Claim.** The pg emission of the published-image CASE mixes a `text` THEN branch with a `jsonb` ELSE branch for `tags` and `meta`, which Postgres rejects with 42804. Design §6.10 names the exact fix and the implementation omits it.

**Evidence.** published-messages.ts:10-11 — `CASE WHEN updated_seq>${ceiling} THEN ${jsonText(sql, sql("previous"), "tags")} ELSE tags END AS tags` (same shape for `meta`), and published-topics.ts:7 for `topics.meta`. Compiled with the real pg compiler this is `CASE WHEN updated_seq>$3 THEN ("previous"::jsonb ->> $4) ELSE tags END AS tags`. docs/database.md:620-626 prescribes `CASE WHEN … THEN previous->>'tags' ELSE tags::text END` and says why: "`tags::text` on the other branch produces JSON text too, so the branches agree, which is also why `messages.tags` … must be `jsonb` on Postgres"; docs/database.md:658 makes `messages.tags`, `messages.meta` and `topics.meta` `jsonb`. `body` (line 9) and the two `jsonInt` columns (lines 12-13, cast `::bigint`) are already consistent, so only these three columns are wrong.

**Scenario.** Move a board to Postgres with the schema the design mandates. `publishedMessages`/`publishedTopics` are composed into every read — `GET /api/messages`, `GET /api/topics/<path>`, page visibility (pages.ts:48/101), `public-page-policy.ts:45`. The first such query fails at parse with `ERROR: CASE types jsonb and text cannot be matched`, so the board serves no reads at all. Choosing `text` columns instead to dodge it silently abandons §7.2's jsonb requirement (and the jsonb index story) while leaving the decoders working by luck. Nothing in the tree would catch either outcome: no test executes a core query against Postgres.

**Suggestion.** Emit the ELSE branch through the same helper discipline: add a `jsonColumn(sql, column)` that is the bare identifier on SQLite/MySQL and `${column}::text` on pg, and use it for the `tags`/`meta` ELSE branches in published-messages.ts:10-11 and published-topics.ts:7. Add a compiled-SQL assertion per dialect for the two CTEs (dialect.test.ts already has the harness) so the cast cannot be dropped again before step 5 has a live Postgres to catch it.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed in the worktree. published-messages.ts:10-11 emit `CASE WHEN updated_seq>${ceiling} THEN ${jsonText(sql, sql("previous"), "tags")} ELSE tags END AS tags` (and the same for meta); published-topics.ts:7 repeats it for topics.meta. dialect.ts:41 makes the pg THEN branch `("previous"::jsonb ->> $n)` i.e. text, while the ELSE branch is the bare column, which design §7.2 (database-design.md:658 table row "JSON … messages.tags, messages.meta, topics.meta → jsonb") requires to be jsonb, and §6.10 (database-design.md:621-626) spells out the fix verbatim (`ELSE tags::text`, `ELSE meta::text`) and the reason. body (line 9, text/text) and the two jsonInt columns (::bigint/bigint) are consistent, so exactly the three columns named are wrong. Corroborating evidence that the author knew the CTE output type is unsettled: public-page-policy.ts:46 defensively writes `meta::jsonb` on the CTE's meta column. Severity stays major: §6.10's cast discipline is a named step-3 deliverable (database-design.md:1091) in the exact queries this PR rewrites, the failure is in the CTE every read composes, and nothing in the tree executes a core query off SQLite so it cannot be caught. It is latent rather than live only because the pg DDL does not exist yet (body defers "distinct-role schema initialization"), which is why it is major and not blocker.


### A-2 [major] `readTransaction` skips the one transaction every message and topic read runs inside

`packages/server/src/kernel/read-snapshot.ts:30` (dialect-and-queries)

**Claim.** The new read-isolation helper is routed only at the two pages.ts sites; the shared read-snapshot wrapper and the extension-data read still open bare `sql.withTransaction`, so on Postgres the principal read paths keep READ COMMITTED and lose the single snapshot the published-image rule depends on.

**Evidence.** read-snapshot.ts:29-32 — `const snapshot = sql\n.withTransaction(\n  Effect.gen(function* () {\n    yield* sql`SELECT epoch FROM kernel_writer`;` — unchanged by this PR, and `makeReadSnapshot` is the `read` used by publication.ts:32 for messages, topics and extension capabilities (topics.ts:20's own `withTransaction` is nested inside it, so it becomes a SAVEPOINT and correctly takes no prelude). extension-data.ts:77-80 is a second unrouted top-level read transaction. Only pages.ts:39 and :91 use `readTransaction` (dialect.ts:103-118). docs/database.md:843-846 lists seven such sites and states the consequence: "A missing prelude is silent. One read path that forgets `readTransaction` loses cross-statement consistency with no error and no failing test", and requires "a compiled-SQL assertion that each of the seven sites opens with the prelude" — there is no such test.

**Scenario.** On Postgres, `GET /api/topics/<path>` opens BEGIN at READ COMMITTED. Inside it topics.ts:24 runs the `visible_topics`/`visible_messages` rollup and then topics.ts:33 runs a second statement, `SELECT path FROM visible_topics WHERE deleted_at IS NOT NULL`. A mutation committing between the two statements (the live writer, or a retained generation's relay) gives each statement its own snapshot: a topic can be listed with an unread count computed from the first snapshot and simultaneously appear in the deleted set from the second, or be filtered out of the list while its messages were already counted into an ancestor's rollup. No error is raised and no test fails.

**Suggestion.** Route read-snapshot.ts:30 and extension-data.ts:77 through `readTransaction` in this PR — it is a two-line change each and it is the site that matters — and add the §9.2 prelude-presence assertion over the read sites so a future read path cannot forget it. If routing read-snapshot.ts is deliberately deferred (it is the one wrapper with the poison/ownership machinery around it), say so in the body, because as it stands the helper is routed at the two least-used of the five sites.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. readTransaction has exactly two production call sites, pages.ts:39 and pages.ts:91 (grep over packages: only those plus dialect.ts:103 and dialect.test.ts). read-snapshot.ts:29-32 still opens `sql.withTransaction(...)` with the `SELECT epoch FROM kernel_writer` snapshot select inside, and makeReadSnapshot is the `read` handed to every core reader (publication.ts:34 `const read = makeReadSnapshot(...)`), so topics.ts:20's own withTransaction nests inside it as a SAVEPOINT and correctly takes no prelude — which means the only place the pg prelude could be issued for messages/topics/extension reads is read-snapshot.ts:30, and it is not. extension-data.ts:77-80 is a second unrouted top-level read transaction with the same snapshot select. Design §9.2 (database-design.md:834-858) names the seven sites, states the prelude must be the first statement, and says a missing prelude is silent with no failing test; it also requires a compiled-SQL assertion per site, which does not exist (dialect.test.ts only proves the helper itself emits/skips the prelude). Major: a named step-3 deliverable half-landed at the two least important sites, with a silent wrong-answer failure mode on Postgres.


### B-4 [major] readTransaction is routed at 2 of 7 sites and misses the one that sets isolation for every read

`packages/server/src/kernel/read-snapshot.ts:30` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The helper the design calls "the subtlest thing in the design" is merged and unit-tested, but the transaction that every message and topic read actually runs inside is still a bare sql.withTransaction, and so is topics.ts's four-statement snapshot in a file this PR rewrote. On Postgres that means no read path gets REPEATABLE READ, and the two sites that were converted are precisely the ones whose nesting check makes them skip the prelude when reached through the read path.

**Evidence.** read-snapshot.ts:29-36 is `const snapshot = sql.withTransaction(Effect.gen(function* () { yield* sql\`SELECT epoch FROM kernel_writer\`; const ceiling = (yield* fence).published_through; ... return yield* read(ceiling).pipe(Effect.provideService(ReadFence, ceiling)); }))` — the whole read body runs inside it. packages/server/src/ext/core/topics.ts:20-22 is `return yield* sql.withTransaction(Effect.gen(function* () { yield* sql\`SELECT epoch FROM kernel_writer\`; ...` and then issues four statements in that one transaction: `rows` (:24-33), `deletedTopics` (:34), `archivedTopics` (:41). topics.ts:1 already reads `import { isDescendant } from "@comms/storage/dialect";` — the PR added isDescendant to this exact file and not readTransaction two lines below. The only readTransaction call sites in the tree are pages.ts:39 and pages.ts:91 (grep over boot/src and server/src). Design §6.9 lists 7 sites and §9.2 names them: messages.ts:179 and :243, pages.ts:38 and :88, topics.ts:40, extension-data.ts:63, extension-capabilities.ts:74. Design §9.2: "What the rule actually needs is a consistent snapshot, not a lock."

**Scenario.** On Postgres, topics.ts:20's transaction runs at READ COMMITTED, so its four statements see four different snapshots. A topic.deleted mutation committing between the `rows` query at :24 and the `archivedTopics` query at :41 lets `rows` return a topic with an unread count computed as if it were visible while `archivedTopics` then hides it, or lets `detail` answer topic_not_found for a path `rows` just returned — a silently wrong topic list and unread count, not an error. Worse, read-snapshot.ts:30 is the transaction every message read and every extension read runs inside, so converting pages.ts alone achieves nothing: when pages.visible is called through read(), dialect.ts:105's nesting check correctly finds the enclosing transaction and skips the prelude, so the effective number of read paths getting REPEATABLE READ on Postgres is zero. This is the invariant §9.2 says the whole lock-free argument rests on.

**Suggestion.** Route read-snapshot.ts:30 and topics.ts:20 through readTransaction in this PR — they are the two sites that decide the property, and topics.ts is already open in this diff. Then add a generated-SQL test that renders one full read path under the pg compiler and asserts SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ is the first statement after BEGIN, so the remaining four sites cannot be forgotten silently. Also implement the MySQL side of dialect.ts:112-113 (assert @@transaction_isolation at client startup) rather than leaving it as a comment, since on MySQL the helper emits nothing at all.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed, with one sub-claim corrected. readTransaction exists at dialect.ts:103-117 with the nesting check, and its only production call sites are pages.ts:39 and :91; read-snapshot.ts:30 is a bare `sql.withTransaction` and is the transaction every message/topic/extension read runs inside, so on Postgres no read path would get REPEATABLE READ and the two converted sites skip their prelude when reached through read() (dialect.ts:105). That is the decisive defect and it is real. The topics.ts:20 half of the scenario is wrong, though: topics.detail runs inside read(...) (topics.ts:16-20), so its withTransaction is nested and would inherit the outer isolation — its four statements cannot see four snapshots once read-snapshot is fixed. Kept at major because it is the property §9.2's lock-free read argument rests on, the helper landed in this PR, and nothing in the PR body flags 2-of-7 routing.


### B-5 [major] §6.10's per-column cast discipline is missing from the published-image CASE columns, and no test renders any rewritten core query off SQLite

`packages/server/src/ext/core/published-messages.ts:10` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The design gives an explicit per-column table for these five CASE columns and says this is "the place a careless port breaks silently". The port routes the THEN branch through jsonText/jsonInt and leaves the ELSE branch as the bare column, so the two branches do not agree in type on Postgres. Nothing can catch it, because no test in the PR compiles any rewritten core query under a pg or mysql compiler.

**Evidence.** published-messages.ts:10 is `CASE WHEN updated_seq>${ceiling} THEN ${jsonText(sql, sql("previous"), "tags")} ELSE tags END AS tags` and :11 is the same for meta; published-topics.ts:7 is the same for meta. dialect.ts:41's pg branch emits `(${column}::jsonb ->> ${key})`, which is text. Design §6.10's table requires `CASE WHEN … THEN previous->>'tags' ELSE tags::text END` and says why: "`tags::text` on the other branch produces JSON text too, so the branches agree, which is also why `messages.tags` (and `mentions`, `previous_mentions`) must be `jsonb` on Postgres (section 7.2)". The decoder at messages.ts:21 is `tags: Schema.fromJsonString(Schema.Array(Schema.String))`, which needs JSON text, not a parsed object. packages/storage/test/dialect.test.ts exercises the helpers in isolation only; `grep -rln 'publishedMessages|publishedTopics'` over packages/server/test and packages/storage/test returns nothing, and the only server tests mentioning a pg dialect are three unrelated fixtures.

**Scenario.** When step 4 creates the Postgres schema with messages.tags as jsonb, as §7.2 mandates, publishedMessages renders `CASE WHEN updated_seq>$1 THEN ("previous"::jsonb ->> $2) ELSE tags END` — text in one branch, jsonb in the other. Postgres has no implicit jsonb-to-text coercion (select_common_type resolves at COERCION_IMPLICIT, and find_coercion_pathway only allows the I/O route at COERCION_ASSIGNMENT or above), so the statement fails at parse with 42804 "CASE types jsonb and text cannot be matched" — and publishedMessages/publishedTopics are composed by every read path, so the whole board fails to serve. If step 4 instead makes the column text to dodge this, the `::jsonb` casts dialect.ts:41 and :60 add mean every read re-parses the JSON document per row. Either way the decision is being made implicitly by an omitted cast rather than explicitly.

**Suggestion.** Apply §6.10's table: cast the ELSE branch per column (`tags::text`, `meta::text` on pg) inside a dialect helper so the rule lives in one place, and add a test that compiles publishedMessages and publishedTopics under the pg and mysql compilers and snapshots the text — the same shape as dialect.test.ts:24-51, which already builds standalone compilers for all three dialects, so the machinery is present and costs nothing to reuse.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed exactly as written. published-messages.ts:10-11 and published-topics.ts:7 render `THEN ${jsonText(...)} ELSE tags END` / `ELSE meta END` with a bare column, while dialect.ts:41's pg branch emits `(${column}::jsonb ->> ${key})` (text). Design §6.10 (database-design.md:612-626) gives the explicit rule `CASE WHEN … THEN previous->>'tags' ELSE tags::text END` and states that `tags::text` is what makes the branches agree and why the column must be jsonb, and calls this 'the place a careless port breaks silently'. The decoder needs JSON text (messages.ts `Schema.fromJsonString`). No test compiles publishedMessages/publishedTopics under a pg or mysql compiler (dialect.test.ts builds per-dialect compilers but only for the helpers), so nothing catches it. This is the one verified portability defect in the code this PR rewrote.


### A-4 [minor] No `upsert` helper: the MySQL branch is copied per call site and two app-side upserts have no MySQL form at all

`packages/server/src/ext/core/read-marks.ts:14` (dialect-and-queries)

**Claim.** Design §6.5's `upsert` was not built; the two core sites inline their own three-branch `on(...)`, and the remaining app-side `ON CONFLICT` statements — including the extension kv writer — still emit syntax MySQL cannot parse.

**Evidence.** read-marks.ts:14 inlines `${on(sql, { sqlite: … ON CONFLICT(instance,topic) DO UPDATE SET seq=excluded.seq WHERE reads.seq<excluded.seq, pg: <the same string again>, mysql: … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE seq=GREATEST(seq,${input.seq}) })}`, and topic-move.ts:119 and :126-133 repeat the pattern. Unrouted app-side sites: kernel/extension-data.ts:70 `INSERT INTO kv(...) ON CONFLICT(ns,key) DO UPDATE SET previous=kv.value,…` and ext/system.ts:50 `INSERT INTO system_cursor(id,seq) … ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE SET seq=excluded.seq`, plus protected-sql-tables.ts:24 `INSERT OR IGNORE`. docs/database.md:545-557 specifies the helper, the `AS new` row-alias form, and that an empty set must become `ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE <col>=<col>` rather than `INSERT IGNORE` — topic-move.ts:119's hand-rolled `ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE path=path` does follow that rule.

**Scenario.** On MySQL, any agent writing extension kv state (`extension-data.ts:70`) or the `system` extension advancing its cursor (`ext/system.ts:50`) hits a syntax error at `ON CONFLICT`, inside a mutation that has already reserved a sequence range — so the failure lands in the mutation/abort path rather than at startup. Meanwhile the duplicated sqlite/pg strings in read-marks.ts:14 are a copy-paste divergence waiting to happen: the two branches are byte-identical today and nothing asserts they stay so.

**Suggestion.** Build §6.5's `upsert` (or at minimum a `conflictDoNothing`/`conflictUpdate` pair) and route the five app-side sites in this PR's blast radius; boot's can wait for its own step. Collapse read-marks.ts:14's identical sqlite/pg branches into one `on` default so a future edit cannot change only one of them.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. dialect.ts exports no `upsert` (grep for upsert across packages/ matches nothing outside this finding's description). read-marks.ts:14 inlines a three-branch `on(...)` whose sqlite and pg strings are byte-identical, and topic-move.ts:119 and :126-133 repeat the pattern. App-side sites with no MySQL form remain: kernel/extension-data.ts (`ON CONFLICT(ns,key) DO UPDATE`), ext/system.ts (`ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE`), kernel/protected-sql-tables.ts (`INSERT OR IGNORE`), plus the boot list. Design step 3 lists `upsert` (8 sites) explicitly (database-design.md:1091) and the PR body does not list it as deferred. Minor is right: MySQL cannot be selected yet (store.ts:38 refuses `postgres:`/`mysql:` with store_engine_unsupported), so the cost today is duplication plus an unrecorded gap.


### B-1 [minor] redactUrls is still absent while render() is widened to carry a credential into the child environment

`packages/storage/src/store.ts:30` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The design names redactUrls as the mechanism that keeps a connection URL out of child stderr, out of generations.stderr and out of edit-failure bodies, and applies it at the supervisor and cutover redaction sites. It does not exist anywhere in the tree, and this is the PR that creates the credential-bearing descriptor and widens render() to return it — yet the PR body's deferral list does not mention it.

**Evidence.** store.ts:30-33 is now `export const render = (store: Store): Redacted.Redacted<string> => store._tag === "file" ? Redacted.make(...) : store.url;` — the remote branch returns the full `scheme://user:password@host/db`. packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:149 unwraps it: `APP_STORE: Redacted.value(render(store))`. `grep -rn redactUrls` over packages and scripts in the worktree returns zero occurrences. Every stderr and cause path still redacts with redactHex only: supervisor.ts:111 `error: redactHex(Cause.pretty(cause))`, :112 `stderr: redactHex(state.stderr)`, :324 `generations.failed(generation.n, redactHex(String(result.failure)), stderr, attempt)`, :370, :382, and cutover.ts:356, :360. redactHex is `text.replace(/[a-f0-9]{64}/g, "[redacted]")` (packages/boot/src/auth-primitives.ts:50), which does nothing to a connection string. Design doc line 310: "`redactUrls` is applied at both sites before anything is stored or returned ... section 12.3 item 8 is the test that a wrong-password start produces a failure body with no password in it."

**Scenario.** Today nothing leaks, because supervisor.ts:116 still types its parameter `store: FileStore` and childStore() rejects postgres:/mysql: (store.ts:38). The whole exposure is one line away: the moment AppRecovery.store can hold a RemoteStore, a child started against a wrong-password Postgres dies printing the DSN, supervisor.ts:112 stores that stderr tail in generations.stderr through redactHex, and SPEC.md §7.1 invariant 5 then returns it in the edit-failure body that agents read. The password is persisted in the boot store and served over HTTP. Because the PR body does not list redactUrls as remaining work, the integration PR has no prompt to add it.

**Suggestion.** Add `redactUrls` to packages/storage/src/store.ts in this PR and apply it at supervisor.ts:111-112, :324, :370, :382 and cutover.ts:356, :360 before anything is stored or returned — it is six call sites and one regex, and it is cheap now and a credential disclosure later. If it must wait, name it explicitly in the PR body's remaining-work paragraph alongside "surviving connection guardians", so the integration PR inherits it.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed factually: grep for redactUrls over packages and scripts in the worktree returns 0; design-doc §4.2's Store API block (database-design.md:290-294) lists `redactUrls` immediately after `render`, and this PR is the one that widens render to the remote branch (store.ts:30-33, diff vs aaf6396 changes `store: FileStore` to `store: Store`). Boot still redacts with redactHex only (auth-primitives.ts:50 `/[a-f0-9]{64}/g`). But the exposure is fully gated: supervisor.ts:116 types `store: FileStore`, app-recovery.ts:105 constructs `const store: FileStore`, and childStore/parse reject postgres:/mysql: (store.ts:38), so no credential can reach stderr today. The PR body does say remote startup and ownership 'are being integrated separately', which is where the redaction sites live, so this is an unnamed-but-plausibly-covered deferral rather than a claimed-done-not-done. Also: the finder's site list is incomplete — proxy.ts:240 (`stderr: redactHex(state.stderr)` served over HTTP), events.ts:301-302, preparation-process.ts:84 and supervisor.ts:257 are the same class of sink.


### B-2 [minor] RemoteConnection.tls is a bare boolean with no producer, and the descriptor grammar cannot express TLS at all

`packages/storage/src/remote-session.ts:19` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** This is the first code in the tree that sends a database credential over a socket, and there is no expressible way to require verified TLS against a private or internal CA. The descriptor rejects every query parameter, so sslmode cannot be carried; RemoteConnection.tls is boolean-only, so no CA bundle, client certificate or servername can be supplied; and the design document never mentions TLS.

**Evidence.** remote-session.ts:19 `readonly tls: boolean;` with no producer anywhere — nothing converts a RemoteStore into a RemoteConnection (grep for RemoteConnection outside remote-*.ts and the test fixture returns nothing). remote-driver.ts:22 passes it straight through as `ssl: options.tls`, and :26 as `...(options.tls ? { ssl: { rejectUnauthorized: true } } : {})`. store.ts:77 rejects any raw containing `?` or `#`: `if (!match || /[?#\\\x00-\x20\x7f]/.test(raw)) throw new Error();`, and packages/storage/test/store.test.ts:80 pins that: `"postgres://app:secret@db/a?schema=app"` must fail store_descriptor_invalid. Design §4.1: "Any query parameter is rejected." `grep -i 'tls|ssl|certificate'` over the 1,421-line design doc returns exactly one hit, line 463, and it is about PGSSLMODE in a pg_dump child environment, not about the client.

**Scenario.** An operator points a deployment at a managed or internal Postgres whose server certificate is signed by a private CA: `DATABASE_URL=postgres://app:pw@db:5432/comms_app`. With tls true, repos/effect/packages/sql/pg/src/PgConnection.ts:1838-1842 calls Tls.connect({ host, socket }) with no ca and node's default rejectUnauthorized, so the handshake fails against the system trust store and the board cannot start; there is no descriptor syntax and no config key to add the CA. With tls false, PgConnection.ts:1830 skips the SSLRequest entirely and every row of board content crosses the network in cleartext (SCRAM protects the password exchange, MySQL's default gives not even that). Both outcomes are wrong for the deployment shape §3.2 describes.

**Suggestion.** Decide the TLS contract in the design doc (§3.2, §3.3 and §4.1) before the integration PR, then implement it here: either carve out an explicit allowlist of descriptor query parameters (sslmode at minimum) against §4.1's blanket rejection, or define out-of-band environment keys (DATABASE_TLS, DATABASE_CA_FILE) and widen RemoteConnection.tls from boolean to the ConnectionOptions shape both drivers already accept. Either way the default for a remote descriptor should be verified TLS, not tls: false.

**Skeptic.** Mechanics confirmed: remote-session.ts:19 `readonly tls: boolean` with no producer (only test/fixtures/remote-sessions.ts:26 builds a RemoteConnection, with `tls: false`); remote-driver.ts:22 `ssl: options.tls` and :26 `...(options.tls ? { ssl: { rejectUnauthorized: true } } : {})`; store.ts:76-77 rejects any `?`/`#` so no sslmode can be carried; vendored PgConnection.ts:2063-2068 does `Tls.connect({ host, ...(typeof config.ssl === 'object' ? config.ssl : {}), socket })`, so `ssl: true` means system-trust verification with no way to add a CA, and PgConnection.ts:2075 skips SSLRequest entirely when ssl is false. Only one tls/ssl hit in the 1,421-line design (line 463, PGSSLMODE for pg_dump). So the gap is real — but it is a gap in the design document that this PR faithfully implements (§4.1 mandates rejecting query parameters), the field has no production producer, and the operator-facing contract belongs to the startup PR. Downgraded: a design-contract hole to settle, not a defect in shipped behaviour.


### B-3 [minor] Every statement costs a fresh lease, two metadata round trips and a globally serialized durable registration through one pinned connection

`packages/storage/src/remote-client.ts:51` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The guarded acquirer runs per statement, not per connection, and each run does an identify() round trip on the leased connection plus an identify() round trip on the inspector's single pinned connection plus the caller's durable persist, all three of the latter serialized process-wide behind a semaphore of 1. This per-statement cost appears nowhere in the design document and is not in §9.4's freeze-budget arithmetic, and the mechanism itself has no design-doc entry.

**Evidence.** remote-client.ts:51-65 is the acquirer body: `const tag = yield* attemptTag(options); const raw = yield* open(...)` then per acquisition `const connection = yield* Scope.provide(raw.reserve, lease); const session = yield* identify(connection, options.connection, tag); yield* inspector.register(session, options.register(session)).pipe(Effect.interruptible, Effect.timeout("5 seconds"))`. In repos/effect/packages/effect/src/unstable/sql/SqlClient.ts:163-168, `getConnection` falls back to `options.acquirer` whenever there is no enclosing transaction, and remote-client.ts:77 passes no `borrower`, so a plain statement acquires a fresh lease. remote-inspector.ts:34 wraps register in `admission.withPermit(...)` where `admission` is `Semaphore.make(1)` (:29), and inside it :46 runs `const current = yield* identify(connection, options.connection, inspectorTag)` on the one connection pinned at :25 (`const connection = yield* raw.reserve`), with the caller's `persist` executed at :51 inside the same permit. remote-driver.ts:18 hardcodes `maxConnections: 4`. packages/storage/test/fixtures/remote-sessions.ts:162 confirms the shape: `assert((yield* Ref.get(registered)).length >= 5)` after five statements. RemoteClientOptions' own contract (remote-client.ts:18) says persist "Must durably acknowledge through independent storage/IPC, never this client's pool" — in the fixture that is an appendFile plus an fsync (:90-98); in production it can only be an HTTP call to boot plus a boot-store commit. `grep -nw lease` over the 1,421-line design doc returns nothing.

**Scenario.** A board page read runs on the order of twenty statements through kernel/read-snapshot.ts. On a remote engine each one pays: a pool reserve, one identify round trip on the leased connection, one identify round trip on the inspector's pinned connection, and one durable registration — and the last two are globally serialized, so the process's ceiling for all work combined is roughly one statement per inspector round trip. At a 1 ms RTT that is ~1000 statements/second for the entire child, before persist; if persist is an HTTP call to boot plus an fsync it is a few hundred. Inside a mutation this lands in §9.4's freeze budget, which the design already warns "Four admitted mutations at 2.5 s each exceed the budget" — and nothing in §9.4 accounts for three extra round trips and an fsync per statement. Separately, the inspector deliberately never reconnects ("A server restart breaks it; no pooled reacquisition can turn a new empty server into proof", remote-inspector.ts:23-24), so one transient TCP drop on that single connection makes every subsequent statement in the child fail remote_inspection_failed until boot restarts it — correct for the proof property, but the blast radius is the whole board.

**Suggestion.** Write this mechanism into the design doc as its own section and add its cost to §9.4 before wiring it, so the freeze-budget rule is computed against the real per-mutation latency. Then reduce the per-statement cost: register per physical connection rather than per lease (cache the registration keyed on connectionId so a recycled pooled connection is not re-registered), take the inspector's liveness check off the admission critical path, and derive maxConnections from the mutation gate and request concurrency instead of hardcoding 4 at remote-driver.ts:18. The PR body's "surviving connection guardians" already covers the no-reconnect half honestly; the cost half is not mentioned anywhere.

**Skeptic.** Half-confirmed, and the headline is overstated. The acquirer fallback is real (SqlClient.ts:163-168 `onNone: () => options.acquirer`; Statement.ts:1322 `Effect.scoped(Effect.flatMap(this.acquirer, ...))`), and remote-client.ts:77 passes no borrower, so a statement outside a transaction does pay a fresh lease + identify + inspector identify + caller persist, with the last two serialized under remote-inspector.ts:29,:34 `Semaphore.make(1)` and the pool hardcoded at remote-driver.ts:18 `maxConnections: 4`. But the scenario 'a board page read runs on the order of twenty statements... each one pays' is wrong: every read goes through kernel/read-snapshot.ts:30 `sql.withTransaction`, and inside a transaction getConnection resolves from transactionService, so the whole read body shares one lease and one registration. The real cost is per transaction (plus per stray statement), not per statement. The design-doc omission is verified (`grep -nw lease` over database-design.md returns nothing) and the §9.4 budget genuinely has no entry for it. Real as a cost/documentation gap, not a bug.


### B-7 [minor] assertNoSessions is single-shot but needs a polling budget the interface does not provide

`packages/storage/src/remote-inspector.ts:69` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** assertNoSessions is documented as the positive closure proof, but it observes server state once with no retry budget, and the registered-connection check fails while a just-released pooled connection is still listed by the server. The PR's own fixture needs a 100-iteration, 50 ms poll to get a success, which a production caller will not have.

**Evidence.** remote-inspector.ts:56-81 is one pass: `if ((yield* sessions(connection, options.connection.engine, tag)).length !== 0) return yield* failure("remote_sessions_open"); const ids = yield* Ref.get(registered); if ((yield* connectionIds(connection, options.connection.engine)).some(([id]) => ids.has(id))) return yield* failure("remote_sessions_open");` with no retry. The fixture at packages/storage/test/fixtures/remote-sessions.ts:247-253 works around it: `let closed = false; for (let index = 0; index < 100; index++) { closed = Exit.isSuccess(yield* inspector.assertNoSessions(Effect.void).pipe(Effect.exit)); if (closed) break; yield* Effect.sleep("50 millis"); } assert(closed, "Tagged sessions did not disappear")` — five seconds of polling after Scope.close(child) at :245. remote-inspector.ts:49-51 also records the connectionId before persist, so a failed registration leaves the id in the set permanently: `yield* Ref.update(registered, (ids) => new Set([...ids, session.connectionId])); yield* persist;`.

**Scenario.** Boot calls assertNoSessions(childExited) immediately after proving the child process is gone. The kernel has sent FIN for each pooled socket, but Postgres has not yet reaped every backend, so pg_stat_activity still lists pids in the registered set and the call returns remote_sessions_open. Boot reads that as "cannot prove closure" on a perfectly clean shutdown and takes the unproven-closure path — the same outcome as a genuinely stuck child, on every ordinary cutover. The fixture's five-second poll is the evidence that this is the common case, not the rare one.

**Suggestion.** Give assertNoSessions an explicit bounded retry with a named budget inside the service (the same shape as the 5-second timeouts already used at remote-driver.ts:51 and remote-client.ts:64), so the contract the caller gets is "proved, or not proved within N", and document the budget in packages/storage/docs/README.md:17 next to the closure-proof description.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed: remote-inspector.ts:56-81 observes sessions() and connectionIds() once with no retry budget, and the PR's own fixture needs a 100-iteration/50 ms loop after `Scope.close(child)` to get a success (test/fixtures/remote-sessions.ts:245-253), which is direct evidence that a just-closed pool is still visible on the server. remote-inspector.ts:49-51 also records the connectionId before persist, so a failed registration leaves that id in `registered` permanently, making the second check permanently unsatisfiable for that pid until it is reaped. A caller could wrap the retry itself, and there is no caller yet, so minor is right.


### A-3 [note] MySQL read isolation rests on an assertion the PR's own client startup does not make

`packages/storage/src/dialect.ts:112` (dialect-and-queries)

**Claim.** `readTransaction` emits no prelude on MySQL and justifies that with a precondition — that client startup asserts REPEATABLE-READ — which neither this PR nor anything else in the tree implements, and which the body does not list as deferred.

**Evidence.** dialect.ts:111-113 — `pg: () => sql`SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ`.pipe(Effect.asVoid),` / `// Client startup must assert MySQL REPEATABLE-READ; SET TRANSACTION inside BEGIN is invalid.` / `mysql: () => Effect.void,`. `rg transaction_isolation packages` returns nothing. remote-driver.ts:42-61 (`identify`), the startup probe this PR adds, asserts `pg_catalog.current_setting('max_prepared_transactions')='0'` for Postgres and checks only server uuid/database/user/tag for MySQL; `RemoteFailure` (remote-session.ts:34-41) has no isolation code. docs/database.md:852 requires "boot asserts `SELECT @@transaction_isolation` at startup and refuses `store_isolation_unsupported` when an operator has changed it".

**Scenario.** A MySQL server configured with `transaction_isolation=READ-COMMITTED` (a common DBA default for replication-lag reasons) accepts every connection silently. `readTransaction` adds nothing, so the multi-statement read paths behave exactly as in A-2's scenario — inconsistent rows across statements — on an engine the design says "needs nothing". The precondition is load-bearing and invisible.

**Suggestion.** Either add the one-row check to `identify` in remote-driver.ts (it already runs a bootstrap metadata query per lease; `@@transaction_isolation` costs nothing there) with a `remote_isolation_unsupported` failure, or change the dialect.ts:112 comment to point at the step-6 item that owns it and list it in the PR body's remaining-work paragraph alongside "distinct-role schema initialization".

**Skeptic.** Facts confirmed: dialect.ts:111-113 emits the pg prelude and nothing on MySQL with the comment "Client startup must assert MySQL REPEATABLE-READ"; grep for `transaction_isolation`/`REPEATABLE` across packages/ matches only those two dialect.ts lines. remote-driver.ts:47 probes `current_setting('max_prepared_transactions')='0'` for pg and remote-driver.ts:48 checks only server uuid/database/user/tag for MySQL, and remote-session.ts has no isolation failure code. But the obligation design §9.2 (database-design.md:852) assigns is to *boot startup*, and the PR body explicitly defers "Remote startup, ownership, schema initialization" to a later PR, so this is a recorded status gap rather than an omission inside this PR's scope; the only in-scope defect is a comment asserting a precondition with no pointer to who owns it. Downgrade to note.


### A-5 [note] Four dialect helpers ship with zero call sites, and a second SQLite-only `isDescendant` survives alongside the new one

`packages/storage/src/dialect.ts:95` (dialect-and-queries)

**Claim.** `lockRow`, `distinctFrom`, `globPrefix` and `plannerHint` are exported and unit-tested but used nowhere, and `packages/storage/src/descendant.ts` remains as a competing SQLite-only prefix helper still imported by boot — so step 3's boot half is untouched while the package now exports two answers to the same question.

**Evidence.** `rg '\b(lockRow|distinctFrom|globPrefix|plannerHint)\b' packages` matches only dialect.ts and dialect.test.ts. descendant.ts:5-6 is still `sql`substr(${child},1,length(${ancestor})+1)=${ancestor}||'/'`` with no dialect branch, still exported at packages/storage/package.json:9 (`"./descendant"`), and still imported at packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:1, while topics.ts:1 switched to `@comms/storage/dialect`. The sites those four helpers exist for are all intact: packages/boot/src/events.ts:280/336/348 (`GLOB`), :339 (`instance IS NOT ${...}`), the `NOT INDEXED`/`INDEXED BY` hints in the same file, and the unlocked singleton reads in events.ts's `reserve`/`append`/`abort`/`writeBoot`.

**Scenario.** Cost, not breakage: the next person porting boot must rediscover which of two `isDescendant` implementations is current, and `@comms/storage/descendant` is the one whose emitted SQL is silently SQLite-only — a boot store on MySQL would match `||` as logical OR and rewrite `public_paths.path` to 0/1 (the failure §6.2a calls out) with no error. Unused exports also give false assurance: a reader grepping for `lockRow` sees a helper and assumes §9.3's rule is enforced.

**Suggestion.** Either route the boot sites now (events.ts and public-paths.ts are the whole list) and delete descendant.ts plus its package export, or state in the PR body that step 3's boot half and the four unrouted helpers are deferred, so the gap is a recorded status rather than something a reader has to grep for.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. grep for lockRow/distinctFrom/globPrefix/plannerHint across packages/server/src, packages/boot/src, packages/storage/src and the test dir matches only dialect.ts and dialect.test.ts. descendant.ts:5-6 still holds the SQLite-only `substr(${child},1,length(${ancestor})+1)=${ancestor}||'/'`, is still exported at packages/storage/package.json:9, and is still the import at packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:1 (used at :52 and :58, where :58 also keeps the value-position `||`). The unrouted boot sites the four helpers exist for are intact: events.ts GLOB filters (~277-281, 336, 348) and `instance IS NOT ${...}` at :339. The finder's own framing — "cost, not breakage" — is accurate, the boot half of step 3 is plainly out of this PR's stated blast radius, and no current path executes any of it off SQLite, so note rather than minor.


### A-6 [note] The dialect test's compiler does not reproduce production identifier escaping, leaving the one genuinely new risk untested

`packages/storage/test/dialect.test.ts:12` (dialect-and-queries)

**Claim.** The test harness supplies its own `onIdentifier` that quotes the whole string, where production uses `Statement.defaultEscape`, which also escapes dots — so the tests never pin the behaviour of the dotted identifiers this PR introduced at a dozen sites.

**Evidence.** dialect.test.ts:12-13 — `onIdentifier: (value) => name === "mysql" ? `\`${value.replaceAll("`", "``")}\`` : `"${value.replaceAll('"', '""')}"`` — and every assertion uses a single-segment identifier (`sql("topic")`, `sql("previous")`, `sql("tags")`). Production goes through `makeCompilerSqlite`/`escapeSqlite = defaultEscape("\"")` (repos/effect/packages/effect/src/unstable/sql/Statement.ts:1075-1109), whose doc comment is "escapes dots between identifier parts". The PR's switch from PR #4's `sql`m.topic`` fragments to `sql("m.topic")` identifiers (topics.ts:25-31, published-messages.ts:16, pages.ts:101, topic-move.ts:123) depends entirely on that dot handling: I compiled it and got `substr("m"."topic",1,length("t"."path")+1)="t"."path"||'/'`, which is correct, but the harness in this file would have produced `"m.topic"` and still passed every assertion.

**Scenario.** No current defect — I verified the production emission. The cost is that the test suite cannot detect a regression in exactly the direction this PR moved: if the pg or mysql client's escaper ever differs (or a helper is reused with a dotted name under a custom compiler), `"m.topic"` resolves to a non-existent column and the failure appears as a runtime SQL error in the unread rollup, not in the dialect tests.

**Suggestion.** Use `Statement.defaultEscape('"')` and `Statement.defaultEscape('`')` in the test compilers, and add one assertion on a dotted identifier (e.g. `isDescendant(sql, sql("m.topic"), sql("t.path"))`) per dialect so the qualified form is pinned.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed. dialect.test.ts:10-13 supplies `onIdentifier: (value) => name === "mysql" ? backtick-quote : double-quote` with only delimiter doubling, while production compilers use Statement.defaultEscape, whose doc comment at repos/effect/packages/effect/src/unstable/sql/Statement.ts (defaultEscape) is "wraps names in the given delimiter, doubles delimiter characters, and escapes dots between identifier parts" and whose body does `.replace(/\./g, dot)`. Every assertion in the file uses single-segment identifiers (sql("topic"), sql("previous"), sql("tags"), sql("type"), sql("instance"), sql("seq")), so the dotted identifiers this PR introduces (topics.ts:25-31 sql("m.topic")/sql("t.path"), published-messages.ts:16, pages.ts:101, topic-move.ts:123-125) are unpinned. No live defect; note is correct.


### B-10 [note] attemptTag's base64url re-encoding makes the attempt id invisible to boot's existing redactHex

`packages/storage/src/remote-session.ts:88` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The 32-byte boot attempt is re-encoded from 64 hex characters to 43 base64url characters so it fits Postgres's application_name limit. A useful change, but it also means the same bytes stop matching the only redaction boot applies to child stderr and stored failure text.

**Evidence.** remote-session.ts:85-88: `const bytes = Encoding.decodeHex(attempt); ... return \`comms:${Encoding.encodeBase64Url(bytes.success)}\`` with the comment "49 printable characters, below PostgreSQL's 63-byte application_name limit." packages/boot/src/auth-primitives.ts:50 is `redactHex = (text: string) => text.replace(/[a-f0-9]{64}/g, "[redacted]")`, which matches the raw attempt and cannot match the base64url form. The tag is then written into the handshake at remote-driver.ts:22 (`applicationName: tag`) and :27 (`connectAttributes: { comms_attempt: tag }`), and compared against server-reported values at remote-driver.ts:58.

**Scenario.** Latent today, because the tag exists only inside packages/storage/src/remote-*.ts and nothing in boot or the server calls it. Once the remote client is wired, any diagnostic that prints the tag — a child's stderr tail, a Cause.pretty, a status body — carries the attempt identifier past supervisor.ts:112's redactHex unchanged, whereas the raw 64-hex form it is derived from would have been redacted. The exposure is narrow (Postgres restricts pg_stat_activity.application_name to the owning role and pg_read_all_stats; MySQL's session_account_connect_attrs to the same account without PROCESS), so this is a redaction-coverage note rather than a disclosure.

**Suggestion.** When redactUrls is added (B-1), extend the same redaction pass to the `comms:`/`inspect:` tag shape — a single `/(?:comms|inspect):[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}/g` clause — so the derived encoding is covered wherever the raw attempt already is.

**Skeptic.** Factually confirmed (remote-session.ts:85-88 re-encodes the 64-hex attempt to base64url; auth-primitives.ts:50 only matches `/[a-f0-9]{64}/g`), and the tag does travel into the handshake at remote-driver.ts:22 and :27. But the finder itself concedes it is latent with no caller, and the boot attempt id is an ownership token visible to the child anyway, so the disclosure value is close to zero. Keep as a note to fold into the redactUrls work.


### B-6 [note] attemptTag requires a non-empty password, making a credential mandatory and ruling out IAM, trust and peer authentication

`packages/storage/src/remote-session.ts:79` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The guard that validates the attempt and the connection also rejects an empty password, so a deployment cannot use any passwordless authentication method. Since attemptTag gates the whole guarded client (remote-client.ts:51) and the inspector (remote-inspector.ts:20), this is a hard constraint on the deployment shape, not a hint.

**Evidence.** remote-session.ts:74-84: `if (!/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/.test(attempt) || !connection.host || !connection.database || !connection.username || !Redacted.value(connection.password) || !Number.isSafeInteger(connection.port) || ...) return yield* failure("remote_configuration_invalid");`. The empty-string check on the unwrapped password is the fifth clause. Design §3.2 and §3.3 show password-based roles only and do not state that a password is required; nothing in the design forbids short-lived IAM tokens or a Unix-socket peer-authenticated boot role.

**Scenario.** A deployment on a managed Postgres that issues short-lived IAM auth tokens, or one where the boot role authenticates over a local Unix socket with peer/trust, cannot construct a RemoteConnection at all: attemptTag fails remote_configuration_invalid before any connection is attempted, with a code that does not say which field was wrong (by design). The operator sees a generic configuration failure and has no way to learn that a literal password string is mandatory.

**Suggestion.** Either drop the emptiness check and let the server reject a bad credential (the sanitized path already handles that), or keep it and document the requirement in packages/storage/docs/README.md and in design §3.2/§3.3 as an explicit deployment constraint: comms requires password authentication for both roles.

**Skeptic.** Code confirmed: remote-session.ts:74-84 includes `!Redacted.value(connection.password)` in the guard, and attemptTag gates both remote-client.ts:51 and remote-inspector.ts:20, so a passwordless role is unusable. Worth noting the finder missed the adjacent inconsistency: parseDescriptor (store.ts:76-87) happily accepts `postgres://app@host/db` with no password, so the descriptor layer and the session guard disagree. Severity lowered to note: the design shows password roles for both stores, no IAM/peer deployment is in scope for this track, and nothing is wired, so this is a documentation/consistency item.


### B-8 [note] replacePrefix is merged but the two boot-side sites it exists to protect are not routed

`packages/boot/src/events.ts:169` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The helper whose stated purpose is to stop MySQL's || from silently turning a moved path into 0 or 1 is merged, tested and routed at one of its four sites; the two that the design singles out as the silent-corruption cases remain raw, and this PR touches neither file.

**Evidence.** dialect.ts:29-34 implements replacePrefix with the mysql CONCAT form. The only call site is packages/server/src/ext/core/topic-move.ts:122. Still raw: packages/boot/src/events.ts:169 `sql\`UPDATE events SET topic=${move.to} || substr(topic,length(${move.from})+1)\`` and packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:58 `sql\`UPDATE public_paths SET path=${to}||substr(path,length(${from})+1)\``. Design §6.2a: "The value-position `||` is a logical OR on MySQL, so the rewrite would silently set every moved path to `0` or `1` rather than fail."

**Scenario.** On MySQL, a topic move would set every matching events.topic and public_paths.path to 0 or 1 with no error. The rows are boot-owned — events is the event log and public_paths is what boot projects for public page serving — so the corruption lands in the two tables the board's history and its public surface depend on, and it does so quietly. This cannot fire today (MySQL does not run), but the helper that prevents it shipped without its two highest-consequence consumers, so the protection is not where the risk is.

**Suggestion.** Route events.ts:169-170 and public-paths.ts:58 through replacePrefix and isDescendant in this PR, or state in the body that boot/src is deliberately out of scope for step 3 so the next PR inherits a named list. The same applies to the five GLOB sites at events.ts:280, :281, :336, :339, :348, for which globPrefix and plannerHint are merged with zero production callers.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed: dialect.ts:29-34 has replacePrefix; the only call sites are topic-move.ts:121 and :125; boot/src/events.ts:169 and boot/src/public-paths.ts:58 still use value-position `||`, and the design (§6.2a) says that silently becomes a logical OR on MySQL. Two mitigations lower the severity: this PR touches no file under packages/boot/src at all (git diff aaf6396..HEAD -- packages/boot shows only boot/test/request-events.test.ts), and boot's SQL portability is step 3 work the body defers ('Remote startup, ownership, schema initialization ... being integrated separately'). It cannot fire today since MySQL never runs. Note rather than minor.


### B-9 [note] The mysql2 patch path is version-pinned in five places and the editable generation now installs a driver nothing imports

`packages/server/stage-runtime.ts:24` (remote-safety)

**Claim.** The exact patch filename, including the dependency version, is hardcoded in five files that must change together on every @effect/sql-mysql2 bump; and every editable generation's frozen install now pulls mysql2 and seven transitive packages that no code in the tree imports.

**Evidence.** The literal `@effect%2Fsql-mysql2@4.0.0-rc.113.patch` appears at stage-runtime.ts:24 and :25, package.json:39, packages/server/runtime/package.json:39, and twice in packages/server/test/generation-revert.test.ts (:20-22 and :301-303). packages/server/runtime/bun.lock gains @effect/sql-pg, @effect/sql-mysql2, mysql2 and aws-ssl-profiles, generate-function, is-property, long, lru.min, named-placeholders, sql-escaper, and bumps iconv-lite from 0.6.3 to 0.7.3 for the whole runtime seed. Nothing under packages/server/src or packages/ui/src imports @effect/sql-pg or @effect/sql-mysql2 (grep returns only packages/storage/src/remote-driver.ts and remote-values.ts, which have zero production callers).

**Scenario.** A routine dependency bump changes the patch filename; stage-runtime.ts:23's fs.copyFile then fails and the build stops, which is loud and fine, but packages/server/runtime/package.json and the two generation-revert fixtures have to be edited in lockstep or the frozen install inside generation preparation fails at packages/boot/src/generation-preparation.ts:102 and every candidate is rejected until someone notices. Separately, the iconv-lite minor bump in the runtime seed is a transitive consequence of adding mysql2 for a code path the seed cannot reach, so an editable generation's dependency surface moved for no benefit it can use yet.

**Suggestion.** Derive the patch filename once — read it from the root package.json's patchedDependencies key in stage-runtime.ts rather than repeating the literal, and have the generation-revert fixtures copy whatever patches/ contains instead of naming a version. Consider deferring the @effect/sql-pg and @effect/sql-mysql2 dependency edges in packages/storage/package.json to the PR that first imports them from a reachable path, keeping the drivers and the patch out of the editable seed until something in it can use them.

**Skeptic.** Confirmed literally: `@effect%2Fsql-mysql2@4.0.0-rc.113.patch` appears at stage-runtime.ts:24 and :25, package.json:39, packages/server/runtime/package.json:39 and generation-revert.test.ts:21-22 and :302-303, and runtime/bun.lock gains the pg/mysql2 driver set for a seed that imports neither. A failing copyFile is loud, so the cost is maintenance plus a widened frozen-install surface. Note is correct.


### Item status


#### dialect-and-queries

Dimension: the dialect layer and the core query rewrite (PR #8, 686e07c, base aaf6396).

**SQLite is safe.** I compiled the real helpers against the vendored Effect compiler (`repos/effect/.../Statement.ts`) and differentially executed old vs. new SQL on bun:sqlite 3.51 (the production driver, `packages/storage/src/client.ts:11`). Every routed site is behaviour-equivalent, and the two silent-risk changes are genuinely safe: `sql("m.topic")` compiles to `"m"."topic"` (not `"m.topic"`) because `Statement.defaultEscape` escapes dots (`Statement.ts:1102-1109`), and `jsonText`'s bound path `$."tags"` behaves identically to the old inline `$.tags`. I verified row-for-row identity for `publishedMessages`/`publishedTopics`, the mention filter (old `json_each(...) mention … IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(targets))` vs. new `OR`-of-`jsonArrayHas`, including empty, duplicate and multi-target cases), the tag filter, the recursive topic filter (incl. the empty-topic case), the `reads` move upsert with its new column aliases, the `reads` delete, and the 200-char move guard (safe because `validTopic` is ASCII-only, `messages.ts:25`, so JS `.length` == SQLite `length()`). `bodyMatch` `1` → `1=1` and `mentionMatch` → `1=0` are truth-identical on SQLite and fix real pg parse errors. The mention grammar (`mentionsIn`) and the read-mark/ancestor-rollup predicates are untouched apart from identifier quoting. No SQLite regression found.

**Portability is compile-only, and two things are provably wrong.** No core query executes against Postgres or MySQL anywhere: `dialect.test.ts` asserts generated text, and the container suite (`packages/storage/test/remote-sessions.test.ts`, `scripts/remote-session-acceptance.sh`) exercises leases, session inspection and the integer codec with `sql.unsafe("SELECT 1")`-class statements only. Against that gap, (1) the published-image CTE omits the `ELSE tags::text` / `ELSE meta::text` casts that design §6.10 prescribes, so with the `jsonb` columns design §7.2 mandates Postgres raises 42804 on the query every read composes; and (2) `readTransaction` landed at the two pages.ts sites but not at `read-snapshot.ts:30`, the transaction every message/topic/extension read actually runs inside, so on Postgres the whole read path stays at READ COMMITTED — exactly the silent failure design §9.2 calls "the subtlest thing here". Four helpers (`lockRow`, `distinctFrom`, `globPrefix`, `plannerHint`) ship with zero call sites, `upsert` was not built (two core sites hand-roll it, and `kernel/extension-data.ts:70` plus `ext/system.ts:50` still emit MySQL-invalid `ON CONFLICT`), and `returning` plus per-engine search are step 5/6 work the body defers honestly.

Would a board moved to Postgres return the same rows as SQLite today? No — it cannot be moved at all (`store.ts:38` still refuses `postgres:`/`mysql:`), and if it were, reads would fail to parse before returning anything. Three deviations from the design are improvements and the doc should be corrected: MySQL `BINARY` in `isDescendant`/`globPrefix` (design §6.2/§6.7b omit it; without it MySQL's default `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` would make every subtree match case- and accent-insensitive — worth checking the `BINARY` operator form against MySQL's 8.0.27 deprecation note before shipping), `jsonText`/`jsonInt`'s `JSON_TYPE(...)='NULL'` CASE instead of design §6.3's `NULLIF`, which correctly distinguishes JSON `null` from the JSON string `"null"`, and binding the JSON path as a parameter rather than interpolating it. The module also lives at `packages/storage/src/dialect.ts`, not §6's `packages/boot/src/dialect.ts`, and `on` is typed on `Constructor` so fragments compose.

| status | item | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| fixed | §6.1 `on` three-dialect wrapper that fails loudly on other dialects | packages/storage/src/dialect.ts:8-17 wraps `sql.onDialectOrElse` with a throwing `orElse`. Typed on `Constructor` rather than §6.1's `SqlClient.SqlClient`, which is an improvement: fragments and the client both satisfy it, which is what lets `on` nest inside `sql` templates (read-marks.ts:14, topic-move.ts:119). |
| partly | §6.2 `isDescendant` routed at 41 sites | Routed at every app core site (messages.ts:166, message-operations.ts:65/73, published-messages.ts:16, topics.ts:25-31, topic-operations.ts:51/60, topic-move.ts:71/84/87/100/121/123/125/134, topic-page-continuation.ts:17-19, pages.ts:48/101, public-page-policy.ts:49). Boot is untouched: `packages/boot/src/events.ts:170` still has the raw `substr(topic,1,length(?)+1)=?` form and `packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:1` still imports the older SQLite-only `@comms/storage/descendant`. |
| partly | §6.2a `replacePrefix` at 4 sites | topic-move.ts:121 and :125 routed. `packages/boot/src/events.ts:169` (`SET topic=${move.to} \|\| substr(...)`) and `packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:58` still use the value-position `\|\|` that §6.2a says silently yields 0/1 on MySQL. |
| improvement | §6.3 `jsonText` / `jsonInt` (19 occurrences) | dialect.ts:37-53. MySQL emits `CASE WHEN JSON_TYPE(JSON_EXTRACT(col,?))='NULL' THEN NULL ELSE JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(col,?)) END` instead of §6.3's `NULLIF(col ->> '$.k','null')`, so a stored JSON string "null" stays a string where the design's form would have turned it into SQL NULL; dialect.test.ts:63-65 asserts `not.toContain("NULLIF")`. The key is bound, not interpolated. Design §6.3's table should be corrected. |
| improvement | §6.4 `jsonArrayHas` plus the separate mention fragment | dialect.ts:56-62 routed at messages.ts:171 (mentions) and :200 (tags). §6.4 said the mention filter must be a bespoke three-dialect fragment with `JSON_TABLE` on MySQL; the PR instead rewrites it as an OR of `jsonArrayHas` per target, which I verified row-identical on SQLite and which needs no `JSON_TABLE`. Design §6.4's second paragraph should be corrected. |
| unchanged | §6.5 `upsert(sql, table, row, conflict, set)` — 8 sites | No `upsert` export in dialect.ts. Two core sites hand-roll `on(...)` (read-marks.ts:14, topic-move.ts:119 and :126-133) and the rest are SQLite-only: `INSERT OR IGNORE` at protected-sql-tables.ts:24, generations.ts:75, app-recovery.ts:39, application.ts:108/117, app-store-identity.ts:131; `ON CONFLICT` with no MySQL branch at extension-data.ts:70, ext/system.ts:50, settings.ts:77/104, app-store-layout.ts:67/72/102, tokens.ts:124, public-paths.ts:46, edit-lock.ts:255/298. Not listed as deferred in the PR body. |
| fixed | §6.6 `greatest` — 1 site | dialect.ts:65-70, routed at topic-move.ts:128/130; compiles to `MAX("reads"."seq","excluded"."seq")` on SQLite, which I executed against the base form and got identical rows. |

#### remote-safety

Reviewed PR #8 (686e07c) against base aaf6396 in the wt-pr8 worktree, from the credentials / network-guarantees / packaging dimension. Read packages/storage/src/{store,dialect,remote-client,remote-driver,remote-session,remote-inspector,remote-values}.ts in full, scripts/remote-session-acceptance.sh, packages/server/stage-runtime.ts, the runtime manifest and lock diff, the @effect/sql-mysql2 patch, all six new/changed storage tests, the ext/core rewrites, and the vendored Effect sources under repos/effect that the new code depends on (SqlClient.ts, SqlConnection.ts, Statement.ts, PgClient.ts, PgTypes.ts, PgConnection.ts).

Credentials: the redaction discipline inside packages/storage is genuinely good and I could not break it. RemoteStore.url stays Redacted end to end (store.ts:11, :30-33); the only Redacted.value sites outside storage are boot-channel.ts:35 and supervisor.ts:149, and supervisor.ts:116 still types its store as FileStore, so no credential can reach a child today. Every driver entry point is behind sanitized() — all six Connection members via safeConnection (remote-client.ts:31-46), open, identify, sessions, connectionIds, register, the caller's persist callback, and — because safeConnection wraps executeUnprepared — even the orDie'd BEGIN/COMMIT/ROLLBACK. There is no logging, no retry, and no stringification of a credential anywhere in packages/storage/src. The fixture's deliberate Effect.fail(new Error(password)) at test/fixtures/remote-sessions.ts:89 with the assertion at :133 is the right adversarial test and it holds. Both TLS paths verify certificates (remote-driver.ts:22, :26), the pg int8 registry is per-client with no global mutation (remote-values.ts:5-18, asserted at test/remote-values.test.ts:11), and the MySQL pool-ownership patch is verified against the real installed artifact by a hermetic test (test/mysql-pool-cleanup.test.ts).

What is wrong is not inside storage — it is what the PR left outside it. redactUrls, which the design names as the mechanism that keeps a connection URL out of child stderr and out of generations.stderr, does not exist anywhere in the tree, and is not in the PR body's deferral list even though this is the PR that creates the credential-bearing descriptor and widens render() to carry it. RemoteConnection.tls is a bare boolean with no producer, and the descriptor grammar rejects query parameters, so there is no expressible way to require verified TLS with a private CA — the design never mentions TLS at all. readTransaction is merged and unit-tested but routed at only 2 of the design's 7 sites, and the one site that sets the isolation for every read, kernel/read-snapshot.ts:30, is not among them, so on Postgres no read path would get REPEATABLE READ; topics.ts:20, a file this PR rewrote, is also unrouted. §6.10's per-column cast discipline is missing from published-messages.ts:10-11 and published-topics.ts:7, and no test renders any rewritten core query under a pg or mysql compiler. Finally, the guarded-lease design itself — one fresh lease, two metadata round trips and one globally serialized durable registration per statement, through a single pinned inspector connection behind a semaphore of 1 and a hardcoded pool of 4 — appears nowhere in the design document and its cost is not in the §9.4 latency budget.

None of this breaks a core guarantee today: the whole remote surface has zero production callers (verified by grep across boot/src and server/src), and the SQLite behaviour is preserved. The defects are unadmitted deferrals of named design requirements plus one verified portability defect that bites at step 4.

Three riskiest changes I could not break. (1) remote-client.ts:53-74, the uninterruptible lease ownership around the registration handshake: I traced Effect's makeWithTransaction acquireConnection (SqlClient.ts:338-341) and confirmed the upstream leak it compensates for is real, and could not find a path where the physical connection escapes, is double-closed, or survives a failed registration; the fixture's 6-iteration reject loop against a pool of 4 is the correct test. (2) remote-session.ts:47-69, sanitized/sanitizedCause: I enumerated every call site that can see a driver cause and found no unsanitized path from a credential to an error, an event payload, or a schema decode issue. (3) the MySQL patch plus test/mysql-pool-cleanup.test.ts: the test substitutes a fake mysql2 into a temp node_modules and runs the installed, patched adapter, so it verifies the shipped artifact; I could not construct a case where the pool survives a failed or timed-out initial query.

### Skeptic corrections


**dialect-and-queries.** I agree with all 21 rows as written; corrections and additions, all re-verified in the worktree:

1. "§6.9 readTransaction — partly" understates the unrouted set. Besides kernel/read-snapshot.ts:30 and kernel/extension-data.ts:77, two more top-level transactions that will run read-only statements are unrouted: kernel/sql-read-worker.ts:29 and kernel/health.ts:21 (`sql.withTransaction(...)`). It also understates the other direction in the PR's favour: because publication.ts:34 wires `read = makeReadSnapshot(sql, ...)` and topics.ts:20 nests inside it, routing the single line read-snapshot.ts:30 would cover four or five of design §9.2's seven named sites at once (messages.ts:179/:243, topics.ts:40, extension-capabilities.ts:74). That makes A-2 a cheaper fix and a larger gap than the row implies.

2. "§6.2 isDescendant — partly" is right but should name the second form explicitly: boot keeps a *different implementation*, not just unrouted SQL — packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:1 imports `isDescendant` from `@comms/storage/descendant` and calls it at :52 and :58, while the app's 12 core modules import `@comms/storage/dialect`. Same for "§6.2a replacePrefix": packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:58 (`SET path=${to}||substr(path,length(${from})+1)`) and packages/boot/src/events.ts:169.

3. "§13 step 3 acceptance — fixed" is correct as scoped to SQLite byte-equivalence, but the row reads as if step 3 passed. Step 3's "Changes" paragraph (database-design.md:1091) also requires `upsert` (8 sites), `globPrefix` plus the hint helper (5), `distinctFrom`, `lockRow`, and `readTransaction` at the seven read sites — by the rows' own evidence four of those are unrouted or unbuilt. Worth splitting into "SQLite equivalence: fixed" and "step-3 site coverage: partly".

4. "§6.10 — claimed-but-not" is confirmed and deserves one more piece of corroboration the row does not cite: public-page-policy.ts:46 already writes `meta::jsonb -> 'public' = 'true'::jsonb` against the same CTE column, i.e. the code elsewhere assumes the CTE's `meta` may arrive as text — yet the CASE that produces it cannot parse at all under the §7.2 jsonb schema.

5. "§6.3 jsonText/jsonInt — improvement" is right about the `JSON_TYPE(...)='NULL'` CASE and the bound path; add that the pg branch binds the *key* as a parameter to `->>` (`previous::jsonb ->> $n`) rather than using a literal as §6.3 does. That is the one remaining untested unknown in the helper: Postgres must resolve `jsonb ->> unknown` among the (jsonb,text) and (jsonb,integer) overloads, and nothing in the tree executes it. Worth a single live pg statement before step 5 rather than a finding.


**remote-safety.** Two enumeration errors in the reviewer's own evidence, both in the credential-flow claim:

1. Row "§4.2 render returns Redacted and the credential stays in Redacted end to end" (and the summary's "the only Redacted.value sites outside storage are boot-channel.ts:35 and supervisor.ts:149") is incomplete. `grep -rn "render("` over boot/src and server/src returns a third unwrap of a store descriptor: packages/boot/src/linux-ownership.ts:136 `APP_STORE: Redacted.value(render({ _tag: "file", filename: \`${directory}/comms.db\` }))`. It is harmless today (a FileStore literal), but it is a third child-environment site that the remote-startup PR must handle, and the row's "the one site the design permits" framing hides it. The conclusion (no credential can reach a child today) still holds, for the reason given: supervisor.ts:116 types `store: FileStore` and app-recovery.ts:105 builds `const store: FileStore`.

2. Row "§4.2 / design:310 redactUrls ..." lists only supervisor.ts:111-112, :324, :370, :382 and cutover.ts:356, :360. The same redactHex-only treatment also guards packages/boot/src/proxy.ts:240 (`const safeState = { ...state, stderr: redactHex(state.stderr) }` — the state served over HTTP), packages/boot/src/events.ts:301-302 (stored error/stderr returned from the event log), packages/boot/src/preparation-process.ts:84 and packages/boot/src/supervisor.ts:257. If redactUrls is added, it is nine or ten sites, not six.

Everything else I spot-checked matched: `dialectOf` and `redactUrls` are absent (0 hits over packages and scripts); client.ts:6-12 still takes `FileStore` only; readTransaction's only production callers are pages.ts:39 and :91; replacePrefix's only callers are topic-move.ts:121 and :125; globPrefix/plannerHint have zero production callers; dialect.ts:112-113 leaves the MySQL isolation assertion as a comment and remote-driver.ts:11-33 issues none; store.ts:106-125 does add the `app.database !== boot.database` predicate the design does not ask for (a genuine improvement, and §4.2 should record it); dialect.ts:37-45's JSON_TYPE form is an improvement over the design's NULLIF; scripts/check-invariants.ts is untouched by this PR (git diff aaf6396..HEAD -- scripts shows only the new remote-session-acceptance.sh) and no file under packages/ui/src imports @comms/storage.


### What the skeptics found that the finders missed


**dialect-and-queries.** The MySQL branch of the read-mark move upsert is invalid SQL: packages/server/src/ext/core/topic-move.ts:132 emits `SELECT incoming_instance,incoming_topic,incoming_seq FROM (${sourceMarks}) AS moved ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE seq=GREATEST(reads.seq,incoming_seq)`. In an `INSERT ... SELECT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE`, MySQL resolves unqualified names in the update assignment against the *insert target* (`reads`), which has columns (instance, topic, seq) only — the source columns are reachable only through the derived-table/row alias (`moved.incoming_seq`), which is exactly the `AS new` row-alias discipline design §6.5 (database-design.md:545-557) prescribes and which the sibling site at topic-move.ts:119 does follow (`ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE path=path`). The aliases exist on the inner select (topic-move.ts:128: `SELECT instance AS incoming_instance,${replacePrefix(...)} AS incoming_topic,seq AS incoming_seq FROM reads ...`) precisely so the outer clause can reference them, and then the reference is left unqualified. Failure scenario: on MySQL, `POST /api/topics/<path>/move` reaches its body, reserves a sequence range, rewrites `topics` and `messages`, and then fails at this statement with ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR (1054, "Unknown column 'incoming_seq' in 'field list'") — mid-mutation, after the reservation, so the failure lands in the abort/poison path rather than at startup, and every topic move is permanently broken on that engine. It is invisible today for the same reason as A-1 and A-2: dialect.test.ts never compiles topic-move's upsert (grep shows no `incoming_seq` assertion) and no test executes a core query against MySQL. This is the same class as A-4 but a concrete wrong statement rather than a missing helper, and the finder's A-4 evidence actually praised the neighbouring site for §6.5 compliance without checking this one.


**remote-safety.** Two same-named `isDescendant` helpers now coexist in packages/storage, one portable and one SQLite-only, and the SQLite-only one is the one with a live production caller on the boot side.

packages/storage/src/descendant.ts:5-6 is still exported (packages/storage/package.json:9 `"./descendant": "./src/descendant.ts"`) and still contains the unportable body:

    export const isDescendant = (sql: SqlClient, child: Fragment, ancestor: Fragment) =>
        sql`substr(${child},1,length(${ancestor})+1)=${ancestor}||'/'`;

This PR added the portable version at packages/storage/src/dialect.ts:22-27 (`starts_with(...)` on pg, `BINARY substr(...)=BINARY CONCAT(...)` on mysql) and routed the server's ext/core files to it, but left descendant.ts in place, exported, and imported by the only boot-side consumer: packages/boot/src/public-paths.ts:1 uses it at :52 (`DELETE FROM public_paths WHERE path=${value.path} OR ${isDescendant(...)}`) and :59 (`movePublicPaths`). On MySQL, `${ancestor}||'/'` is a logical OR, so that predicate becomes `substr(path,1,n) = 0|1`, MySQL coerces the string side to a number, and the comparison is false for every ordinary path: the cascade delete silently removes nothing and the move matches nothing, so a deleted or moved topic's public paths stay published in the table boot projects for public page serving. On Postgres it happens to work (text `||`), which is worse — the bug appears only on one of the two new engines.

The finder reported the value-position `||` inside public-paths.ts:58 (B-8) but not that the helper immediately above it, which *looks* dialect-aware because it lives in @comms/storage and takes `sql`, is a second, unportable implementation of the exact function this PR was porting. It is the trap most likely to survive step 3: a porter grepping for `isDescendant(` sees boot already "using the helper" and moves on. scripts/check-invariants.ts has no rule that would catch it. The fix is to delete src/descendant.ts and its package.json export in this PR and point public-paths.ts at dialect.ts (its two call sites already pass `sql` fragments, so it is a one-line import change plus the `||` fix in movePublicPaths); if that must wait for the boot-side PR, the design doc's §6.2 site list should name descendant.ts explicitly as a module to remove rather than a helper already in use.


### Claims check

All commands run in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-cryogenicplanet-general-comms/f370fb0c-0b48-4731-a18c-45a3703a3b43/scratchpad/wt-pr8` (note: the worktree is at `scratchpad/wt-pr8`, **not** `scratchpad/stack/wt-pr8` as the task stated), detached at `686e07c`. `git status --short` was empty before and after every command — **no tracked file was modified, no formatting changes were written.**
